- v1.5: the README shows an `evaluate` transcript on the demo's `outcomes.jsonl` (11). `tests/test_readme.py` runs every fenced `text` block in the README whose first line starts with `$ model-routing`, in README order in one empty folder (so later blocks read what earlier ones wrote), and requires exit code 0 and stdout equal to the rest of the block line for line, so the demo, report and evaluate transcripts cannot drift

### 12.6 Public evidence: the RouterBench converter (`evidence/routerbench/convert.py`, v1.6)
Purpose: turn a public dataset that already holds every model's output, correctness and cost per prompt into the 12.1 matrix, so the evaluator runs on third-party data with no API key. It lives outside the package: `model_routing` never imports it, it is standard library except that reading the frame needs pandas (the `evidence` extra), and the tests that need pandas use `pytest.importorskip`, so CI passes without it
- Source, pinned: dataset `withmartian/routerbench` on Hugging Face at revision `784021482c3f320c6619ed4b3bb3b41a21424fcb`, file `routerbench_0shot.pkl`, `SIZE = 99567659` bytes, `SHA256 = "ba4f77f19517610a707c374e99322d7750c30fc4ae7ff5527888595a1e65d36d"` (its LFS object ID). The RouterBench code is MIT licensed (https://github.com/withmartian/routerbench); the dataset card states no data licence, and the evidence README says so
- `default_cache_dir()` is `~/.cache/ai-model-routing-evidence/routerbench/` (from `Path.home()`), never inside the repo. `fetch(cache_dir=None) -> Path`: the file is `cache_dir / "routerbench_0shot.pkl"`. A present file is verified and used, and one that fails `verify` is a `ValueError` naming it and is left in place (delete it to download again); a missing file is downloaded with `urllib.request.urlopen` from `https://huggingface.co/datasets/withmartian/routerbench/resolve/<revision>/routerbench_0shot.pkl` into a `.part` file next to it, verified, then renamed, and the `.part` file is removed when the check fails
- `verify(path, size=SIZE, sha256=SHA256)`: `ValueError` naming the expected and the actual value when the byte size or the sha256 differs. Nothing is unpickled before it passes
- `RestrictedUnpickler(pickle.Unpickler)`, `restricted_load(fileobj)` and `load_frame(path)`; `load_frame` opens the file, runs `restricted_load` and turns any other exception raised while unpickling (an empty or truncated file, an allowed callable given bad arguments) into a `ValueError` naming the file, so only `pickle.UnpicklingError` and `ValueError` leave it. In `restricted_load`, `find_class` resolves only the (module, name) pairs in `ALLOWED_GLOBALS`, exactly the 11 globals the pinned file references (found with a static `pickletools` opcode scan, which runs no code): `builtins.slice`, `numpy.dtype`, `numpy.ndarray`, `numpy.core.multiarray._reconstruct`, `numpy.core.numeric._frombuffer`, `pandas._libs.internals._unpickle_block`, `pandas.core.frame.DataFrame`, `pandas.core.indexes.base.Index`, `pandas.core.indexes.base._new_Index`, `pandas.core.indexes.range.RangeIndex`, `pandas.core.internals.managers.BlockManager`. Any other global raises `pickle.UnpicklingError` naming it, with no module-prefix wildcard; names are checked as written, with no Python 2 name mapping (`fix_imports=False`), and persistent IDs are refused. Because the file is hash-pinned, the list only has to cover that one file; a pickle written by another pandas or numpy version is refused, not trusted
- `model_id(column) -> str`: a column name of the form `vendor/model` (one `/`, both sides non-empty) is kept as is, and any other name containing `/` is a `ValueError`; a name starting `claude-` becomes `anthropic/<name>` and one starting `gpt-` becomes `openai/<name>`; anything else is a `ValueError` rather than a guess
- `frame_columns(frame) -> dict[str, list]`: `ValueError` naming the type when `frame` has no `columns` attribute (a pickle that loads to something other than a frame); otherwise plain Python lists, from `.tolist()`, for `sample_id`, `eval_name` and, for every model column `<m>`, both `<m>` (the score) and `<m>|total_cost`. A model column is any column without `|` other than `sample_id`, `prompt`, `eval_name` and `oracle_model_to_route_to`. Prompts and responses are never read. `ValueError` when `sample_id` or `eval_name` is missing, there is no model column, or a model has no `|total_cost` column
- `convert(columns, pass_score=None, min_items=100) -> tuple[list[dict], dict]`, where `columns` is what `frame_columns` returns (any `dict[str, list]` of that shape):
  - `ValueError`, checked before any output: columns of different lengths; a `sample_id` that is not a non-empty string or is repeated; an `eval_name` that is not a non-empty string; a score that is a bool, not an int or float, NaN or outside [0, 1]; a cost that is a bool, not an int or float, not finite or below 0 (score and cost messages name the `sample_id` and the column); `pass_score` that is not `None` and is a bool, not an int or float, or outside (0, 1] (so NaN is rejected); `min_items` that is a bool, not an int, or below 1; a model column that `model_id` rejects, or two columns mapping to one ID
  - each source row is one item: task = `eval_name`, item = `sample_id`, and one output row per model with model = `model_id(column)`, `cost_usd` = the recorded `total_cost` as a float, with a negative zero written as `0.0`
  - scores and costs must be Python `int` or `float` values (a `float` subclass such as numpy's float64 counts); other numeric types such as numpy's int64 or float32 are rejected, which `.tolist()` never produces but a hand-built `columns` dict could
  - graded tasks: a task is graded when any of its scores, for any model, is not exactly 0 or 1. With `pass_score=None` a graded task is dropped; otherwise it is kept. In a kept task `correct` is `score >= pass_score` when `pass_score` is set and `score == 1` when it is not, which agree on 0 or 1 scores for every allowed `pass_score`. The pinned file's graded tasks (`grade-school-math` with scores in {0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1}, `consensus_summary`, the `mtbench` tasks, `chinese_idioms`) do not document what a partial score means, so excluding them is the default and including them is the user's stated choice
  - small tasks: a task left with fewer than `min_items` items is dropped. The default 100 gives calibration and held-out halves of about 50 items, so in practice 12.3's empty-split rule does not trip and `min_samples = 30` can be met
  - output rows have the keys `task`, `item`, `model`, `correct`, `cost_usd` in that order, sorted by (task, item, model) as strings; they form the complete matrix 12.3 requires, and the result does not depend on the source row order
  - the report has keys `models` (IDs ascending), `kept`, `dropped_graded` and `dropped_small` (each `{task: number of items}` with tasks ascending) and `rows` (the number of output rows)
- `write_jsonl(rows, path)`: one `json.dumps(row)` per line, newline-terminated, in the given order
- CLI: `python evidence/routerbench/convert.py --out PATH [--cache DIR] [--pass-score T] [--min-items N]`, entry point `main(argv=None) -> int`. Options are checked first, so a bad one downloads nothing (`--out` must pass the 12.14 `--out` check, v1.174, row H4b; to v1.173 `--out` was not checked before the fetch, so a bad one failed only at the write or the rename, after the download and the unpickling); then it fetches, verifies, loads with `load_frame`, converts, writes `<out>.part`, prints the report as `json.dumps(report, indent=2)`, flushing stdout, and only then renames `<out>.part` onto `--out` (v1.94, as 13.11), so a write to stdout that fails (a closed pipe) is an I/O error that leaves `--out` as it was. After a `BrokenPipeError` from the print, stdout is pointed at `os.devnull` as in 13.11 (v1.150), so the process exits 2 with that one `error:` line, not 120 with two more lines from the interpreter's flush at exit. It returns 0 on success and 2 with exactly one `error: ...` line on stderr and nothing on stdout for a missing or invalid option, a size or hash mismatch, a refused global, a file that does not unpickle to a frame, invalid data or an I/O error, but for a rename that fails after the print, the one failure with the report already on stdout. The matrix is written to `<out>.part` and renamed onto `--out`; on any failure `--out` is not written and `<out>.part` is removed
- Reading it: the 11 models date from 2023 and the costs are the ones the dataset recorded at that time's prices; the evaluator routes per task (an `eval_name`), not per prompt

### 12.7 RouterBench results (`evidence/routerbench/results.py`, v1.7)
Purpose: commit the evaluator's numbers on RouterBench in a file small enough to review, regenerated byte for byte from stated commands, with the tables in `RESULTS.md` checked against it by the test suite. Standard library only: it reads JSON and never imports pandas
- `reduce(result: dict) -> dict`: from an `evaluate` result (12.3, with or without `cells`, so the `evaluate --json` output too), a new dict with the keys `config`, `tasks`, `summary` and `per_task` in that order, where `per_task` keeps only the rows whose `strategy` is `routed`, in their order; `cells` and the other per-task strategies are left out so the file stays under about 300 KB. Values are copied unchanged and the input is not modified, so `reduce(reduce(r)) == reduce(r)`. `ValueError` naming the key when one of the four is missing
- `table(results: dict) -> str`: two tables in the layout of 12.4 (header, a dash row, one row per line, trailing spaces stripped), separated by one blank line, with no newline after the last:
  - routed: one row per (bar, flavor) in config order, from that block's `routed` summary row, with columns `bar` (as Python `str` prints it), `flavor`, `success`, `cost_usd`, `vs_best_model`, `cleared_rate` and `bar_violation_rate`; `vs_best_model` is the routed `cost_usd` divided by the best model's `cost_usd`
  - baselines: from the summary rows of the first (bar, flavor) in config order (held-out success and cost do not depend on the bar or the flavor), one row per strategy other than `routed`, in summary order, with columns `strategy`, `success` and `cost_usd`
  - the best model is the `model:<id>` row of that first block with the highest `success`, ties by lower `cost_usd`, then by strategy name
  - success, rates and `vs_best_model` to 3 decimals, `cost_usd` to 4; `-` for `None`, and for `vs_best_model` when the best model's cost is 0
  - `ValueError` when `config` or `summary` is missing, a (bar, flavor) in the config has no `routed` summary row, or the first block has no `model:` row
- CLI: `python evidence/routerbench/results.py --evaluate PATH --out PATH` writes `json.dumps(reduce(result), indent=2)` and a newline to `--out`, where `result` is the JSON object read from `--evaluate`; `python evidence/routerbench/results.py --table PATH` prints `table(results)` for the JSON object read from `PATH`. Exactly one of `--evaluate` and `--table` is given, and `--out` comes with `--evaluate` and never with `--table`. The file is written to `<out>.part` and renamed onto `--out` only after the write completes; on any failure an existing `--out` is left as it was, a new one is not created, and `<out>.part` is removed. Entry point `main(argv=None) -> int`: 0 on success, 2 with exactly one `error: ...` line on stderr and nothing on stdout for a usage error, a missing or unreadable file, invalid JSON, a JSON value that is not an object, a write or rename error, or a `ValueError` above; a carriage return or line feed inside the message (a path can contain one) is written as `\r` or `\n`, so the message stays on one line
- Committed: `evidence/routerbench/results.json`, from the default converter matrix (12.6) and `model-routing evaluate --bar 0.6 0.7 0.8 0.9 --seeds 50 --json` reduced as above, and `evidence/routerbench/RESULTS.md` with the regenerate commands, the caveats of 12.6 "Reading it", and the `table` output in a fenced `text` block whose first line is `$ python evidence/routerbench/results.py --table evidence/routerbench/results.json`. `tests/test_evidence_results.py` checks that the committed file is under 300,000 bytes, is exactly `json.dumps(reduce(itself), indent=2)` plus a newline, has that config, the converter's 11 model IDs on every task and its 65 tasks with their item counts (pinned by the sha256 of `json.dumps({task: items}, sort_keys=True)`, which matched the converter's `kept` report when the file was made), 28,281 items in all, unique summary and per-task keys and 50 seeds behind every `routed_models`, and that `RESULTS.md` contains that block with the current `table` output, so the published tables cannot drift from the published file
- CI: one Python version also installs the `evidence` extra and runs the converter tests, so the tests that need pandas run in CI and not only locally
- RESULTS.md prose (v1.18): `tests/test_evidence_results.py` also builds from the committed file, and requires verbatim in `RESULTS.md`, the intro counts (models, evals, prompts, seeds) and every number of the "Reading it" bullets: for `cost_effective` the first bar's cleared rate as a percentage to 1 decimal, its cost ratio to the best single model, its success and that model's, the last bar's cleared rate as a percentage and its cost ratio (the ratio must rise with the bar), and the lowest and highest bar-violation rate over the bars next to the nominal one-sided `(1 - confidence) / 2` and the confidence; for `cost_blind` its success and cost ratio, which must be the same at every bar, next to those of `best_on_calibration` and how much more it costs as a percentage to 1 decimal, with the words "the same success to three decimals" allowed only while both successes print the same to 3 decimals and `cost_blind` costs more; how many evals hold the fewest prompts and that count; the oracle's success and cost. No bullet may call `cost_blind` level with `best_on_calibration`
- Checks on the pinned file (v1.18): when the pinned pickle is found (the `ROUTERBENCH_PKL` path when that variable is set, else the default cache) and pandas is installed, the tests also rebuild, from the converter's output, the held-out half sizes of the evals with the fewest prompts over the seeds (the 12.2 split on their prompt IDs, as a lowest-to-highest range) and the graded-evals sensitivity counts (the graded evals dropped by default, the two that `--pass-score 0.75` brings back with their prompt counts, the rest still under 100 prompts, and that matrix's evals, prompts and rows), and require them verbatim in `RESULTS.md`; without `ROUTERBENCH_PKL` they skip when the file or pandas is missing, so CI never downloads the file, and with it set a missing file or a missing pandas is a failure, not a skip. The sensitivity run's routed numbers need an evaluate of about 20 seconds, so they are rebuilt only when `ROUTERBENCH_PKL` is set. The evaluate time and output size in the Regenerate paragraph are not checked

### 12.8 README evidence section (v1.10; AutoBench v1.17; at a glance v1.23; judges v1.28; workflow v1.30; variant points v1.48; tau2-bench v1.55; LiveCodeBench v1.65; delegation on tau2-bench v1.80; its pooled run v1.84; its cached point v1.92; LLMRouterBench v1.164; delegation on LLMRouterBench v1.200; LLM Routing Response Bank v1.296; delegation on the LLM Routing Response Bank v1.316; size budget v1.334; delegation on tau2-bench trimmed v1.336; shrinks with each trim v1.344)
Purpose: the README itself shows what the router did on public data, with links a reader can follow to the source, and no number that can drift from the committed results
- README.md has a section `## Evidence: held-out results on public data`, placed before the price and leaderboard section, with one `###` subsection per dataset that has committed results; v1.17 has two, `### RouterBench` and then `### AutoBench`, and no other `###` heading; v1.22 adds a third after them, `### Delegation on AutoBench` (13.10); v1.28 adds a fourth, `### AutoBench judges` (12.12), between `### AutoBench` and `### Delegation on AutoBench`, so the headings are `RouterBench`, `AutoBench`, `AutoBench judges` and `Delegation on AutoBench` in that order; v1.55 adds a fifth, `### tau2-bench` (12.15), between `### AutoBench judges` and `### Delegation on AutoBench`, so from v1.55 the headings are `RouterBench`, `AutoBench`, `AutoBench judges`, `tau2-bench` and `Delegation on AutoBench` in that order. The section's opening paragraph holds for every subsection: it describes each dataset's label (right or wrong on RouterBench, a Delta-Hit from judge scores on AutoBench, and, v1.28, agreement with the other judges' grades for a judge model's grade in AutoBench judges, and, v1.55, after them, whether an agent's conversation passed the run's program checks on tau2-bench) and says that each cost is either recorded in the dataset or estimated from its token counts at list prices (v1.28), the recorded cost being that of a whole conversation on tau2-bench (v1.55). v1.30: the opening paragraph ends with the 12.13 workflow sentence, which links `.github/workflows/evidence.yml`, names the results files of its `Compare` line (the three `results.json` files; v1.48 adds `evidence/delegation/variants.json` after them, and v1.55 adds `evidence/tau2-bench/results.json` after the judges file and the tau2-bench subsection to the subsections it names, 12.13) and says why RouterBench is left out. v1.65 adds a sixth subsection, `### LiveCodeBench` (12.17), between `### tau2-bench` and `### Delegation on AutoBench`, so from v1.65 the headings are `RouterBench`, `AutoBench`, `AutoBench judges`, `tau2-bench`, `LiveCodeBench` and `Delegation on AutoBench` in that order; the opening paragraph's label list ends, after the tau2-bench label, with whether a model's first sample passed the problem's test cases on LiveCodeBench, its cost clause says in brackets that LiveCodeBench records neither a cost nor token counts, so it is `cost_blind` evidence only, and its workflow sentence names `evidence/livecodebench/results.json` after the tau2-bench file and the LiveCodeBench subsection after tau2-bench (12.13). v1.80 adds a seventh subsection, `### Delegation on tau2-bench` (13.12), after `### Delegation on AutoBench`, so from v1.80 the headings are `RouterBench`, `AutoBench`, `AutoBench judges`, `tau2-bench`, `LiveCodeBench`, `Delegation on AutoBench` and `Delegation on tau2-bench` in that order; the opening paragraph keeps its words, since its labels and costs already name tau2-bench and its workflow sentence's "delegation subsections" covers both, and the sentence's list of files gains `evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/results.json` last (12.13); v1.84 adds `evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/pooled.json` after it, the 13.12 pooled run, and keeps the other words
- Each subsection links the dataset page and its pinned revision, states the licence as the dataset states it, links that dataset's `RESULTS.md`, `results.json` and evidence README, gives the regenerate commands, and states the limits (model age, recorded prices, routing per task rather than per prompt, the task mix, what the converter drops); the delegation subsection (13.10) links the same AutoBench dataset page, pinned revision and licence, and links its own `RESULTS.md` and `results.json` (and, v1.48, `variants.json`) in place of an evidence README
- Results at a glance (v1.23): the opening paragraph is followed, before the first `###`, by the line `Results at a glance, each number from the committed results of the subsection it links:`, a blank line and one bullet per subsection in heading order, then (v1.124) a blank line and the one paragraph of the where-routing-pays bullet below, then (v1.258) a blank line and the one paragraph of the where-delegation-pays-at-a-glance bullet below, and by nothing else. Each bullet starts with a link to its subsection, the heading as display text and GitHub's anchor for it as target (lower case, characters other than letters, digits, spaces, `-` and `_` dropped, spaces to `-`), then a colon, and holds at most two result values (a held-out success, a cost or a cost ratio, at the rounding of that subsection's tables: success and ratio to 3 decimals, cost to 4) taken from that subsection's committed results.json, plus the bar, the parent count and the context sizes that qualify them, and (v1.40) whether inline work takes a parent call of its own. Each keeps its unfavourable result: RouterBench, the `cost_effective` routed row at the lowest bar (held-out success and the 12.7 best-single-model cost ratio) and the best single model, named, whose held-out success no routed row at any bar reaches; AutoBench judges (v1.28), after a lead that names the label as agreement with the other judges and not a human label, the same two values and claim with the best single judge of 12.12 in place of the best single model; AutoBench, that no routed row beats always calling `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` on held-out success and cost together, in the 12.10 sense, with its held-out success and cost; tau2-bench (v1.55), on trial 0, that no routed row beats any of the single agents on held-out success and cost together, in the 12.15 sense, and that at the lowest bar `cost_effective` routing costs more than always calling the cheapest single agent (named, chosen as in 12.15) for no more held-out success, with those two costs as its two values; LiveCodeBench (v1.65), that the dataset records no cost, so only `cost_blind` routing is run, and that it reaches one held-out success at every bar, below that of always calling the best single model (named, chosen as in 12.7), with those two successes as its two values (a file with a second flavor, a routed success that differs between bars, or one that reaches the best model's fails the test); delegation, at bar 0.8, with the brief assumed to carry the whole question and (v1.40) inline work in a parent call of its own (the 13.10 points with `inline_separate_call` True), that the `cost_effective` decision delegates on every seed for every parent at every context size of those points, so its summary rows have the success and cost of always delegating exactly (v1.32; before v1.32 it was level with always delegating to two decimals and cost more), its mean held-out success over the parents against always inline (each the same at every context size, means as in 13.10), and the context sizes where it costs more and where it costs less than always inline, both sets non-empty and the larger-cost sizes first in context order (a file where the decision costs more, or less, at every size fails the test, so that sentence is reworded instead of printed one-sided); delegation on tau2-bench (v1.80), on trial 0, that the `cost_effective` decision delegates on every seed for each of the parents at every bar and point (every `delegate_rate` of its decision rows is 1.0), and that at the lowest bar and point 0 (8,000 context tokens, inline work in a parent call of its own) its mean cost over the parents is the first of two costs against always inline's, for less held-out success, with those two costs as its two values (a file where a decision row delegates on fewer seeds, where that mean cost is not below always inline's, or where the decision's mean held-out success is not below always inline's at 3 decimals fails the test). `tests/test_evidence_at_a_glance.py` builds the whole list from the committed files (four since v1.28, five since v1.55, six since v1.65, seven since v1.80) and requires it verbatim there; each data-dependent word (no routed row reaches or beats, every seed, more, less, no more) is asserted against the files, so a results file that breaks one fails the test instead of leaving a stale sentence
- RouterBench tables, from the committed `results.json` summary, in this order with one blank line between them:
  - routed: one row per (bar, flavor) in config order, columns `Bar` (as Python `str` prints it), `Flavor` (in backticks), `Held-out success`, `Cost (USD)`, `Cost / best single model`, `Cost / cheapest model`, `Cleared rate`, `Bar-violation rate`; the best single model is chosen as in 12.7, and the cheapest model is the `cheapest` summary row of the first (bar, flavor); success, rates and the best-model ratio to 3 decimals, cost to 4, the cheapest-model ratio to 1
  - baselines: the oracle, the best single model (named), `best_on_calibration` and `cheapest` rows of the first (bar, flavor), columns `Baseline`, `Held-out success`, `Cost (USD)`, success to 3 decimals and cost to 4
- `tests/test_evidence_results.py` rebuilds both tables, and the counts sentence (models, evals, how many of them start with `mmlu`, prompts, seeds), from the committed file and requires them verbatim in that subsection (from its `###` heading to the next `###` or `##` heading); it also requires the dataset URL, the revision link (display text the first 7 characters of the converter's `REVISION`, target the tree URL at the full `REVISION`), the code licence link, the three evidence links, the bar range, the kept-of-86 eval count, and the five-line regenerate block taken from `RESULTS.md`
- RouterBench "Reading it" (v1.18): no bullet calls `cost_blind` level with `best_on_calibration`. The test builds from the committed file and requires verbatim in the subsection the `cost_blind` sentence (its held-out success and cost, which must be the same at every bar, those of `best_on_calibration`, and the percentage more it costs, under the three-decimal rule of 12.7), the nominal one-sided rate with the confidence of the Wilson bound, and the oracle's success and cost next to the highest routed success of either flavor at any bar, which must be at least 0.1 below the oracle's for "nowhere near"; and it checks the `cost_effective` bullet's claims against the file: its cleared rate falls and its cost rises as the bar rises, at the two lowest bars it spends under half of the best single model's cost at a lower held-out success than `cost_blind`, and at the highest bar its cost is at least 0.9 of the best single model's
- AutoBench tables (v1.17), from the committed `evidence/autobench-2026/results.json` summary, in the same order with one blank line between them:
  - routed: the RouterBench routed columns, order and formats, except that a rate the file holds as `null` (the bar-violation rate when no seed cleared) is printed as `-`
  - baselines: the oracle, the best single model (named, chosen as in 12.7), the model the dataset card's headline names (`openai/gpt-5.6-luna`, named), `best_on_calibration` and `cheapest` rows of the first (bar, flavor), columns as in RouterBench. The oracle label says some model has a Delta-Hit on every question, so the test also requires the oracle's success to be 1.0
- `tests/test_evidence_autobench_results.py` rebuilds both AutoBench tables, the counts sentence (models, the items of the one pooled task out of the 252 questions, seeds), the bar range and the Luna statement of 12.10 from the committed file and requires them verbatim in the `### AutoBench` subsection (from its heading to the next `###` or `##` heading), next to the card sentence; it also requires the number of files the converter reads (the `FILES` entries other than `SHA256SUMS`, as a word), the dataset URL, the revision link (display text the first 7 characters of the AutoBench converter's `REVISION`, target the tree URL at the full `REVISION`), the CC-BY-4.0 licence link and the attribution, the three evidence links, the default width named as the dataset's `delta_signal` of 0.2007 and not its full-precision value, the 2026-08-29 freeze, the relative-label caveat, the 12 to 33 questions per domain, the dropped counts of 12.9 (49 questions with an unscored pool cell, 107 cells, 388 rows for 13 models outside the pool), and the five-line regenerate block taken from `RESULTS.md`. A test also requires the opening paragraph, before the first `###`, to name the three labels as v1.28 words them (the two of v1.17, then the judge label) and the list-price estimate
- AutoBench judges (v1.28): `### AutoBench judges` links the AutoBench dataset page, its pinned revision (display text and target as in `### AutoBench`) and the CC-BY-4.0 licence with the attribution, links `evidence/autobench-judges/RESULTS.md`, its `results.json` and the 12.11 section of the AutoBench evidence README (`evidence/autobench-2026/README.md#judge-evidence-one-item-per-graded-answer`), and states the answer items, the candidates and the seeds of the committed file, its bar range, the 12.11 default tolerance and that a committee leaves out the judge and the answer model. Its two tables, in the same order with one blank line between them, from the committed `evidence/autobench-judges/results.json` summary:
  - routed: the AutoBench routed columns, order and formats, with the two ratio columns headed `Cost / best single judge` (the best single judge of 12.12) and `Cost / cheapest judge` (the `cheapest` summary row of the first (bar, flavor))
  - baselines: the oracle, the best single judge (named), `best_on_calibration` and `cheapest` rows of the first (bar, flavor), columns as in RouterBench; while the oracle's success is below 1 its label gives the 12.12 fallback (the cheapest judge, counted wrong, where none agrees with the committee), and when it is 1 it says that some judge agrees on every answer
  - Reading it: four of the 12.12 Reading it bullets exactly as the RESULTS.md test builds them (every seed clears, bar violations, the oracle, price), with the price bullet's link to the AutoBench RESULTS.md pointing to `#autobench` instead; the partly-and-never and `cost_blind` bullets are left to RESULTS.md
  - Limits: the committee label is agreement with other model judges, not a human gold label; each cost is a list-price estimate with the 12.11 rubric and grade tokens, never a billed cost; the candidates are those whose set keeps the most answers, chosen for that and not for quality or price, with the kept and dropped counts as the 12.11 evidence README section states them (kept of the scored answers, answered by a candidate, not graded by some candidate); answers to one question are separate items and can fall in both halves. Then the five-line regenerate block taken from RESULTS.md
  - `tests/test_evidence_autobench_judges_results.py` builds both tables, the counts sentence, the four bullets and the limits from the committed file, the 12.11 defaults and the evidence README counts, and requires each verbatim in the subsection (from its heading to the next `###` or `##` heading), with the links, the licence and the regenerate block
- tau2-bench (v1.55): `### tau2-bench` links the repo page `https://github.com/sierra-research/tau2-bench` and its pinned revision (display text the first 7 characters of the 12.14 converter's `REVISION`, target the tree URL at the full `REVISION`), states the MIT licence as the repo's LICENSE file does, with its copyright line, links `evidence/tau2-bench/RESULTS.md`, its `results.json` and `evidence/tau2-bench/README.md`, and states the agents, the three domain tasks with their item counts, the trial and the seeds of the committed file, its bar range, that the bars were chosen after a first run at 0.6 to 0.9 (12.15), and what a success and the cost are (12.14). - When no model clears (v1.144; six datasets from v1.341, row R32): `research/no-model-clears.md` and its script `research/no_model_clears.py` (standard library and `model_routing`) ask whether a `cost_effective` router, on a cell where no model clears the bar and the task's `on_no_model` is `best_available`, should keep the 5.3 fallback (the highest calibration bound) or take the cheapest model. The script takes the matrices of the six routing datasets that record a cost (LiveCodeBench records none) as their RESULTS.md Regenerate blocks write them: `--routerbench`, `--autobench`, `--judges` with `--judges-registry`, `--tau2`, and from v1.341 `--llmrouterbench` and `--response-bank` (the LLMRouterBench and response-bank converters' default matrices, 12.18 and 12.20), each read once with `load_outcomes`, `--judges` with its registry and the rest with none. It reads each dataset's bars, seeds, `calibration_fraction`, `min_samples` and `confidence` from its committed results file (12.7, 12.10, 12.12, 12.15, 12.19, 12.21) and runs `evaluate` (12.3) with that config on the `cost_effective` flavor alone. Per dataset, in the order of those options with a blank line between datasets, it prints one line with the tasks, models, bars and seeds, then per bar: the cells on which no model clears (`cleared` false) as a count and share of the bar's cells, with their held-out items, per task when the dataset has at most five tasks and otherwise the number of tasks with such a cell, and on how many of them the routed model is the `best_on_calibration` model and the `cheapest` model; on those cells three rows, `fallback` (the `routed` strategy there), `cheapest` and `best_on_calibration`, each with its held-out success (correct held-out items over held-out items, pooled over the cells), its cost per held-out item and the cells on which its held-out success is at least the bar; and the bar's whole `cost_effective` row as 12.3's `summary` computes it (the mean over seeds of each seed's pooled success and of its summed cost), once as routed and once with the `cheapest` strategy in place of `routed` on the cells where no model clears, with the change in success and the change in cost as a share. The as-routed row must equal the committed summary's `routed` row at that bar and flavor, success and cost each within a relative 1e-12, else the script stops. A bar on which every model clears prints only its count line. Exit 0, or 2 with one `error: ` line on a missing or unreadable file, a row `load_outcomes` refuses, a missing key or a routed row that differs from the committed one. The note opens with `## Facts` and holds, in order, `## Method`, `## Results` (one `text` block, the command line then the output, compared byte for byte with the script's lines on the cached pinned files when they and pandas are present, skipped otherwise; from v1.341 those include the pinned LLMRouterBench and response-bank files), `## Reading it`, from v1.341 `## LLMRouterBench and the response bank (2026-11-13)` (the share of the two datasets' `cost_effective` cells where no model clears as the bar rises and, on those cells, the cheapest model's held-out success and cost per item against the fallback's, per bar or as a range over the bars, with the whole row's change; v1.343: each bar's values are in the Results block), `## Recommendation`, `## Limits` and `## Regenerate`; its Facts, Method, Recommendation and Limits cover every dataset the script reads, the Method linking each one's RESULTS.md; every number of its prose outside the block and outside code spans, dates aside, is in that output or is a DESIGN section number. README Limitations gains one bullet linking it, after the bullet on 13.5 row 7, and `research/README.md` its row and a bullet. From v1.341 the bullet says the note `measures it on the six routing datasets that record a cost, RouterBench, AutoBench, the AutoBench judges, tau2-bench, LLMRouterBench and the LLM Routing Response Bank, and recommends keeping the rule`, the row opens its answer `On the six routing datasets that record a cost (RouterBench, AutoBench, the AutoBench judges, tau2-bench, LLMRouterBench and the LLM Routing Response Bank)`, and a test checks that each lists the script's `DATASETS` in order and that its count word is their number. - Per-task ceiling on RouterBench (v1.146): `research/routerbench-per-task.md` and its script `research/routerbench_per_task.py` (standard library, `model_routing`, and the 12.6 converter loaded from its path, whose reader needs pandas, the `evidence` extra) ask how much per-task routing could gain in success on RouterBench if each eval took its own best model. The script never downloads: it reads the pinned file from the converter's cache folder (`--cache`, default the converter's `default_cache_dir()`; v1.148: `--root`, default this checkout, names the repository root that holds the results file) through the converter's checked reader, `verify` (size and sha256), `load_frame` (the restricted unpickler), `frame_columns` and `convert` with its defaults, so the matrix is the 12.6 default one; it reads the config of the committed `evidence/routerbench/results.json` (12.7) and runs `evaluate` (12.3) with its seeds, `calibration_fraction`, `min_samples` and `confidence` at its first bar on the `cost_blind` flavor alone. The best single model is 12.7's (the `model:` summary row with the highest held-out success, then lower cost, then ID). It prints: one line with the file, its revision, the evals, models, items and seeds, `calibration_fraction` and `confidence`; the best single model; one row per eval, ascending, with its items, its calibration half (the `statistics.median_low` over seeds of its cells' `calibration_items`), its best model on all its items (highest correct count, then lower total cost by `math.fsum`, then ID), how many models share that correct count, that model's success and the best single model's on all its items, the margin between them, the Wilson shortfall of the best model's success at the half (`success - wilson_lower_bound(round(success * half), half, confidence)`, as in the v1.124 bullet), on how many seeds that model is also the best on the held-out half (highest held-out success, then lower held-out cost, then ID) and how many models are that best on some seed; then the evals whose best model is not the best single model, by name, how many of them have a higher success than it, how many by more than the shortfall, and the largest margin with its eval, model and shortfall (v1.148: `largest margin: none above 0` when no eval has a higher success); on how many cells and evals the `routed` and the `best_on_calibration` model is not the best single model, and (v1.148) how many of the evals where `routed` takes another model have two or more routed models across seeds, with the lowest and highest calibration half among them; then, for all evals, the evals whose name starts with `mmlu` and the rest, six rows: the best single model, `routed`, `best_on_calibration`, the per-eval best on all items, the per-eval best on each seed's held-out half and the 12.3 `oracle`, each with its held-out success to 4 decimals and cost as 12.3's summary computes them over that group's evals (the mean over seeds of each seed's pooled success and of its summed cost) and the mean over seeds of its correct held-out items minus the best single model's; and, for margins 0.02, 0.05 and 0.1 at a success of 0.8, the calibration half `items_needed(0.8, round(0.8 - margin, 2), confidence)` (4.3), from which the shortfall at that success is within the margin, and the share of items that evals with that margin over the best single model must hold for a gain of 0.01 in success (0.01 / margin). Every `model:` row, `routed`, `best_on_calibration` and `oracle` over all evals must equal the committed summary's at that bar on `cost_blind`, success and cost each within a relative 1e-12, else the script stops. Exit 0, or 2 with one `error: ` line on a missing or unreadable file, a file whose size or sha256 is not the pinned one, a refused or failed unpickle, a missing key or a row that differs from the committed one. The note opens with `## Facts` and holds, in order, `## Method`, `## Results` (one `text` block, the command line `$ python research/routerbench_per_task.py` then the output, compared byte for byte with the script's lines when the pinned file and pandas are present, skipped otherwise), `## Reading it`, `## What another dataset needs`, `## Limits` and `## Regenerate`; every number of its prose outside the block, a link target and a code span is in that output or is a DESIGN section number. `research/where-routing-pays.md` `## Limits` links it in its bullet on the per-task single-model rows, and `research/README.md` gains its row and a bullet. Tests: `tests/test_research_routerbench_per_task.py`. No change under `model_routing/`, to any results file or to any README transcript
- Seed by seed on LLMRouterBench (v1.177): `research/llmrouterbench-seeds.md` and its script `research/llmrouterbench_seeds.py` (standard library and `model_routing`) ask whether the one held-out success gain over the best single model in the committed routing files, LLMRouterBench's (the v1.166 clauses of the where-routing-pays bullet), holds on each seed or only in the mean over seeds that 12.3's `summary` gives, since no committed file holds a seed's value. The script never downloads: `--matrix` names the 12.18 converter's output, the matrix of the 12.19 commands, read once with `load_outcomes`, and `--root`, default this checkout, the repository root that holds `evidence/llmrouterbench/results.json`, whose `config` (bars, seeds, flavors, `calibration_fraction`, `min_samples` and `confidence`) it runs `evaluate` (12.3) with. The best single model is 12.7's, read from the committed summary (the `model:` row with the highest held-out success, then lower cost, then ID; a file where it differs between bars or flavors is an error). On a seed a strategy's pooled held-out success is its correct held-out items over the held-out items, both summed over that seed's cells of one bar and flavor as in 12.3's summary, and its cost the `math.fsum` of those cells' `cost_usd`; the seed's gap is that success minus the best single model's on the same cells, and the seed is above, equal or below by the two correct counts. It prints: one line with the results file, the tasks, models, items, bars and seeds, and the lowest and highest held-out items of a seed; one line naming the best single model and saying what a gap is; one strategy line for `best_on_calibration`, read at the first bar and flavor (12.3 v1.135 makes it the same at every one); then per flavor, in config order, a `<flavor> routed:` line, one strategy line for `routed` per bar in config order, and one line with the number of seeds whose gap has the same sign (above, equal or below) at every bar and the number of (task, seed) cells routed to the same model at every bar. A strategy line gives the seeds above, equal and below out of all seeds; the lowest, median (`statistics.median`, so the mean of the two middle gaps for an even count of seeds), highest and mean gap, signed to 4 decimals; and the lowest and highest of its per-seed cost over the best single model's, to 3 decimals (`-` when that model's cost is 0 on some seed). At every bar and flavor the mean over seeds of the per-seed success of `routed`, `best_on_calibration` and the best single model must equal the committed summary row's `success`, and (v1.179) the mean over seeds of its per-seed cost that row's `cost_usd`, each within a relative 1e-12, else the script stops, so the cost ratios rest on the committed costs too. Exit 0, or 2 with one `error: ` line on a missing or unreadable file, a row `load_outcomes` refuses, a missing key or a row that differs from the committed one. The note opens with its title and a paragraph, then holds, in order, `## Facts`, whose first bullet states the finding without a number, `## Method`, `## Results` (one `text` block, the command line `$ python research/llmrouterbench_seeds.py --matrix llmrouterbench-matrix.jsonl` then the output, compared byte for byte with the script's lines on the matrix that the 12.19 session fixture `llmrouterbench_pinned` writes when the pinned file is cached, skipped otherwise), `## Reading it`, `## Limits` and `## Regenerate`; every number of its prose outside the block, a link target and a code span is in that output or is a DESIGN section number. A test checks on that output that `best_on_calibration` and the `cost_blind` routed row at every bar are above the best single model on every seed, the finding the README's LLMRouterBench lines rest on; if a later matrix fails it, the README wording is a new row, not an edit made here. `research/where-routing-pays.md` `## Limits` links the note in one bullet, and `research/README.md` gains its row and a bullet. Tests: `tests/test_research_llmrouterbench_seeds.py`. No change under `model_routing/`, to any results file, to README.md or to any README transcript
- Fallback on a failed check, on LLMRouterBench (v1.181): `research/check-fallback.md` and its script `research/check_fallback.py` (standard library and `model_routing`) ask what the executor's move to the next model when `validate` is falsy (7) buys on public data, which no committed row measures, since each routed row scores the primary alone. LLMRouterBench records every pool model on every item, so a check as good as the record's score can be replayed exactly. `--matrix` and `--root` are those of the seed by seed bullet, and the script runs `evaluate` (12.3) with the committed config at two bars, the lowest and highest of its `bars`. For each cell it rebuilds the route as 12.3 builds the cell's router (the calibration counts and mean calibration costs of the cell's `split_items` halves of the matrix, then 12.3's registry and `Task`), once for each `max_fallbacks` of 0, 2 and the number of pool models minus one, the last giving the whole list that 5.3 sorts (every passing model when one passes, else every eligible measured one); the route is `(primary, *fallbacks)`, and the script stops unless every route's primary is the cell's routed model. On each held-out item the route's models are tried in turn until a record scores 1 (12.18 `correct`), and every attempt's recorded `cost_usd` is spent, so on a cell a route's success is the held-out items some attempt scores 1 on and its cost the `math.fsum` of every attempt's cost; both are pooled over a seed's tasks and averaged over seeds as 12.3's `summary` does, and so is the attempts per held-out item. It stops unless, at both bars and in each flavor, `max_fallbacks` 0's success and cost are the committed summary's `routed` row and each task's are the committed `per_task` `routed` row, and the oracle's (12.3) the committed `oracle` row, each within a relative 1e-12. It prints: one line with the results file, the tasks, models, items and seeds, the two bars and the number of committed bars; one line saying what a route and an attempt are; the oracle's success and cost; then per flavor in config order and per bar, a `<flavor> bar <bar>:` line and one line per `max_fallbacks` value giving success and cost (4 decimals), the cost over `max_fallbacks` 0's and the attempts per item (3 decimals), the shortest and longest route, and the share of the routed to oracle success gap it recovers, (success minus `max_fallbacks` 0's) over (the oracle's minus `max_fallbacks` 0's) on the means, to 3 decimals, `-` when the two are equal; then a line naming the highest bar and, per task ascending, one line with its items, each flavor's held-out success at `max_fallbacks` 0 and with the whole list, the shortest and longest whole-list route over both flavors and the seeds (v1.182), and the oracle's. Exit 0, or 2 with one `error: ` line on a missing or unreadable file, a row `load_outcomes` refuses, a missing key, a route whose primary is not the routed model or a row that differs from the committed one. The note opens with its title and a paragraph, then holds, in order, `## Facts`, whose first bullet says that every number is an upper bound, for a check as good as the record's score, `## Checks per task` (one table row per task: how the dataset's own scoring code made the score, linked at a pinned commit of the dataset's code repository, and whether a caller could run that kind of check at call time without the answer key), `## Method`, `## Results` (one `text` block, the command line `$ python research/check_fallback.py --matrix llmrouterbench-matrix.jsonl` then the output, compared byte for byte with the script's lines on the matrix of the 12.19 session fixture `llmrouterbench_pinned` when the pinned file is cached, skipped otherwise), `## Reading it`, `## Limits` and `## Regenerate`; every number of its prose outside the block, a link target and a code span is in that output or is a DESIGN section number. A small-matrix test checks success, cost, attempts and the recovered share against hand-computed values. `research/README.md` gains its row and a bullet. If the finding bears on the README Limitations' first bullet, the README wording is a new backlog row, not an edit made here; R1's confidence cascade stays a separate question. Tests: `tests/test_research_check_fallback.py`. No change under `model_routing/`, to any results file, to README.md or to any README transcript in v1.181 to v1.184. README Limitations (v1.185) holds, first, one bullet that says the router routes per task, not per prompt, picking each task's first model with no difficulty prediction or confidence-based cascade, and that what reacts per call is the executor's fallback when the caller's `validate` check fails (7); then, from the note's Results block and after saying they are upper bounds (v1.186), the lowest and highest recovered share and cost ratio over both flavors and bars, for two fallbacks and for the whole sorted list (`max_fallbacks` the number of pool models minus one), saying that at a high bar that list holds only the models that clear it (5.3); then that a check at call time can only do worse and every task's score but `arenahard`'s is made against an answer key a caller does not hold, in part `livecodebench`'s; and it links the note, the one Limitations bullet that does. The note's last `## Reading it` bullet (v1.186) says that bullet now states the per-call move and links the note. The same module builds the bullet from the note's Results block and checks it; no transcript changes
- Below-bar fallback, on LLMRouterBench (v1.191): `research/below-bar-fallback.md` and its script `research/below_bar_fallback.py` (standard library, `model_routing` and `research/check_fallback.py`, loaded from its own folder by path) ask whether a route should go on below the bar after its clearing models fail, which 5.3 did not do before v1.201 and does not do by default: when any model passes and the task's `fallback_below_bar` (4.2, v1.201) is off, `fallbacks` come from the passing list alone, so a call whose passing models all fail the caller's check ends with `AllModelsFailed` (7), while research/check-fallback.md (v1.182) finds that at the highest bar this leaves the fallback short of the oracle on the tasks where one or a few models clear. `--matrix` and `--root` are those of the fallback on a failed check bullet, and the script runs `evaluate` (12.3) with the committed config at the lowest and highest of its `bars`. For each cell it rebuilds the router as that bullet does, from the same calibration counts and mean costs, once, with `max_fallbacks` the number of pool models minus one, so the decision's primary and fallbacks are the whole 5.3 list, and stops unless the primary is the cell's routed model. It then forms two routes for each `max_fallbacks` of 0, 2 and the number of pool models minus one: the 5.3 route, that list cut after the primary and `max_fallbacks` more models; and the below-bar route, the same list then every other candidate that is `eligible` with `trials > 0`, sorted by the 5.3 `best_available` key `(-quality_lb, routing_cost, model_id)`, cut the same way. So the 5.3 route is always the first part of the below-bar route, the two are the same when no model passes (the 5.3 list then already holds every eligible measured model), and every success the below-bar route adds is an item on which every model of the 5.3 route failed, answered by a model under the bar. Each held-out item is replayed down a route as in that bullet (in turn until a record scores 1, every attempt's recorded cost spent), and success, cost and attempts per item are pooled over a seed's tasks and averaged over the seeds the same way. It stops unless, at both bars and in each flavor, the 5.3 route with `max_fallbacks` 0 gives the committed summary's `routed` row and each task's committed `per_task` `routed` row, and the oracle (12.3) the committed `oracle` row, each within a relative 1e-12. It prints: the first line of that bullet's script; one line saying what the two routes and an attempt are; the oracle's success and cost; then per flavor in config order and per bar, a line `<flavor> bar <bar>: no fallback success <s>, cost <c>; a model clears the bar in <k> of <n> cells` (k the cells whose decision has `below_bar` False, n the tasks times the seeds) and, per `max_fallbacks` of 2 and the number of pool models minus one, one line giving each route's success and cost (4 decimals) and its shortest and longest route, the below-bar route's attempts per item (3 decimals), the cells where the two routes differ, the success the below-bar route adds (4 decimals), its cost over the 5.3 route's (3 decimals, `-` at a zero cost) and the share of the 5.3 route's gap to the oracle it recovers, (its success minus the 5.3 route's) over (the oracle's minus the 5.3 route's), to 3 decimals, `-` when the two are equal; then a line naming the whole-list `max_fallbacks`, and per bar a `bar <bar>:` line and, per task ascending, one line with its items, per flavor both routes' held-out success with the whole list and the cost ratio (`-` at a zero cost, as on the pooled line, v1.193), the cells of both flavors where the two routes differ, and the oracle's success. Exit 0, or 2 with one `error: ` line on what stops that bullet's script. The note opens with its title and a paragraph, then holds, in order, `## Facts`, whose first bullet says that every number is an upper bound, for a check as good as the record's score, `## Method`, `## Results` (one `text` block, the command line `$ python research/below_bar_fallback.py --matrix llmrouterbench-matrix.jsonl` then the output, compared byte for byte with the script's lines on the matrix of the 12.19 session fixture `llmrouterbench_pinned` when the pinned file is cached, skipped otherwise), `## Reading it`, `## Recommendation` (an option on `Task` with the default unchanged, or none, and the reason) and `## Limits` and `## Regenerate`; every number of its prose outside the block, a link target and a code span is in that output or is a DESIGN section number, and it names no backlog row. A hand-computed test on the four models and eight items of the check fallback tests checks both routes, the added success and the cost ratio line by line; a small hashed matrix checks on every cell that the 5.3 route is the check fallback script's route and the first part of the below-bar route, and the script's checks and exits. `research/README.md` gains its row and a bullet, and the check fallback note's `## Reading it` bullet that called this a design question for a backlog row links the note instead. If the note recommends an option on `Task`, that is a new backlog row, not a change made here. Tests: `tests/test_research_below_bar_fallback.py`. No change under `model_routing/`, to any results file, to README.md or to any README transcript
- Size budget (v1.334, row DOC19): at 0b3e142 README.md is 154,317 bytes and this section 104,199 of them (67.5%), more than six times Quickstart (16,254 bytes), the next largest `##` section. Each measure (v1.335, from review) is the UTF-8 bytes from a heading line, included, through the line before the next heading of the same or a higher level, each line with its newline, so the section runs to the next `##` line and its opening (to its first `###`) and each subsection to the next `###` or `##` line, a blank line before that heading included. The opening is 6,942 bytes and its eleven subsections are RouterBench 4,731, AutoBench 5,078, AutoBench judges 5,691, tau2-bench 7,310, LiveCodeBench 6,194, LLMRouterBench 8,093, LLM Routing Response Bank 8,173, Delegation on AutoBench 13,715, Delegation on tau2-bench 14,187, Delegation on LLMRouterBench 12,881 and Delegation on the LLM Routing Response Bank 11,204. The budget: the section at most 70,000 bytes, its opening at most 8,000, each routing subsection (the first seven) at most 6,500 and each delegation subsection (the last four) at most 7,500. The opening and subsection caps sum to 83,500 bytes: the caps are ceilings and the keep rules set the size, so the keep rules below apply to every subsection, under its cap or not, and a cap only stops one subsection from growing back. A reader who wants every number has the subsection's RESULTS.md one link away, so each subsection keeps, in its present order and words:
  - its source lines: the dataset link and pinned revision, the licence, what was run, and the links to its RESULTS.md, results.json and evidence README (for a delegation subsection, its opening paragraph)
  - its headline results: for a routing subsection, the paragraph that defines held-out success and the ratios, the routed table, the baselines table and any sentence between the tables and `Reading it:`; for a delegation subsection, one `$ python evidence/delegation/results.py --table` transcript, the whole table when that fenced block (its fence lines and every newline, the closing fence's included) is at most 2,500 bytes (Delegation on AutoBench 2,171 and on the response bank 2,185 at 0b3e142), else the per-parent block at the lowest bar and point 0 that follows it (tau2-bench 1,182, LLMRouterBench 2,425)
  - at most two Reading-it bullets, the one on the `cost_effective` rows and the one on the `cost_blind` rows where the subsection has one on each, else the first two in its order (v1.335, from review: AutoBench, AutoBench judges, LiveCodeBench and the four delegation subsections have no such pair); a delegation subsection's bullets after its transcripts are its Reading it, and each row names the two it keeps
  - at most one Limits bullet, the one on where each cost comes from, with when the models date from where it says so (for the three delegation subsections with a Limits list, the lossless brief of 13.9; Delegation on AutoBench has no Limits list and keeps none), then one line that links the RESULTS.md section holding the rest (its `## Caveats` or `## Assumed` where it has one, else its `## Reading it`)
  - its regenerate paragraph and blocks, unchanged, since tests/test_evidence_workflow.py compares them with the `evidence` workflow (12.13)
  - Everything else in a subsection (its other Reading-it and Limits bullets, the delegation transcript it does not keep, the pooled-run and cached-point text of Delegation on tau2-bench) is in its RESULTS.md: each line not already there moves there in the same change, and each test that required the line in the subsection requires it in RESULTS.md instead. A subsection still over its cap after these keeps one Reading-it bullet or shortens its source lines, never a table
  - Rows DOC19a to DOC19k trim one subsection each, in order of present size, largest first: Delegation on tau2-bench (DOC19a), Delegation on AutoBench (DOC19b), Delegation on LLMRouterBench (DOC19c), Delegation on the LLM Routing Response Bank (DOC19d), LLM Routing Response Bank (DOC19e), LLMRouterBench (DOC19f), tau2-bench (DOC19g), LiveCodeBench (DOC19h), AutoBench judges (DOC19i), AutoBench (DOC19j) and RouterBench (DOC19k). Each amends this section's bullets for its subsection (the one that adds it and its tables, Reading it, Limits and Tests sub-bullets, which require its lines verbatim in the subsection) and the results section its text moves to (in heading order 12.7, 12.10, 12.12, 12.15, 12.17, 12.19, 12.21, 13.10, 13.12, 13.13 and 13.14), with a changelog line, and changes no results file, no `evidence` workflow line and no `$ model-routing` README transcript; from v1.344 that amendment of this section deletes or moves what it says of the lines a trim drops (the shrink bullet below)
  - DOC19a adds tests/test_readme_evidence_budget.py: it measures the opening and each subsection as above, maps each `###` heading of the section to its class (routing or delegation) in an explicit table, so a heading the table lacks fails, requires the opening and each trimmed subsection at most its cap, keeps the untrimmed ones in a `PENDING` map from heading to its size at 0b3e142 that each later row empties by one name, a pending subsection at most that size and never required over its cap (LiveCodeBench is under its cap already), fails when a `PENDING` name is not a `###` heading of the section, and, once the map is empty, requires the section at most 70,000 bytes (v1.335, from review). A subsection added later meets the same cap, and a row whose new subsection would take the section past 70,000 bytes first trims another subsection or states a new budget here
  - 12.8 shrinks with each trim (v1.344, row DOC20): this section is measured as above, from its `### 12.8` line through the line before the next heading of the same or a higher level. Each of rows DOC19b to DOC19k, in its spec commit, deletes each sentence of this section that only specifies README lines its trim drops where the spec of the results section that now holds the lines already states them, and moves the sentence there otherwise, its changelog line naming both (v1.345, from review: a sentence only specifies dropped lines when every README line it names, by its words, order, numbers or tests, is one the trim drops, and a sentence that also names a kept line loses only its clauses on the dropped ones); those specs are 12.7 (RouterBench), 12.10 (AutoBench), 12.12 (AutoBench judges), 12.15 (tau2-bench), 12.17 (LiveCodeBench), 12.19 (LLMRouterBench), 12.21 (LLM Routing Response Bank), 13.10 (Delegation on AutoBench), 13.12 (Delegation on tau2-bench), 13.13 (Delegation on LLMRouterBench) and 13.14 (Delegation on the LLM Routing Response Bank). For example, after DOC19a's trim, the Delegation on tau2-bench bullet's v1.84 sentence that puts three pooled-run bullets after the point 2 window bullet specifies only lines v1.337 dropped, so it goes, while its v1.84 sentence on the `The pooled run:` line and the pooled regenerate block, which the subsection keeps, stays. v1.346 and v1.347 (row DOC20a) apply this to the sentences DOC19a's trim left in that bullet and lowers the entry for 10 names to the size it leaves. Which sentences go is checked by review, not by a test. tests/test_readme_evidence_budget.py `DESIGN_12_8_CEILINGS` maps a count of `PENDING` names to a ceiling on this section in bytes: the section is at most the entry for the present count, a count with no entry fails, and an entry for a smaller count is smaller, so the row that removes a `PENDING` name adds the entry for the new count, the size its trim leaves. A change outside the trims that would take this section past its present entry first deletes as many bytes elsewhere in it, or raises that entry, below the entry for the next larger count where one exists, and says why in its changelog line. Each entry is this section's size at the version that adds it: for 10 names v1.347 (98,443 at v1.344, 99,206 at v1.345 and 96,239 at v1.346, its size at each), for 9 v1.348 (row DOC19b), for 8 v1.350 (row DOC19c)
  - This clause changes no README text

### 12.9 Public evidence: the AutoBench 2026 converter (`evidence/autobench-2026/convert.py`, v1.14; tokens and registry v1.21)
Purpose: a second public outcome matrix in the 12.1 format, with 2026 models and billed cost per call. Standard library only (argparse, csv, hashlib, json, math, re, sys, urllib, pathlib), so it needs no extra; the package never imports it
- Source, pinned: dataset `AutoBench/router-benchmark-2026-10` on Hugging Face at revision `43755180be7d8097068982d7a3f37fc04cd15379`, licence CC-BY-4.0 (the card asks users to cite its paper). `FILES` maps each file the converter reads or checks to its byte size and sha256: `matrix.csv` (1,899,644 bytes), `questions.csv` (298,329), `pool.json` (6,582), `frozen_params.json` (2,042) and `SHA256SUMS` (1,198). The pins in the script are the trust root; the dataset's own `SHA256SUMS` must agree with them for the four data files, as a second check that the pins came from this revision
- `default_cache_dir()` is `~/.cache/ai-model-routing-evidence/autobench-2026/` (from `Path.home()`), never inside the repo. `fetch(cache_dir=None) -> Path` returns that folder after, for each file in `FILES` order: a present file is verified and kept, and one that fails `verify` is a `ValueError` naming it and is left in place; a missing one is downloaded with `urllib.request.urlopen` from `https://huggingface.co/datasets/AutoBench/router-benchmark-2026-10/resolve/<revision>/<name>` into `<name>.part`, verified, then renamed, and the `.part` file is removed when that fails. Last, `check_sums` runs on the text of `SHA256SUMS`
- `verify(path, size, sha256)`: `ValueError` naming the expected and the actual value when the byte size or the sha256 differs, as in 12.6
- `check_sums(text)`: reads lines of the form `<64 lowercase hex>  <name>` (two spaces, as `sha256sum` writes them); blank lines are skipped and any other line is a `ValueError`. `ValueError` when one of the four data files has no entry, or an entry that differs from `FILES` (the message names the file)
- `model_id(openrouter_id) -> str`: a string with exactly one `/` and both sides non-empty, else `ValueError`. Hyphens are removed from the vendor part with `re.sub("-", "", vendor)`, so `x-ai/grok-4.6` becomes `xai/grok-4.6` (models.json writes `xai/grok-4.7`) and `z-ai/glm-5.3` becomes `zai/glm-5.3`; the model part is kept as is. A rule, not a list, so a new hyphenated vendor needs no code change
- `pool_models(pool) -> dict[str, str]`: display ID to `model_id(openrouter_id)` for each entry of `pool["members"]`, in pool order. `ValueError` when `pool` is not a dict, `members` is missing or not a non-empty list, an entry is not a dict, a `display_id` or `openrouter_id` is not a non-empty string, a display ID repeats, or two entries map to one ID
- `pool_registry(pool) -> dict` (v1.21): a registry in the models.json format that `Registry.load` (4.1) reads, built from the pool's list prices: `{"source": {"dataset": "AutoBench/router-benchmark-2026-10", "revision": <the pinned revision>, "file": "pool.json"}, "models": [...]}`, one model per `members` entry, sorted by ID ascending so the pool order does not matter, each with the keys `id` (`model_id(openrouter_id)`), `family` (as written), `price_in` and `price_out` (`input_cost_per_mtok` and `output_cost_per_mtok` as floats, USD per 1M tokens, the unit of 4.1) and `context_window`, in that order. `ValueError` for an invalid pool as in `pool_models` (checked first, with its messages), and for a member whose `family` is not a non-empty string, whose `input_cost_per_mtok` or `output_cost_per_mtok` is not a finite number `>= 0` (a bool is not a number), or whose `context_window` is not an int `>= 1` (a bool is not an int); these messages name the display ID and the key. The prices are the pool's recorded list prices, frozen with it, so a cost from this registry is a list-price estimate (13.9), never the billed `cost`
- `delta_hit(score, pool_scores, width, top_k) -> bool`: the reference is the `math.fsum` of the `top_k` highest values of `pool_scores` divided by `top_k`, and the result is `score >= reference - width`. This is the dataset's Delta-Hit: with `width = delta_signal` and `top_k` from `frozen_params.json`, it gives the `delta_hit_signal` value of the dataset's `decisions.csv` on all 6,291 router decisions that carry one (checked when this section was written, against every scored pool cell of the question; `>` instead of `>=` also gives 6,291, so the boundary is not tested by the data)
- `convert(matrix, questions, pool, width, top_k=3, task_by="domain", tokens=False) -> tuple[list[dict], dict]`; `matrix` and `questions` are lists of dicts as `csv.DictReader` yields them (every value a string), `pool` is the parsed `pool.json`:
  - `ValueError`, checked before any output and the pool first, because `top_k` is bounded by its size: an invalid pool as in `pool_models`; a `width` that is a bool, not an int or float, not finite or `<= 0`; a `top_k` that is a bool, not an int, below 1 or above the number of pool models; a `task_by` other than `domain` and `all`; a `tokens` that is not a bool; a question whose `prompt_id` is not a non-empty string or repeats, or whose `ddc_domain` is not a non-empty string when `task_by` is `domain`; a matrix row whose `prompt_id` or `model` is not a non-empty string, whose `prompt_id` is not a question, or whose (`prompt_id`, `model`) pair repeats; for a pool model's row, a non-empty `score` that does not parse as a finite float, or a scored row whose `cost` does not parse as a finite float `>= 0` or, only when `tokens` is True, whose `input_tokens` or `output_tokens` is missing or does not parse as a finite float that is a whole number `>= 0` (these messages name the `prompt_id`, the model and, for a token count, the column)
  - repeated pairs: a (`prompt_id`, `model`) pair in two rows, for example once with `source` `original` and once with `fill_7_4`, is refused, never resolved by picking one. The pinned file has none: its 388 `fill_7_4` rows are all for 13 models outside the 20-model pool
  - out of pool: rows for a model that is not a pool display ID are dropped and counted (in the pinned file, exactly those 388 `fill_7_4` rows, which the dataset uses to grade router picks outside the pool)
  - unscored: an empty `score` is an unscored cell, and a pool model with no row for a question counts as one too. A question is kept only when every pool model has a score on it, so the output is the complete matrix 12.3 requires; a question with an unscored pool cell is dropped and counted, never scored as a miss
  - each kept question is one item: item = `prompt_id`; task = its `ddc_domain` as written (for example `9 History`), or `all` for every item when `task_by` is `all`; one row per pool model with model = its mapped ID, `correct` = `delta_hit(score, the scores of all pool models on that question, width, top_k)` and `cost_usd` = the billed `cost` as a float, with a negative zero written as `0.0`
  - output rows have the keys `task`, `item`, `model`, `correct`, `cost_usd` in that order, sorted by (task, item, model) as strings; the result does not depend on the row order of either file
  - tokens (v1.21): with `tokens` True each row also has `input_tokens` and `output_tokens`, in that order after `cost_usd`: that cell's `matrix.csv` columns written as ints (the pinned file writes them as, for example, `303.0`). `output_tokens` already counts the reasoning tokens (13.9), so `reasoning_tokens` is never read. With `tokens` False, the default, no token column is read, so the rows, the report and every error are those of v1.14. The report is the same in both
  - the report has keys `models` (IDs ascending), `width`, `top_k`, `task_by`, `kept` and `dropped_unscored` (each `{task: number of questions}` with tasks ascending), `unscored_cells` (pool cells with no score, missing rows included), `dropped_out_of_pool` (`{model as written: rows}`, ascending) and `rows` (the number of output rows)
- `read_csv(path) -> list[dict]` reads UTF-8 with `csv.DictReader`; `write_jsonl(rows, path)` as in 12.6
- CLI: `python evidence/autobench-2026/convert.py --out PATH [--cache DIR] [--width W] [--task-by {domain,all}] [--tokens] [--registry PATH]`, entry point `main(argv=None) -> int`. Options are checked first, so a bad one downloads nothing (`--out`, and `--registry` when it is given, must pass the 12.14 `--out` check, in that order and before the four-files check below, v1.174, row H4b; to v1.173 only the four-files check came before the fetch, so a bad `--out` or `--registry` failed at the write or the rename after the download); then it fetches and checks the files, takes `top_k`, and `delta_signal` as the width unless `--width` is given, from `frozen_params.json`, converts, writes `<out>.part` (and, with `--registry`, the registry's `.part` file), prints the report as `json.dumps(report, indent=2)`, flushing stdout, and only then renames them (v1.94, as 13.11), so a write to stdout that fails (a closed pipe) is an I/O error that leaves `--out` and `--registry` as they were. After a `BrokenPipeError` from the print, stdout is pointed at `os.devnull` as in 13.11 (v1.150), so the process exits 2 with that one `error:` line, not 120 with two more lines from the interpreter's flush at exit. `--width 0.3011090610836323` is the dataset's official width (`delta_official`). `--tokens` (v1.21) passes `tokens=True`. `--registry PATH` (v1.21) also writes `json.dumps(pool_registry(pool), indent=2)` plus a newline to PATH; a `--registry` for which `--out`, `--registry` and their two `.part` paths do not resolve to four different files (for example `--registry` equal to `--out`, or `--out x.part --registry x`) is a usage error whose message says they name the same file, and an invalid pool for the registry fails before any file is written. Both files are written in full to their `.part` paths before either is renamed, `--out` first. A `--width` that is not a finite number `> 0` is a usage error whose message names the value; a `frozen_params.json` that is not an object, has no `top_k`, or has no `delta_signal` when `--width` is not given is invalid data whose message names the file and the key. It returns 0 on success and 2 with exactly one `error: ...` line on stderr and nothing on stdout for a bad option, a size, hash or `SHA256SUMS` mismatch, invalid data or an I/O error, but for a rename that fails after the print (v1.94), the one failure with the report already on stdout; on any failure before the renames neither `--out` nor `--registry` is written, and each `.part` file is removed
- Default options on the pinned files (checked when this section was written): 20 models; 203 of 252 questions kept, 49 dropped for 107 unscored pool cells; 4,060 rows; 10 domain tasks of 12 to 33 questions. Mean hit rate over all cells 0.667 at the default width and 0.762 at the official width. With `--tokens` (v1.21) the rows are the same with the two counts added: `input_tokens` 66 to 2,341 and `output_tokens` 3 to 42,188; 2 rows are billed 0, and on the other 4,058 the `--registry` list prices times those counts give a median ratio of 1.000 to the billed `cost`
- Reading it: a Delta-Hit is relative, not an absolute acceptance check. It means "rated about as well as the best three answers in this pool on this question" by a committee of up to 10 judge models taken from the same pool, so a hit depends on which other models were asked, and the ratings are model judgments, not gold labels. The costs are the billed USD per call in the matrix frozen on 2026-08-29 (the card dates the `fill_7_4` rows 2026-09-05, and every one of them is dropped). A domain has 12 to 33 kept questions, so its calibration half holds fewer than 30 items and the default `min_samples = 30` (5.3) cannot be met per domain; `--task-by all` pools them into one task of 203 items, which is the matrix the results slice commits (the per-domain matrix only as a stated sensitivity at a lower `--min-samples`)

### 12.10 AutoBench results (`evidence/autobench-2026/results.json`, v1.16)
Purpose: the evaluator's numbers on the AutoBench matrix in the shape of 12.7, regenerated byte for byte from stated commands, with the tables and the numbers RESULTS.md states (listed under Tests below) checked against the committed file or, for the sensitivity runs and the split sizes, against the pinned files when they are cached
- Script: `evidence/routerbench/results.py` (12.7) is reused unchanged. Nothing in `reduce` or `table` depends on the dataset, so the AutoBench file is written and printed by the same `--evaluate` and `--table` commands with AutoBench paths
- Committed: `evidence/autobench-2026/results.json`, from the converter's `--task-by all` matrix at the default width (12.9: one task, `all`, of 203 items over 20 models) and `model-routing evaluate --bar 0.6 0.7 0.8 0.9 --seeds 50 --json` at the default `min_samples` 30 and confidence 0.95, reduced by `results.py --evaluate`; and `evidence/autobench-2026/RESULTS.md` with the regenerate commands, the caveats of 12.9 "Reading it", and the `table` output in a fenced `text` block whose first line is `$ python evidence/routerbench/results.py --table evidence/autobench-2026/results.json`
- The dataset card's headline: RESULTS.md quotes the card at the pinned revision, "no commercial pass beats always calling GPT-5.6 Luna on quality and cost together", and states from the committed summary whether any routed (bar, flavor) row beats the `model:openai/gpt-5.6-luna` row of the first (bar, flavor) on both counts: held-out success at least as high and cost at most as high, compared at full precision, one of the two strictly. The test builds that statement from the file, so it changes when the numbers do. RESULTS.md also gives the number of the 50 seeds that `cost_effective` at bar 0.7 routes to that model, taken from its routed per-task row
- Sensitivity, stated in RESULTS.md with the commands that give it and not committed as files: the official width (`--width 0.3011090610836323`) at bar 0.8, and the per-domain matrix (the default `--task-by domain`, 10 tasks of 12 to 33 items) at `--min-samples 5` at bars 0.6 and 0.8, each with 50 seeds
- Tests, in `tests/test_evidence_autobench_results.py`: the committed file is under 300,000 bytes, is exactly `json.dumps(reduce(itself), indent=2)` plus a newline, has that config, the one task `all` with 203 items and the converter's 20 model IDs, unique summary and per-task keys, and 50 seeds behind every `routed_models`; RESULTS.md contains the table block with the current `table` output, the five-line regenerate block, the card sentence, the Luna statement, the bar 0.7 seed count with the direction of its gap to that model and the weaker single-model rates of the other models it routes to, the "Reading it" numbers built from the file (the bar 0.6 cost ratio and success against the best single model, the cleared-seed count and cost ratio at bar 0.8, the best single model's success at 0.9, the `cost_blind` row next to `best_on_calibration`, the bar-violation counts at bars 0.7 and 0.8 over the seeds that cleared, the nominal `(1 - confidence) / 2`) and the committed file's byte size. When the pinned files are in the default cache (12.9), three more tests rebuild `results.json` in process (convert, `evaluate` with `cells` removed, `reduce`) and require the committed bytes; rebuild every sensitivity number and require each phrase; and rebuild the calibration-half sizes (pooled over the 50 seeds, and per domain over every (domain, seed) split, whose largest must stay below `min_samples`) with the count of per-domain halves smaller than the fewest questions on which a perfect score has a 95% Wilson lower bound of 0.8, and the pooled half sizes in the "Reading it" sentence for bar 0.9 (v1.17). CI never downloads the files, so there they skip. RESULTS.md says what the card graded only in the card's own words, in double quotes: "Seven routers were then graded by lookup: a router only names a model; we read that cell", and, for `scoreboard.json`, "The published board" and "28 passes" (v1.17).
