- Test status: the tests that read the committed file were committed before it, each under `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=FileNotFoundError)`, which the commit that adds the file (v1.292) removes; the span, calibration-half and zero-cost tests read only the pinned files and pass before it
- Two 12.20 tests from review iter-196 nits 1 and 2 (v1.291), in `tests/test_evidence_response_bank.py`: `read_jsonl` of a line nested past the recursion limit is a `ValueError` naming `<file name>:<line>`, and an item with both a scored cell that has no bill and sample-0 responses whose `prompt_sha256` differs is counted only in `dropped_unbilled`, by the 12.20 check order. Both pass on the v1.290 converter
- RESULTS.md (v1.293, row E20c): `evidence/response-bank/RESULTS.md` holds, in order: an opening paragraph on what is measured (the 5 models, the 4 families as routing tasks with their 12.20 item counts, a success as a `quality` of 1, the cost as each response's billed `official_usd`) and one on the bars (the rule above, with the span's two ends and their (task, model) pairs, and what the 0.9 rows show); `## Source and licence`: the dataset URL, the revision linked to `https://huggingface.co/datasets/Lurume/llm-routing-response-bank/tree/<REVISION>`, the four `FILES` names, each with its size and sha256; the dataset's LICENSE file at that revision (1,480 bytes, sha256 `ebff7b5d9a32da5f04702a29a8b6ce45bbc7b805963cfe45aeb85cd00e0ef6cd` when read on 2026-11-12) quoted whole but for its title, each paragraph or list item joined onto one line: the terms paragraph, which names ODC-BY-4.0 as planned and grants its terms until a formal publication with attribution to the dataset repository, the lead-in of its list, "Per-family terms additionally apply:" (quoted from v1.295, review iter-198 nit 1), each of its four per-family terms (`<family>` subset: "<terms>"), and its closing no-warranty paragraph; then the attribution itself, the dataset's name and repository URL, since the card's `## Citation` section reads `To be added.` at that revision; and that the repo commits no part of any family's questions or of any response; `## Results`, a sentence on the columns, the `table` output in a fenced `text` block whose first line is `$ python evidence/routerbench/results.py --table evidence/response-bank/results.json`, and the 12.15 single-model sentence with `single models` in place of `single agents`, built from the file; `## Reading it`; `## Regenerate`, a five-line `bash` block (`python -m pip install -e .`, the three commands above, then the `--table` command) and a line on the download (1,018,191,580 bytes in four files) and its cache folder
- Reading it (v1.293), each number built from the committed file by the test: for `cost_effective` at the lowest bar, the (task, seed) cells a model clears out of all 200 and the seeds each task clears, `aime` on none (a file where `aime` clears on some seed fails the test); the cost ratio to the best single model of 12.7 (named) with both held-out successes; always calling the cheapest single model (chosen as in 12.15, named) with its success and cost, and the router's cost as a share of that model's, to 3 decimals, for `more` or `no more` held-out success; the `cheapest` baseline's success and cost (12.3: per task, the model of lowest mean calibration cost); and each model that gets every seed of some task, with those tasks (a file where no task goes to one model on every seed fails the test); at the highest bar, the cells cleared and the seeds of each task that clears (a file where none clears fails the test), and that `cost_effective` then has the held-out success and cost of `best_on_calibration` (a file where it does not fails the test), with its success and cost ratio; `cost_blind`'s one success and cost ratio at every bar, how far that success is above the best single model's, to 4 decimals (a file where it is not above fails the test), the span of the single models' held-out successes, and how many seeds of the largest task it routes at the lowest bar to the model with the most of them, which must be the single model with the highest cost (a file where it is not fails the test), with that cost; each (bar, flavor) whose bar-violation rate is above 0, with its violated and cleared cell counts, against the nominal one-sided 0.025; the oracle's success and cost against the highest routed success of either flavor at any bar; and the file size. From the pinned files, checked by the cached test: the `aime` calibration-half sizes above, the 6 kept `missing` cells, the 450 kept length finishes and the 17 of them scored 1, and that the converter drops no item. Limits, as `## Reading it` bullets: each cost is a recorded bill with cache discounts, never a list price of `models.json`, which has none of the five models; routing is per family, not per prompt, and `mmlu_pro` holds 12,032 of the 13,315 items, so the pooled rows mostly measure it; the missing cells and the length finishes as 12.20 counts them; and that the release's two splits are joined and the halves are the library's own (12.20 Choices)
- Evidence README (v1.293): `evidence/response-bank/README.md` says what the folder holds and links `RESULTS.md` and `results.json`; gives the dataset URL, the revision, the four `FILES` with size and sha256, and the terms paragraph and the attribution as RESULTS.md does; a table with the header `| raw ID | model |` of the 5 `MODELS` in order with their `model_id`; a table with the header `| task | calibration | evaluation | items |` of the 4 families, ascending, with the release's split counts (evaluation as the card's `Families (evaluation counts)` line states them, `aime` 22, `gpqa_diamond` 166, `livecodebench` 967 and `mmlu_pro` 11,904; calibration the rest) and the 12.20 item counts; a table with the header `| task | missing | length | length scored 1 | other finish |` of the 12.20 report's `missing_cells`, `length_finishes`, `length_successes` and `other_finishes` per family; what a row keeps (`task`, `item`, `model`, `correct`, `cost_usd`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`) and that no prompt, reference answer, response text or reasoning text is kept; and a two-line `bash` block that makes the matrix (`python -m pip install -e .`, then the convert command of 12.21)
- Tests (v1.293), in `tests/test_evidence_response_bank_results.py`, 8 more, 16 in all: RESULTS.md holds the table block with the current `table` output, the regenerate block, the source strings (the tree link, each file with its size and sha256, the seven quoted LICENSE paragraphs and items (the list's lead-in from v1.295), the attribution, the span's ends and the 0.9 clause), the single-model sentence and every Reading it number above, and is ASCII with no en or em dash and no home path; on a synthetic copy of the file in which the routed row at the lowest bar has no more success than the cheapest single model the phrase builder says `no more`, and on one in which `cost_blind` is not above the best single model it fails; the evidence README holds the converter's constants, the three tables built from `MODELS`, `model_id`, the 12.20 item and finish counts and the card's evaluation counts, the links, the terms and the row keys; when the four pinned files are cached, a test requires the README's split and finish tables to be the converter report's and the calibration counts of `scores_cal.jsonl` (read with `read_jsonl`), every dropped count 0, and the `aime` half sentence, missing-cell count and length counts of RESULTS.md to be the rebuilt ones. The 6 tests that read RESULTS.md or the evidence README were committed before them under strict `xfail(raises=FileNotFoundError)`, which the commit adding the two files (v1.294) removes. From review iter-197 nit 2, `test_real_zero_costs_are_the_missing_cells_and_never_fill_a_pair` says in a comment that a null `input_tokens` is the only mark a `missing` cell leaves in a row

## 13. Delegation: inline or sub-agent (`delegation.py`, v1.4; cost accounting v1.8)
Purpose: an agent (the **parent**: a model already running with its own context) reaches a piece of work that is one call of a task, or from v1.38 K successive calls of it (13.1). It can do the work itself (**inline**) or hand it to a fresh sub-agent running the model the router picks (**delegate**). The decision weighs what routing weighs, the measured success rate against the task's bar and list-price cost, plus what each path does to the parent's context. The delegate is chosen by `Router.select`, so the 5.2 rules, the Wilson admission rule and both flavors apply unchanged.

### 13.1 Inputs
`Router.decide_delegation(task_name, parent_model, flavor="cost_effective", *, context_tokens, brief_tokens, result_tokens, carry_turns=0, work_calls=1, inline_separate_call=True, cached_read_ratio=1.0, generator_model=None, exclude_models=()) -> DelegationDecision`
- `parent_model`: the parent's model ID. An unknown task, parent or `generator_model` raises `KeyError`; an unknown flavor raises `ValueError` (5.1)
- `context_tokens` (C): tokens already in the parent's context
- The work is one call of the task unless `work_calls` says more (v1.38): each call reads S_in = `task.expected_input_tokens` and writes S_out = `task.expected_output_tokens`; from v1.33 the cost of each path uses the 5.2 sizes of the model doing the work (13.2)
- `work_calls` (K, v1.38, default 1): the work is K successive calls of the task on one path. Each call takes S_in of new input and writes S_out, and call j (j = 1 to K) reads its path's base context (C inline, B delegated) plus the j inputs and the j - 1 outputs so far, so the path's context grows by S_in + S_out per call. K must be an integer `>= 1` that is not a bool, else `ValueError` naming `work_calls`, checked after `carry_turns`
- The bar and the bounds stay per call whatever K: K changes the cost of each path (13.2), the 13.4 context check and the delegate's `select` (13.3), never a success count, a bound or a bar check, so the 13.5 rows read the same bounds. `DelegationDecision` (13.6) has no field for K
- `brief_tokens` (B): what the parent writes to hand the work off. `result_tokens` (R): what the parent reads back
- `carry_turns` (T): parent calls after the parent's next call (13.2) that still re-read what this work leaves in its context. The next call itself is always charged, so T = 0 means the parent makes one more call after the work and stops. When the work is one of several sibling subtasks that the parent would otherwise do one after another, each later sibling done inline re-reads this one, so those calls count in T
- `inline_separate_call`: True (the default) when inline work takes a parent call of its own, False when the parent folds the work into its next call (13.2). It must be a bool, else `ValueError`
- `cached_read_ratio` (rho, v1.41, default 1.0): the price of the parent's cached re-reads of its existing context C, as a fraction of the parent's list input price, for a parent whose provider caches the prompt prefix it has already read; 13.2 says which reads it prices. It must be an `int` or a `float` that is not a bool, with 0 <= rho <= 1 (so NaN and the infinities fail), else `ValueError` naming `cached_read_ratio`, checked after `inline_separate_call`. It moves `inline_cost_usd` only: never `delegate_cost_usd` (from v1.219 except, on a parent with `price_tiers`, the dearer re-read of C in a shared parent call that a path takes over a threshold, on both paths, 13.2), the delegate's `select` (13.3), the 13.4 checks, a bound or a bar check. `DelegationDecision` (13.6) has no field for it
- `generator_model` and `exclude_models` mean what they mean in 5.2 and are passed to the delegate's `select`. `exclude_models` limits the delegate only; it never stops the parent working inline
- `context_tokens`, `brief_tokens`, `result_tokens` and `carry_turns` must be integers `>= 0` that are not bools, else `ValueError`
- The router is not modified: `router.tasks` is the same after the call

### 13.2 Cost of each path (list price, reported in both flavors)
The accounting follows the parent's calls, each of which re-reads the parent's whole context. Both paths are charged for the same calls, and each only for what that path adds to them:
- The **decision turn**, the parent call that makes this decision, happens on both paths. On the delegate path it also writes the brief, so B is charged once as parent output; on the inline path it writes nothing extra. Fetching the work's input is free on both paths; reading it is charged on both
- The parent's **next call** after the work is done (on the delegate path, the call that reads the result) and the T calls after it happen on both paths and re-read C on both, so C is not charged in them. Each of these 1 + T calls is charged for the tokens that the path left in the parent's context. What a call writes besides the work, the parent's own next step, is the same on both paths and is not charged
- The work itself: the delegate reads the brief and the work's input in a fresh context and writes the output. Inline, the parent does it either in a call of its own over its whole context (`inline_separate_call=True`) or inside its next call (`inline_separate_call=False`), which then also reads S_in and writes S_out. A separate call fits work that is a step of its own, for example output that goes into a tool call or a file before the parent goes on; folding fits work the parent would do as part of its very next reply anyway. The default, a separate call, gives the higher inline cost

With P the parent's model and D the delegate, for work of one call (K = 1; the v1.38 bullet below gives K calls):
- `delegate_cost_usd = P.cost(0, B) + D.cost(B + S_in, S_out) + (1 + T) * P.cost(B + R, 0)`: the brief written, the delegate's call, then the brief and the result re-read by the next call and the T after it
- `inline_cost_usd = P.cost(C + S_in, S_out) + (1 + T) * P.cost(S_in + S_out, 0)` when `inline_separate_call` is True: the parent's own call for the work, then its input and output re-read by the next call and the T after it
- `inline_cost_usd = P.cost(S_in, S_out) + T * P.cost(S_in + S_out, 0)` when `inline_separate_call` is False: the next call also reads S_in and writes S_out, and both are re-read by the T calls after it
- With K = 1, C appears only in the separate inline call. With a separate call, inline minus delegating to the parent's own model (D = P) is, in exact arithmetic, `P.cost(C, 0) - P.cost(B, B) + (1 + T) * (P.cost(S_in + S_out, 0) - P.cost(B + R, 0))`, so delegating even to its own model can save in two ways: the separate call's re-read of a large context C (the context-isolation case), and later calls that re-read a short brief and result instead of the work's input and output. When the work folds into the next call, C cancels, and delegating to the parent's own model is cheaper only when `T * P.cost(S_in + S_out, 0)` exceeds `P.cost(B, B) + (1 + T) * P.cost(B + R, 0)`, so never with T = 0
- Each expression is evaluated left to right as written, in floating point, from `Model.cost` (4.1); `1 + T` is an integer. `delegate_cost_usd` is `None` when there is no delegate
- v1.212 every `Model.cost` that 13.2 and 13.9 name is `Model.base_cost` (4.1): a total of K calls and a re-read increment carry no one call's input size, so no tier applies to them. On a model without tiers the two are the same value bit for bit, so every decision, reason and committed delegation result keeps its v1.211 bytes; on a model with `price_tiers` both paths stayed at the base prices from v1.212 to v1.218. With K = 1 the delegate's `select` (13.3) is a 5.2 select over one call of the briefed sizes and keeps `Model.cost`, so on a tiered model it ranked the delegates at that call's tier while `delegate_cost_usd` charged the base prices; with K > 1 its candidates were priced at their K calls' total with `Model.base_cost`, as 13.3 v1.38 states (v1.214). From v1.219 (below) a term a tiered model pays is priced per call, so this bullet holds only for a model without tiers, and at K = 1 the delegate is charged what its `select` ranks it at; from v1.222 the 13.9 held-out costs of a tiered model are priced per call too (13.9 v1.222)
- v1.219 per-call tiers (row P1b): on a model with `price_tiers` (4.1) each term of the expressions above that this model's calls pay is priced call by call, each call at its own tier, in place of the v1.212 `Model.base_cost` of a total. A term paid by a model without tiers keeps its v1.212 expression, so on a registry without tiers every decision, reason and committed delegation result keeps its v1.218 bytes (no committed delegation registry has a tier). Write in(n) and out(n) for a model's prices in a call that reads n input tokens (the 4.1 tier of that call, else `price_in` and `price_out`); each call's cost is its tokens times those prices over 1,000,000, in floating point. C, B, R, T, K, rho, S_in and S_out are as above, the sizes those of the model doing the work (v1.33):
  - Work calls. Call j of a path's K (j = 1 to K) reads X + j * S_in + (j - 1) * S_out, X the path's base context (C inline, B delegated), and writes S_out. On a tiered model it pays in(its read) on its charged input and out(its read) on its S_out output; its charged input is its read on the delegate, and rho * C + j * S_in + (j - 1) * S_out inline, C at rho as in v1.41, while its tier is that of the whole read whatever rho. The work term is the `math.fsum` of the calls' costs, j ascending. So with K = 1 the delegate's work term is `D.cost(B + S_in, S_out)` exactly, the cost its K = 1 `select` ranks it at (13.3), and from K = 2 that `select` prices each tiered candidate the same way (13.3 v1.38)
  - Shared parent calls. The decision turn, the parent's next call and the T calls after it happen on both paths and read C, and each is charged only for what the path adds to it, A input tokens (and, for a folded next call, the S_out it writes). On a tiered parent such a call is charged `A * in(C + A) + rho * C * (in(C + A) - in(C))` on input, plus its added output at out(C + A): the added tokens at the tier of the call's whole read, and the dearer re-read of C when A takes the call over a threshold that C alone is not over, at rho like every cached read of C (13.1); the second part is 0 when in(C + A) = in(C), and negative when the tier the call reaches is cheaper than C's own, since the path then lowers the price of the whole call (4.1 does not require a tier to be dearer; no packaged tier is cheaper). So the decision turn's brief on the delegate path is `B * out(C)` (A = 0), each of the 1 + T calls after delegated work has A = B + R, and each after separate inline work has A = K * (S_in + S_out)
  - Folded inline work. The K-th work call is the parent's next call, a shared call with A = K * S_in + (K - 1) * S_out and S_out written, so the folded work term is the `math.fsum` of the K - 1 work calls before it (the work-call bullet) and that next call's charge, in that order, and each of the T calls after it has A = K * (S_in + S_out)
  - Each path is then the v1.212 sum with its terms so priced, evaluated left to right: delegated, brief + the delegate's work + (1 + T) * one call after it; separate inline, the work + (1 + T) * one call after it; folded, the folded work term + T * one call after it. A tiered delegate under a parent without tiers moves only the delegate's work term, and a tiered parent only the parent's terms
  - Not charged, as before: the parent's own output in a shared call, the same tokens on both paths, so where a path's additions take such a call over a threshold the higher price of that output is left out, an under-statement that README Limitations states. The 13.4 context check, the bounds and the 13.5 rows are unchanged
  - v1.242 why that output stays uncharged (row P1d): no input gives it a size. Of the shared calls, only the 1 + T calls after the work can be at a tier that differs between the paths: the decision turn reads C on both paths, A = 0, so it pays in(C) and out(C) on both, and the brief it writes on the delegated path is the `B * out(C)` above. What each of the 1 + T calls writes besides the work, the parent's own next step (in a folded next call, besides its S_out), is the size of no 13.1 input: B is what the decision turn writes, R what the next call reads back and S_out what each work call writes, and each is charged where this section charges it. So a call of those 1 + T that reads C + A on one path and C + A' on the other leaves out o * (out(C + A) - out(C + A')) of the difference between the paths, o that call's own output, which no input states: 0 on a parent without tiers and wherever both reads are in one tier, else an under-statement of the path whose read is in the dearer tier. Pricing it would need a new input, the size of the parent's own output in each later call, which v1.242 does not add: the 13.9 rows record only the work's calls, so no committed delegation result could set it. No cost, reason, check or committed result changes
- v1.33: S_in and S_out in these expressions are the 5.2 sizes of the model doing the work on that path. In `inline_cost_usd` they are the parent's, S_in(P) and S_out(P), in its call for the work and in the later re-reads of that input and output; in `delegate_cost_usd` they are the delegate's, in `D.cost(B + S_in(D), S_out(D))`. For a model without measured token counts they are the task's expected tokens, so with no token totals each expression is the v1.32 one. With D = P both paths use the same sizes, so the D = P difference above holds with S_in(P) and S_out(P). The 12.3 evaluator builds its stats from counts alone, so its results do not move; from v1.35 the 13.9 harness adds each model's calibration-half token totals to its stats, so its decisions price each path at these per-model sizes (13.9)
- v1.38, K work calls (13.1). Write w_in = K(K + 1) / 2 and w_out = K(K - 1) / 2, both integers. The K calls of a path with base context X read `K * X + w_in * S_in + w_out * S_out` tokens in all and write `K * S_out`, and each path prices its K calls as one `Model.cost` of those integer totals (from v1.212 `Model.base_cost`, below), which is the sum of the K calls' costs at the base prices in exact arithmetic since `Model.base_cost` is linear (4.1); on a model with `price_tiers` it is not the sum of the K calls' tiered costs. S_in and S_out are the sizes of the model doing the work, as in v1.33. With P the parent and D the delegate:
  - `delegate_cost_usd = P.cost(0, B) + D.cost(K * B + w_in * S_in + w_out * S_out, K * S_out) + (1 + T) * P.cost(B + R, 0)`: the brief written once, the delegate's K calls, then the brief and the result re-read by the next call and the T after it
  - `inline_cost_usd = P.cost(K * C + w_in * S_in + w_out * S_out, K * S_out) + (1 + T) * P.cost(K * (S_in + S_out), 0)` when `inline_separate_call` is True: K parent calls of the work's own, each over the whole context, then the K inputs and outputs re-read by the next call and the T after it
  - `inline_cost_usd = P.cost((K - 1) * C + w_in * S_in + w_out * S_out, K * S_out) + T * P.cost(K * (S_in + S_out), 0)` when `inline_separate_call` is False: the last of the K calls is the parent's next call, which re-reads C on both paths, so C is charged only in the K - 1 calls before it. Folding takes the last work call into the next call, never all K
  - With K = 1, w_in = 1 and w_out = 0, so the token totals are the integers of the three expressions above and every cost, reason and check is the v1.37 one bit for bit
  - With a separate call, inline minus delegating to the parent's own model is, in exact arithmetic, `P.cost(K * C, 0) - P.cost(K * B, B) + (1 + T) * (P.cost(K * (S_in + S_out), 0) - P.cost(B + R, 0))`: each inline call re-reads C where each delegated one re-reads B, which is where context isolation pays most. Folded it is `P.cost((K - 1) * C, 0) - P.cost(K * B, B) + T * P.cost(K * (S_in + S_out), 0) - (1 + T) * P.cost(B + R, 0)`, so from K = 2 a large C can make delegating to its own model cheaper even with T = 0
- v1.41, cached context reads (13.1). The reads rho prices are the parent's reads of C in the inline work calls: the K calls of its own when `inline_separate_call` is True, the K - 1 calls before its next call when it is False. They are the only reads of C that 13.2 charges (the decision turn, the next call and the T calls after it read C on both paths and are not charged), and each of them finds C as the prefix of its prompt, just read by the decision turn. Those K * C or (K - 1) * C tokens are priced at rho times the parent's list input price; everything else stays at list price on both paths: the work's input and output, their re-reads by later work calls and by the 1 + T later parent calls, the brief, the result, every read of the delegate, and all output. With P the parent:
  - `inline_cost_usd = P.cost(K * C * rho + w_in * S_in + w_out * S_out, K * S_out) + (1 + T) * P.cost(K * (S_in + S_out), 0)` when `inline_separate_call` is True
  - `inline_cost_usd = P.cost((K - 1) * C * rho + w_in * S_in + w_out * S_out, K * S_out) + T * P.cost(K * (S_in + S_out), 0)` when `inline_separate_call` is False
  - `delegate_cost_usd` is the v1.38 expression whatever rho, and so is the delegate
  - The first argument of `P.cost` is evaluated left to right: the integer `K * C` or `(K - 1) * C`, times rho, then the integer terms added, so with a float rho it is a float. With rho = 1.0 its value is the v1.38 integer total, and `Model.cost` (from v1.212 `Model.base_cost`) converts an int to that same float before it multiplies by a float price, so every cost, reason and check is the v1.40 one bit for bit (while the token totals and their products with the prices stay below 2^53, about 9e15); with the int rho = 1 the argument is the v1.38 integer itself. Folded work of one call has no C term, so its cost is the same for every rho
  - In exact arithmetic rho scales the C term of each inline minus delegating difference in the v1.38 bullet. With a separate call it is `P.cost(K * C * rho, 0) - P.cost(K * B, B) + (1 + T) * (P.cost(K * (S_in + S_out), 0) - P.cost(B + R, 0))`, so at rho = 0 the parent's context no longer favours delegating to its own model, and only later calls that re-read a short brief and result instead of the work can; folded, the C term is `P.cost((K - 1) * C * rho, 0)`
  - Not modelled: a surcharge for writing the cache, a cache that expires between calls, a discount on any other re-read on either path, and a cached price for the delegate. rho is one number the caller states for the parent; the AutoBench pool (13.9) has no cached prices, so evidence with rho < 1 states it as an assumption
- This is still an approximation. It assumes that each of the K work calls reads its path's base context and every earlier work call in full, with nothing summarised between calls (13.8), no prompt caching beyond the v1.41 ratio on the parent's reads of C, the same parent output after the work on both paths (checking a delegate's result may take more), and that the parent's later calls fit its context on both paths: C + K * (S_in + S_out) is checked for inline (13.4) but C + B + R is not checked for delegate

### 13.3 The delegate
- The delegate is the primary of `Router.select(task_name, flavor, generator_model, exclude_models)` run with the same registry, stats and priors on a copy of the task whose `expected_input_tokens` is `S_in + B`, so the context rule (5.2 rule 5) and the `cost_effective` ranking see the brief the delegate actually reads. It may be the parent's own model
- `route` is that `RouteDecision`, or `None` when that `select` raises `NoModelClearsBar`. `delegate_quality_lb` is the `quality_lb` of the route's primary candidate. The delegate clears the bar when `route.below_bar` is False
- The parent's share of `delegate_cost_usd` is the same for every delegate, so the `cost_effective` primary of this `select` is also the passing delegate with the lowest `delegate_cost_usd`
- v1.33: in that `select` every model's input size is its 5.2 input size plus B, so a model with measured token counts is priced at `model.cost(mean input + B, mean output)` and any other at `model.cost(S_in + B, S_out)`, which keeps the bullet above true; rule 5 still checks the copy's expected tokens, S_in + B + S_out. One way is a private copy of the stats with B times `token_trials` added to each input total, since rounding up `(I + B * n) / n` gives the rounded-up `I / n` plus B exactly. `router.stats` and `router.tasks` are the same after the call
- v1.38, K work calls: in that `select`, rule 5 checks each model's window against the delegate's last call, `B + K * (S_in + S_out)` with the task's expected sizes (that call reads B + K * S_in + (K - 1) * S_out and writes S_out), not against the K calls' total, and each candidate's `list_cost`, and its `routing_cost` in `cost_effective`, is the cost of its K calls, `model.cost(K * B + w_in * S_in + w_out * S_out, K * S_out)` (from v1.212 `model.base_cost`, the K calls' total at the base prices; from v1.219, on a model with `price_tiers`, the `math.fsum` of its K calls each at its own tier, 13.2) with its 5.2 sizes (13.2), so the `cost_effective` primary is still the passing delegate with the lowest `delegate_cost_usd`, and the 13.7 exit-3 table prints that cost. Pricing a model at its last call alone would rank by the wrong mix of input and output price, since the K calls' input grows as K squared and their output as K. With K = 1 this is the v1.34 `select`, run as v1.34 runs it. One way for K > 1 is a private `Router` subclass whose candidates are checked and priced this way; `router.stats` and `router.tasks` are the same after the call

### 13.4 Is inline allowed?
The first rule that applies blocks inline (the parent must delegate) and becomes the reason:
1. the parent's family is in `task.exclude_families`: `parent_excluded_family`
2. `task.independent_of_generator`, `generator_model` is given, and the parent is that model or in its family: `parent_not_independent` (the parent cannot check its own family's output)
3. `P.context_window < C + K * (S_in + S_out)`, with the task's expected S_in and S_out whatever the parent's measured sizes (v1.33, as 5.2 rule 5): `parent_context_full`. The last of the K work calls (v1.38) reads C + K * S_in + (K - 1) * S_out and writes S_out, folded or not; K = 1 gives the v1.37 check C + S_in + S_out

The parent clears the bar when its measured trials on the task are at least `task.min_samples` and `parent_quality_lb = wilson_lower_bound(successes, trials, task.confidence)` is at least `task.bar`: the admission test of 5.2 rules 6 and 7. `parent_quality_lb` is 0.0 when the parent is unmeasured; the parent is measured when it has `trials > 0`, as in 5.3

### 13.5 Choosing (the first row that applies wins)
1. Inline blocked: delegate, with the blocking reason and `below_bar = route.below_bar`. With no route, raise `NoModelClearsBar`
2. The delegate clears the bar and the parent does not: delegate, `parent_below_bar`
3. Both clear, `cost_effective`: delegate when `delegate_cost_usd < inline_cost_usd` (`delegate_cheaper`); inline when it is greater (`inline_cheaper`); on an exact tie, delegate when `delegate_quality_lb > parent_quality_lb` (`delegate_better`), else inline (`inline_as_good`)
4. Both clear, `cost_blind`: delegate when `delegate_quality_lb > parent_quality_lb` (`delegate_better`), else inline (`inline_as_good`). Cost is not compared
5. Only the parent clears: inline, `only_parent_clears`
6. Neither clears and `task.on_no_model` is `"refuse"`: raise `NoModelClearsBar`
7. Neither clears and `task.on_no_model` is `"best_available"`: reason `none_clear`, `below_bar` True. Delegate when `route is not None and route.basis == "measured" and (parent unmeasured or delegate_quality_lb > parent_quality_lb)`; else, in `cost_effective` (v1.31), delegate when `route is not None and route.basis == "measured"`, the parent is measured, `delegate_quality_lb == parent_quality_lb` and `delegate_cost_usd < inline_cost_usd`; else inline when the parent is measured; else delegate when there is a route (its basis is `prior`); else raise `NoModelClearsBar`
- `below_bar` is False in rows 2 to 5. A raised `NoModelClearsBar` carries the `.candidates` of the failed `select`
- Costs and bounds are compared with plain `<`, `>` and `==` on the floats as computed, never with a tolerance, so two costs that differ only by rounding are not a tie
- Equal-quality ties go inline in `cost_blind` (rows 4 and 7) and, in `cost_effective`, on an exact cost tie (an exact cost tie in row 3 is settled by the bounds first), because a hand-off has costs the model does not price: a brief can drop detail, and the parent has to trust a result it did not produce. In `cost_effective` a strictly lower `delegate_cost_usd` delegates in row 3 whatever the bounds, and in row 7 when the bounds are equal (v1.31)
- A model below the bar is chosen only under `best_available`, in row 1 or row 7, and then `below_bar` is True
- v1.31: row 7 orders the two paths as 5.3 orders the `best_available` fallback, by bound first and then, in `cost_effective` only, by the path's list-price cost (13.2), so a measured delegate whose bound equals a measured parent's and whose `delegate_cost_usd` is strictly lower is taken. The usual case is the parent's own model as the delegate (its bound is the parent's, from the same stats), where delegating saves the separate inline call's re-read of C (13.2). The costs are compared as computed (no tolerance, as above), so a delegate path that is cheaper only by rounding is taken. With a measured parent, `cost_blind`, an exact cost tie, a lower delegate bound and a `prior` route stay inline; an unmeasured parent still delegates to any route, as before. The reason is `none_clear` in every branch of row 7, so no reason code is added (13.6)
- Row 5 has no equal-model case: a parent that clears in 13.4 reaches 5.2 rule 8 (`passes`) in the delegate's `select` unless rules 1 to 5 stop it, since rules 6 and 7 read the same stats, bar, confidence and `min_samples`, and one passing candidate makes the route clear the bar. So when the route is below the bar, the parent was stopped there by rule 1 (it is in `exclude_models`) or rule 5 (its window holds C + S_in + S_out but not S_in + B + S_out); rules 2 to 4 are the 13.4 blocks that send the decision to row 1. Row 5 stays inline in both flavors, since its delegate does not clear the bar and the parent does; that includes a different delegate whose bound equals or exceeds the parent's but that has too few trials (5.2 rule 6)

### 13.6 `DelegationDecision`
- `@dataclass(frozen=True) class DelegationDecision`, fields in this order: `task: str`, `flavor: str`, `parent: str`, `mode: str` (`"inline"` or `"delegate"`), `model: str` (the parent for inline, `route.primary` for delegate), `reason: str`, `below_bar: bool`, `inline_allowed: bool`, `inline_cost_usd: float`, `delegate_cost_usd: float | None`, `parent_quality_lb: float`, `delegate_quality_lb: float | None`, `route: RouteDecision | None`
- `.to_dict()` returns plain JSON-able types, keys in the field order above, with `route` as `route.to_dict()` or `None`
- Module constants with these exact values: `INLINE = "inline"`, `DELEGATE = "delegate"`, and reason codes whose values are their names in lower case: `PARENT_EXCLUDED_FAMILY`, `PARENT_NOT_INDEPENDENT`, `PARENT_CONTEXT_FULL`, `PARENT_BELOW_BAR`, `DELEGATE_CHEAPER`, `INLINE_CHEAPER`, `DELEGATE_BETTER`, `INLINE_AS_GOOD`, `ONLY_PARENT_CLEARS`, `NONE_CLEAR`
- `DelegationDecision` is re-exported from `model_routing` and listed in `__all__`; the constants stay in `model_routing.delegation`
- v1.11: `Executor.call_delegated(decision, messages, ...)` runs a `delegate` decision on its route, with fallback and logging as for any call (7)
- v1.38: the fields, `to_dict()` and the constants are unchanged; K is an input only (13.1)
- v1.41: the fields, `to_dict()` and the constants are unchanged; rho is an input only (13.1)

### 13.7 CLI
- `model-routing delegate TASK --parent MODEL --context-tokens N --brief-tokens N --result-tokens N [--carry-turns N] [--work-calls N] [--fold-inline] [--cached-read-ratio X] [--flavor cost_effective|cost_blind] [--generator MODEL] [--exclude MODEL ...] [--registry PATH] [--tasks PATH] [--stats PATH] [--priors PATH] [--json]`, with the defaults of section 10; `--fold-inline` passes `inline_separate_call=False`; `--work-calls` (v1.38, default 1) passes `work_calls`; `--cached-read-ratio` (v1.41, default 1.0) passes `cached_read_ratio` as a float
- Prints exactly eight lines, one per key in this order, the key, a colon, spaces, then the value: `mode`, `model`, `reason`, `below_bar` (`true` or `false`), `inline_cost_usd` and `delegate_cost_usd` (6 decimals), `parent_quality_lb` and `delegate_quality_lb` (3 decimals); a `None` value prints as `-`. `--json` prints `DelegationDecision.to_dict()`
- Exit codes: 0 success; 2 bad input (as for `select`, plus a `--parent` that is not in the registry, a token option that is not an integer `>= 0`, from v1.38, a `--work-calls` that is not an integer `>= 1` and, from v1.41, a `--cached-read-ratio` that is not a number in [0, 1], `nan` and `inf` included), including a missing required option, with exactly one `error: ...` line on stderr and it is the last line (an argument-parsing error prints the usage first, as for `select`); 3 `NoModelClearsBar`, with the candidate table on stderr as for `select`
- v1.15: after the eight lines, the `--json` output or the exit-3 table, the price expiry warnings of section 10

### 13.8 Limits and test status
- From v1.38 the work may be K calls on either path (13.1, 13.2). Not modelled (14): prompt caching beyond the v1.41 ratio on the parent's reads of C (13.2), a parent or sub-agent that summarises between calls, whether the K calls succeed together (the bar is per call), and the wall-clock gain of running sibling sub-agents at the same time. The committed evidence (13.10, v1.73) has two multi-call points, variant points 0 and 2 (two and 15 folded calls at 64,000 context tokens), beside the one cached point, variant point 1
- The contract tests (`tests/test_delegation.py`) were committed before `delegation.py` exists, under a module-level `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=ModuleNotFoundError)`, as in 12.5. The implementing commit removes that marker
- v1.9: the README shows two `delegate` transcripts on the demo's output (11), one where delegating is cheaper and one where inline is, checked like every README transcript (12.5)
- v1.12: the demo asks the first transcript's question in both flavors and runs the delegated hand-off through `Executor.call_delegated` (11), so its `calls.jsonl` holds records tagged `delegated` and `parent`
- v1.31: the row 7 equal-bound tests that need the new branch are committed ahead of the code under `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=AssertionError)`, next to passing tests of their fixtures and of the cases that stay inline; the implementing commit removes the markers and regenerates `evidence/delegation/results.json`, which the change moves (13.10). v1.32 is that commit
- v1.38: the multi-call tests (`tests/test_work_calls.py`, a file of their own as v1.33 had) are committed ahead of the code, each under a `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)` that names what the v1.37 code raises: `TypeError` where a test passes `work_calls` to `decide_delegation`, whose v1.37 signature has no such keyword, and `AssertionError` where a CLI test passes `--work-calls`, on which v1.37 exits 2 as an unknown option. Next to them, plain tests pin that the v1.38 expressions with K = 1 give the v1.37 costs bit for bit on the existing fixtures and that the closed-form token totals are the sums over the calls. - v1.206, where delegation pays: `research/where-delegation-pays.md` and its script `research/where_delegation_pays.py` (standard library only; `python research/where_delegation_pays.py [--root PATH]`, PATH the repository root, by default the script's checkout) ask whether the per-task 13.5 decision beats a fixed policy, always inline or always delegate, on the committed delegation evidence (row R17). It reads only the `summary` of the committed delegation files, in this order: `evidence/delegation/results.json` (13.10), `evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/results.json` and `evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/pooled.json` (13.12), `evidence/llmrouterbench-delegation/results.json` (13.13) and (v1.323, row R28) `evidence/response-bank-delegation/results.json` (13.14), five files of four datasets (the first four to v1.322). A row is one (bar, flavor, point, parent) with its three strategies (13.9). The decision's (`success`, `cost_usd`) pair is compared exactly, as floats, with always inline's and always delegate's, and the row takes the first class that applies: `= delegate` or `= inline` (equal to that policy's pair in both), `better` (a success at least both and a cost at most both), `beaten on success` (a fixed policy has a higher success at no more cost), `beaten on cost` (one has the same success at a lower cost), else `trade-off`. A row is `= delegate` exactly when its decision `delegate_rate` is 1.0 and `= inline` exactly when it is 0.0, which the script checks. A changed row is a row of the last four classes; its gain is the decision's success minus the higher of the two fixed policies' successes. Output: a title line; per file a line with its name, path, rows, tasks, bars, the number of points and of parents, a header, and one line per flavor and bar in the file's config order with the rows of each class and `top success`, the changed rows with a gain above 0; then per flavor the changed rows by point index, a line against always delegate (the changed rows, those that cost less than it, of those the ones at no less success, and the rest) and the changed row with the largest gain, the first in file order on a tie, with the three pairs and the gain, or `no changed row` on both lines; last, `All five files` (`All four files` to v1.322), the header and one line per flavor over the five files, bar `all`. Successes and costs to 4 decimals, the gain signed. Exit 2 with one `error: ` line naming the file when a file is missing, is not JSON or not a JSON object, lacks a key the report reads, a row lacks a strategy or repeats one, a success or a cost is not a number (an `int` or a `float` that is not a bool), or the rate check fails. (v1.208) The rate check is a consistency check on the committed files, not a property of 13.9: a file whose two fixed policies share one pair, or whose mixed decision ties a policy exactly, would fail it. Counts of rows, tasks, points, parents and changed rows print as `1 row` and `2 rows`. The note states the rule before any count, gives the counts, the rows the per-task choice left unchanged and the largest gain where it changed something, and recommends a README Limitations line, a spec row or nothing; a README line it recommends is a later backlog row, not an edit of this version. No library code, harness or results file changes. `tests/test_research_where_delegation_pays.py` is committed ahead of the script and the note, every test a strict xfail but the one that reads this bullet

(v1.323, row R28) From v1.323 the bullet names the five files of four datasets and takes its counts from the `All five files` lines, and since two `cost_effective` rows of the response bank are `better`, its no-`better` clause becomes the count of `better` rows, each checked on the committed files to be a `cost_effective` row of the response bank at always delegating's held-out success and a cost below both fixed policies', with no `cost_blind` row `better`; it reads, figures in braces: "... and the decision is better than both fixed policies on {better} rows, each a `cost_effective` row of the response bank at always delegating's held-out success for less cost than either. So the evidence shows how the decision prices the two paths at the stated sizes, not a gain from choosing per task; ...". `tests/test_research_where_delegation_pays.py` builds the bullet from the script's output and checks the separate-call claim on the committed files, so a regenerated results file that moves a count, or a separate-call row that is no longer `= delegate`, fails it. The README test is committed ahead of the line as a strict xfail. No library code, script, note or results file changes

- v1.227, per-item delegation paths: `research/per-item-delegation.md` and its script `research/per_item_delegation.py` (standard library and the package; `python research/per_item_delegation.py --matrix MATRIX [--root PATH]`, MATRIX the LLMRouterBench converter's default output (12.18), PATH the repository root, by default the script's checkout) ask how often a held-out item's cheaper path differs from the per-task decision's mode and what taking it would give (row R18; LLMRouterBench (13.13) first, the 13.10 and 13.12 matrices a later row). The script runs 13.9 as `evidence/llmrouterbench-delegation/results.py` does (its `select_rows`, `registry.json`, `BARS`, `SEEDS` and `points()`, loaded from PATH) and fails unless the run's `summary` equals the committed `results.json` one. For each cell it rebuilds, per held-out item, the list-price cost 13.9 charges always inline (the parent at its own sizes on the item) and always delegating (the cell's `delegate` at its own sizes), by the 13.9 expressions for a single-call row and models without tiers, and fails unless the `math.fsum` of each over the held-out half equals the cell's `cost_usd` of that path and the parent's and the delegate's correct counts equal its `correct`. The rule, stated before any number: an item's cheaper path is the one whose rebuilt cost is lower, the cell's mode on an exact tie; an item is switched when its cheaper path is not the mode. Two strategies take each switched item's cheaper path: `pick` on every cell, and `size rule` only on the cells whose decision compared the two paths' costs, 13.5 row 3 with reason `delegate_cheaper` or `inline_cheaper`, every other cell keeping its mode (its choice comes from 13.4 or the bounds, not cost, so a rule that only sees each item's sizes leaves it, and in `cost_blind` it touches no cell). Each item's success is the record of the path it takes, so on cost `pick` is a floor for any rule that chooses between the two paths item by item. A strategy's row (bar, flavor, point, parent) aggregates its cells as the 13.9 summary does; the decision's row must equal the summary's. Output: a title line; a `matrix:` line with the rows, items, tasks and models, the cells, the path pairs rebuilt (one per task, seed, point, parent and delegate); a header; per flavor and bar in config order and per strategy one line with the cells it may change, the switched items over the held-out items of those cells and their share, the strategy's summed row cost over the decision's, the mean over rows of its success minus the decision's (signed), and `better`, the rows where its success is at least the decision's at a lower cost; per flavor the `pick` switched share by point; per flavor and strategy the row with the largest cost cut at no less success, the first in run order on a tie, or `no row`. Shares, costs and successes to 4 decimals, `-` for a share of no items. Exit 2 with one `error: ` line on a missing or unreadable file, a row that `select_rows` or 13.9 refuses, a summary that is not the committed one, a rebuilt cost or count that is not its cell's, a model with price tiers or a row with per-call sizes. The note states the rule before any number, gives the numbers, and recommends a README line, a spec row for a per-item size rule, or nothing; a README line it recommends is a later backlog row. No library code, harness or results file changes. `tests/test_research_per_item_delegation.py` is committed ahead of the script and the note, every test a strict xfail but the one that reads this bullet; a session fixture in `tests/conftest.py` runs the 13.13 evaluation once when the pinned file is cached, for this test and `tests/test_evidence_llmrouterbench_delegation.py`

(v1.330, review iter-216 must-fix 1) The count reads each dataset's unrounded cut, one minus the smallest `size rule` `cost / decision` of its blocks as printed to 4 decimals, never the two-significant-figure percent the bullet shows, which shows a cut of 1.99% (a ratio of 0.9801) as 2%; a test moves the response bank's ratio to 0.9801 and to 0.9800 and requires the first bullet to say `on that evidence` and the second `on three of the four datasets`, both showing 2%
- v1.331, a per-item rule on the input size alone, on the response bank (row R31): `research/per_item_delegation.py` gains a third strategy, `input rule`, which its report gives with `--dataset response-bank` only, the one dataset whose `size rule` cuts a bar's summed cost by more than 2% (v1.328); on the cells where both may switch, every item at its cheaper path costs no more than any rule that chooses per item, so on the other four `input rule` could cut no more than their `size rule` cuts. It changes only the cells `size rule` may change (13.5 row 3, reason `delegate_cheaper` or `inline_cheaper`), every other cell keeping its mode, and of each item it sees only what is known before the call: x, the parent's `input_tokens` on the item. On a cell it takes inline on the held-out items whose x is under the cell's threshold and delegates the rest. The threshold is set on the cell's calibration half alone (the first half `split_items` returns for the cell's seed): the candidates are 0 (every item delegated), each distinct x of a calibration item above the smallest (inline under it), and no bound (every item inline); it is the candidate whose calibration items' summed cost, each item at the path the candidate gives it by the v1.227 rebuilt costs, is lowest, compared exactly (each cost as an integer numerator over one power of two), and on a tie the one that switches the fewest calibration items from the cell's mode, a count that is monotone in the threshold, so the choice is unique. No held-out item is read in setting it. An item is switched when the path the rule gives it is not the cell's mode, its success is the record of that path, and the strategy's row aggregates its cells as v1.227 does. With the input rule on, a matrix with a row with per-call sizes is refused (`the input rule reads single-call rows only`), since x is then not one call's input. Output: the v1.227 lines, unchanged, then, with `--dataset response-bank` only, per flavor in config order on which `size rule` may change at least one cell: a line `  input rule, <flavor>: on the cells size rule may change, inline when the parent's input tokens on the item are under a threshold set on the cell's calibration half`; a header `  bar  point  strategy` and the v1.227 columns; per bar in config order and per point in config order and then `all` (every point of the bar), one `size rule` and one `input rule` line with the v1.227 columns over that bar's rows at that point (the `all` `size rule` line has the columns of the v1.227 bar line); and the v1.227 largest-cut line for `input rule`. No new line starts with `  cost_` or `  flavor `, so the v1.236 README test and the 12.8 v1.260 table check read the v1.227 table alone, and the other four datasets' output is unchanged byte for byte. The note's response-bank block is the new output, the four earlier blocks unchanged; its Facts, Reading it and Recommendation give what the input rule keeps of the `size rule` cut, the Recommendation's first bullet the input rule's largest cut of a bar's summed cost (one minus the smallest `all` `input rule` `cost / decision`, to two significant figures as v1.236 rounds it), and whether a spec row for a per-item rule follows. The tests that read the new output or note text are committed ahead of them as strict xfails, the one that reads this bullet not: the threshold against a brute force over every candidate on exact integers, with ties, an all-delegate and an all-inline cell, and held-out costs that, moved, leave it unchanged; on a hand matrix whose input sizes vary, each row's `input rule` success, cost, switched items, items and cells against a brute force on integer cost numerators; the report's new lines; the per-call refusal; and the hand response-bank root run end to end through `main`. No library code, README, harness, results file or RESULTS.md changes
- v1.253, which 13.5 row chose (row R19): `research/delegation_reasons.py` (standard library and the package; `python research/delegation_reasons.py --matrix MATRIX [--dataset llmrouterbench|autobench|tau2-bench|tau2-bench-pooled] [--registry POOL] [--root PATH]`, the arguments of `research/per_item_delegation.py`, v1.227 to v1.233, whose rerun it shares: the same 13.9 run on the same matrix, checked against the same committed summary) counts the run's cells per (flavor, point) by the 13.5 row and `reason` that chose their path, and states, before any count, a condition from 13.2 under which a separate inline call costs more than delegating. The condition. With inline work in a call of its own, inline minus delegating to the parent's own model is, in exact arithmetic on a model without tiers, `p_in * K * (rho * C - B) - p_out * B + (1 + T) * p_in * (K * (S_in + S_out) - B - R)`, with p_in and p_out the parent's list prices per token and S_in and S_out its 5.2 sizes (the 13.2 difference for D = P, in its v1.38 and v1.41 forms). Where the parent is a candidate that passes the delegate's `select`, the `cost_effective` primary costs no more on its work than the parent's own model would (13.3), and where no candidate passes, the 5.3 order puts a measured primary at a bound at least the parent's and, at an equal bound, a work cost at most its own; so a separate inline call costs more than delegating whenever that difference is above 0, and since the sizes are at least 0 it is at every size when `slack * p_in > B * p_out`, `slack = K * (rho * C - B) - (1 + T) * (B + R)` tokens: for B > 0, a parent whose output price is under slack / B times its input price. A positive slack makes B < C, so a parent that clears 13.4 also passes 5.2 rule 5 in that `select` and 13.5 row 5 cannot occur (its bullet above); rows 1 and 2 delegate; so in `cost_effective`, at a point and parent that meet the condition, on a run that excludes no model and measures every model (13.9), every cell delegates: row 3 with `delegate_cheaper`, row 7 with `none_clear`. At the four 13.10 base points (B = R = 500, T = 1, K = 1, rho = 1) the slack of the three separate points is C - 2,500, 5,500, 61,500 and 197,500 tokens, ratios 11, 123 and 395; the folded point 3 is outside the condition, its inline cost reading no C. Output, for each dataset run: a title line naming the dataset; a `run:` line with the cells and the config; a `condition` line with the inequality; per point its C, B, R, T, `separate` or `folded`, K and rho, and for a separate point its slack, slack / B, the number of the run's parents that meet the condition and the highest output-to-input price ratio among them with its model (the first ascending on a tie), or `does not apply (folded)`; a `cells by 13.5 row and reason` line; per (flavor, point), flavors and points in config order, the cell count and each (row, reason, mode) that chose at least one cell with its count, ordered by row, then by the reason order of the 13.6 constants, then `delegate` before `inline`; and a check line, the `cost_effective` cells at a separate point whose parent meets the condition and how many of them delegate. A reason maps to its row: `parent_excluded_family`, `parent_not_independent` and `parent_context_full` row 1, `parent_below_bar` row 2, `delegate_cheaper` and `inline_cheaper` row 3, `delegate_better` and `inline_as_good` row 3 in `cost_effective` and row 4 in `cost_blind`, `only_parent_clears` row 5, `none_clear` row 7; row 6 raises and the 13.9 task never sets it. Exit 2 with one `error: ` line on what `research/per_item_delegation.py` refuses, a tiered model, or an unknown reason. `research/where-delegation-pays.md` gains a section `## Which 13.5 row chose`, after `## Reading it`, with the condition stated before its four results blocks (LLMRouterBench, AutoBench 2026, tau2-bench per domain and pooled, each the script's output on that dataset's matrix, compared byte for byte where the pinned files are cached), a reading, and a recommended README sentence or none; its Regenerate section gains the commands. The tests (`tests/test_research_delegation_reasons.py`) run the script on a hand-built matrix on which each of 13.5 rows 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 and 7 chooses at least once, check the condition at hand-worked points, at its strict boundary, and on every cell of that run, and are committed ahead of the script and the note, each a strict xfail but the one that reads this bullet. No library code change: `research/per_item_delegation.py` gains a `rerun` that returns the rows, the registry and the run that its `run` reports on, with its output unchanged
