### 13.9 Offline delegation evidence (`delegation_eval.py`, v1.19)
Purpose: show from a public outcome matrix, with no API key, what the 13.5 decision does on items it never saw, next to always working inline and always delegating. No public dataset records inline-versus-sub-agent outcomes, so the method combines what a matrix does measure, each model's acceptance and token counts on each item, with a stated grid for what it does not, the parent's context and the hand-off sizes. The decision comes from `Router.decide_delegation`, so the evidence exercises the shipped rules of 13.2 to 13.5 and not a re-implementation of them. The task it builds excludes no family and names no generator, so of the 13.4 blocks only `parent_context_full` can apply here
- `evaluate_delegation(rows, registry, bars, points, seeds=range(10), flavors=("cost_effective", "cost_blind"), parents=None, calibration_fraction=0.5, min_samples=30, confidence=0.95) -> dict`
- `rows`: dicts with `task`, `item`, `model` and `correct` as in 12.1, and `input_tokens` and `output_tokens`, integers `>= 0` that are not bools, required even when `cost_usd` is present. `cost_usd` and other extra keys are ignored (from v1.74 all but `call_input_tokens` and `call_output_tokens`, the per-call sizes below): every cost here is a list price from `registry.get(model).cost(...)` (4.1; from v1.212 `.base_cost(...)`, 13.2; from v1.222, on a model with `price_tiers`, each call at its own tier, v1.222 below), because the delegate path (13.2) charges one item's tokens to two models, which a billed per-call total cannot be split into
- `registry`: holds every model in the rows; its prices, families and context windows are the ones 13.2 to 13.4 use
- `points`: the grid of assumed sizes, a non-empty sequence of dicts, each with exactly the keys `context_tokens` (C), `brief_tokens` (B), `result_tokens` (R) and `carry_turns` (T), each an integer `>= 0` that is not a bool, and `inline_separate_call`, a bool (13.1), and from v1.43 optionally `work_calls` (K, 13.1), an integer `>= 1` that is not a bool. A point without `work_calls` means K = 1; any other key is still an extra key. A point's values are checked in that key order, so `work_calls` after `inline_separate_call`, and an invalid `work_calls` is a `ValueError` whose message starts `'work_calls' must be an integer >= 1`. Points are compared with a missing `work_calls` read as 1, so a point with `work_calls` 1 and the same point without the key are a repeated point. v1.57: a point may also carry `cached_read_ratio` (rho, 13.1), an `int` or a `float` that is not a bool, with 0 <= rho <= 1 (so NaN and the infinities fail), checked after `work_calls`; an invalid one is a `ValueError` whose message starts `'cached_read_ratio' must be a number from 0 to 1`. A point without it means rho = 1.0. The message for a missing or an extra key is `a point needs exactly the keys [...], and optionally 'work_calls' and 'cached_read_ratio': ` followed by the point, the list being the five keys. Points are compared with a missing `cached_read_ratio` read as 1.0 as well, so a point with `cached_read_ratio` 1.0 or 1 and the same point without the key are a repeated point
- `parents`: the model IDs tried as the parent, in the order given; `None` means every model in the rows, ascending
- Checked before any work, each a `ValueError`: no rows; a row that is not a dict, or whose `task`, `item`, `model` or `correct` is invalid as in 12.1, or whose `input_tokens` or `output_tokens` is missing or invalid; a repeated (task, item, model); a model not in `registry`; an incomplete matrix (12.3); bars, seeds and flavors as in 12.3; no points, a point that is not a dict, has a missing or an extra key or an invalid value, or a repeated point; an empty `parents`, a parent that is not a model of the rows, or a repeated parent; `min_samples`, `confidence` and `calibration_fraction` as in 12.3; an empty calibration or held-out split (12.3); from v1.74 the per-call row, task and point checks below
- One **cell** per (bar, flavor, point, parent, seed, task), nested in that order: bars, flavors, points and parents in the order given, seeds in the order given, tasks ascending. C and H are the task's calibration and held-out items from `split_items` (12.2), so a seed splits a task exactly as `evaluate` does
- The decision uses the calibration half only:
  - stats: for each model, successes = its correct rows in C and trials = |C|, and (v1.35) its token totals over those |C| calls, `Stats.add(task, model, successes, |C|, input_tokens=<the sum of its input_tokens over C>, output_tokens=<the sum of its output_tokens over C>)` with the default `token_trials` (4.3). So `decide_delegation` prices the parent, every candidate of the delegate's `select` and the delegate each at that model's own rounded-up mean calibration sizes (13.2, 13.3). Before v1.35 the stats held counts alone and every model was priced at S_in and S_out
  - S_in and S_out: `statistics.median_low` of `input_tokens`, and of `output_tokens`, over the task's calibration rows of every model, so an integer. They are the task's expected sizes, and since every model of the matrix has token totals (v1.35) they set no price: they are read only where 13.3 and 13.4 read the task's expected tokens whatever a model's measured sizes, the 13.4 rule 3 inline check `C + S_in + S_out` and the 5.2 rule 5 check of the delegate's `select`, `S_in + B + S_out`; for a point with K work calls (v1.43) they are `C + K * (S_in + S_out)` and the delegate's last call, `B + K * (S_in + S_out)` (13.3, 13.4)
  - the decision is `Router(registry, {task: t}, stats).decide_delegation(task, parent, flavor, **point)` with `t = Task(name=task, bar=bar, min_samples=min_samples, confidence=confidence, independent_of_generator=False, expected_input_tokens=S_in, expected_output_tokens=S_out, on_no_model="best_available", max_fallbacks=0)`, so a point's `work_calls` (v1.43) reaches `decide_delegation` as its `work_calls`, and a point without it leaves the default 1; likewise a point's `cached_read_ratio` (v1.57) reaches it as its `cached_read_ratio`, and a point without it leaves the default 1.0
  - v1.219: on a registry with `price_tiers` the decision's two costs price each tiered model's calls at their own tiers (13.2 v1.219), while every held-out cost below stayed at the base prices (`Model.base_cost`) until v1.222 (below); no committed delegation registry has a tier, so no committed result moved
  - a cell whose decision has no route, because no model passes 5.2 rule 5 for S_in + B + S_out (for K work calls, for the delegate's last call, B + K * (S_in + S_out), v1.43), is a `ValueError` whose message names the task, the parent and the point as `point <index>`. That is also the only case in which this `decide_delegation` raises `NoModelClearsBar`, which becomes the same `ValueError`
- Strategies, scored on H, in this order: `decision` (inline on the parent when `mode` is `inline`, else delegated to the decision's `model`), `inline` (inline on the parent for every item, even where 13.4 blocks it; the cell's `inline_allowed` says whether it could), `delegate` (delegated to `route.primary` for every item)
- Held-out cost of one item i, with in(i, m) and out(i, m) the tokens of model m's row for i, P the parent, D the model that does delegated work, and C, B, R and T from the point; each expression is evaluated left to right in floating point, as in 13.2:
  - inline with `inline_separate_call` True: `P.cost(C + in(i, P), out(i, P)) + (1 + T) * P.cost(in(i, P) + out(i, P), 0)`
  - inline with `inline_separate_call` False: `P.cost(in(i, P), out(i, P)) + T * P.cost(in(i, P) + out(i, P), 0)`
  - delegated: `P.cost(0, B) + D.cost(B + in(i, D), out(i, D)) + (1 + T) * P.cost(B + R, 0)`
  - v1.43, a point with K work calls (13.1): the cost follows the 13.2 K-call expressions with the item's recorded tokens as the sizes of each of the K work calls, S_in = in(i, m) and S_out = out(i, m) for the model m doing the work on that path, and w_in = K(K + 1) / 2 and w_out = K(K - 1) / 2:
    - inline with `inline_separate_call` True: `P.cost(K * C + w_in * in(i, P) + w_out * out(i, P), K * out(i, P)) + (1 + T) * P.cost(K * (in(i, P) + out(i, P)), 0)`
    - inline with `inline_separate_call` False: `P.cost((K - 1) * C + w_in * in(i, P) + w_out * out(i, P), K * out(i, P)) + T * P.cost(K * (in(i, P) + out(i, P)), 0)`
    - delegated: `P.cost(0, B) + D.cost(K * B + w_in * in(i, D) + w_out * out(i, D), K * out(i, D)) + (1 + T) * P.cost(B + R, 0)`
    - every token total is an integer, so with K = 1 (w_in = 1, w_out = 0) each first and second argument of `P.cost` and `D.cost` is the integer of the K = 1 expression above and every held-out cost is the v1.42 one bit for bit; a point without `work_calls` is that case
    - the item's success on each path is still its one recorded answer, so `correct` counts each held-out item once whatever K: whether the K calls succeed together is not measured, and each of the K calls is assumed to read and write what the item's one recorded call did
  - v1.57, a point with `cached_read_ratio` rho (13.1): the inline cost prices the parent's reads of C in its work calls at rho times its list input price, as 13.2 v1.41 does, and nothing else changes:
    - inline with `inline_separate_call` True: `P.cost(K * C * rho + w_in * in(i, P) + w_out * out(i, P), K * out(i, P)) + (1 + T) * P.cost(K * (in(i, P) + out(i, P)), 0)`
    - inline with `inline_separate_call` False: `P.cost((K - 1) * C * rho + w_in * in(i, P) + w_out * out(i, P), K * out(i, P)) + T * P.cost(K * (in(i, P) + out(i, P)), 0)`
    - delegated: the v1.43 expression whatever rho, and the delegate (`route.primary`) is that of the same point without the key, since rho never reaches the delegate's `select` (13.1); so the `inline` and `delegate` strategies' `correct` and the `delegate` strategy's cost are those of that point too. Only the inline cost moves, and through the decision's own rho-priced `inline_cost_usd` its `mode` and what follows from it: the cell's `model` and `reason`, the `decision` strategy's `correct`, `success` and `cost_usd`, and the summary's `delegate_rate`
    - the first argument is evaluated left to right: the integer `K * C` or `(K - 1) * C`, times rho, then the integer terms added. A point without the key keeps the v1.43 integer total, so its held-out costs are the v1.56 ones bit for bit; with `cached_read_ratio` 1.0 the argument is a float equal to that integer and `Model.cost` (from v1.212 `Model.base_cost`) converts the integer to the same float before it multiplies by a price, so the costs are again the v1.56 ones bit for bit (while the totals stay below 2^53)
  - so the decision is made on calibration-half sizes, each model's own mean since v1.35 (the pooled medians before), and is scored on each held-out item's own recorded sizes for the model that did the work, as before: no held-out cost uses a mean or a median
- v1.74, per-call sizes: a multi-call item, such as one tau2-bench conversation (12.14, [`research/delegation-datasets.md`](research/delegation-datasets.md)), can be priced on its own calls only if its row keeps each call's position, so a row may carry two optional keys, both or neither:
  - `call_input_tokens` (a_1 to a_K), a non-empty list of integers `>= 0` that are not bools: a_1 is the first call's whole read, the work's own base context (on tau2-bench the system prompt with the domain policy and the tool schemas, the greeting and the user's first message), and a_j for j > 1 is call j's new input, what it reads beyond call j - 1's read and output. `call_output_tokens` (b_1 to b_K), a list of the same kind and length: each call's output. K = K(i, m) is the number of work calls of model m on item i, so two models may take different numbers of calls on one item
  - call j reads a_1 to a_j and b_1 to b_{j-1}, so the K calls read Rd = the sum over j of (K - j + 1) * a_j + (K - j) * b_j in all, write O = b_1 + ... + b_K, and leave A + O in the context, with A = a_1 + ... + a_K. The row's `input_tokens` must equal Rd and its `output_tokens` must equal O, so a per-call row's two counts are what its calls read and write, the summed counts the 12.14 `--tokens` matrix records for a model whose every new input is at least 0, and `evaluate` (12.3) on the same matrix prices each item at the list cost of its calls. Means per call never price a held-out item: with K = 2, inputs 1 and 9 and outputs 2 and 2, the two calls read 1 and 12, Rd = 13, while means of 5 and 2 would give 3 * 5 + 1 * 2 = 17 (review iter-072 must-fix 1)
  - a_1 is the work's input, which 13.2 says is fetched free and read on both paths: inline the parent reads it after C, delegated the delegate reads it after B. So B stays what the parent writes to hand the work off (13.1), never a copy of the policy; a brief that also counted a_1 would charge it twice. This replaces the research note's "the brief carries the domain policy and the tool schemas, so it is at least the first call's read": the policy reaches the delegate as the work's own input
  - checked before any work, each a `ValueError` whose message starts as quoted and ends with the row, in the same pass as the row's 12.1 and token checks, row by row: a row's per-call checks run right after its token checks and before the next row is read, so a bad per-call row is reported before a later row's 12.1 error. They are: one key without the other (`'call_input_tokens' and 'call_output_tokens' go together`); a key that is not a non-empty list of integers `>= 0` that are not bools, a float or a bool among them included (`'call_input_tokens' must be a non-empty list of integers >= 0`, and likewise for `'call_output_tokens'`); lists of unequal length (`'call_input_tokens' and 'call_output_tokens' must have the same length`); an `input_tokens` other than Rd or an `output_tokens` other than O (`'input_tokens' must be what the row's calls read`, followed by Rd, then `, and 'output_tokens' what they write, ` followed by O). After the incomplete-matrix check, for tasks ascending, a task with rows of both kinds (`task <name>: either every row or no row carries per-call sizes`, the name as a quoted repr), since the decision's sizes pool the task's rows. After the point checks, for points in order, a point whose `work_calls` (read as 1 when missing) is above 1 while any task has per-call sizes (`point <index> sets 'work_calls' above 1 and task <name> has per-call sizes`, the first such task ascending): a row's lists are already its work calls, and a point's K would repeat each of them K times. A point with `work_calls` 1 is the point without it (points above) and is accepted. A task whose rows have no per-call sizes runs as before beside one whose rows have them
  - held-out cost of item i on a task with per-call sizes, with K(i, m), Rd(i, m), A(i, m) and O(i, m) of model m's row for i, P the parent, D the model that does delegated work, and C, B, R and T from the point, each expression evaluated left to right in floating point:
    - inline with `inline_separate_call` True: `P.cost(K(i, P) * C + Rd(i, P), O(i, P)) + (1 + T) * P.cost(A(i, P) + O(i, P), 0)`
    - inline with `inline_separate_call` False: `P.cost((K(i, P) - 1) * C + Rd(i, P), O(i, P)) + T * P.cost(A(i, P) + O(i, P), 0)`
    - delegated: `P.cost(0, B) + D.cost(K(i, D) * B + Rd(i, D), O(i, D)) + (1 + T) * P.cost(B + R, 0)`
    - with `cached_read_ratio` rho, the integer `K(i, P) * C` or `(K(i, P) - 1) * C` is multiplied by rho and Rd(i, P) is added, as v1.57 does; nothing else moves
    - each path counts the calls of the model doing the work on it, so inline and delegated may count a different K for one item. A row of K equal calls, a_j = s and b_j = u for every j, gives Rd = w_in * s + w_out * u, A = K * s and O = K * u, the integers of the v1.43 expressions, so it prices as the row (s, u) without the keys at a point with `work_calls` K, bit for bit, and the item's success is still its one recorded answer. With `cached_read_ratio` (v1.75) that holds while `K * C * rho` (or `(K - 1) * C * rho`) is a whole number, as at rho 0.5 on C = 100,000; otherwise the float may differ in its last bit, since this adds the one integer Rd to it and v1.57 adds w_in * s and then w_out * u
  - the decision on a task with per-call sizes forecasts an unseen item from the calibration half, so it reads means there, as v1.35 does, an approximation that no held-out cost uses:
    - K_dec = `statistics.median_low` of K(i, m) over the task's calibration rows of every model. It reaches `decide_delegation` as `work_calls`, in place of the point's 1, so one K prices both paths in the decision even where the parent and a delegate take different numbers of calls
    - stats: `Stats.add(task, m, successes, |C|, input_tokens=|C| * ceil(A_C / K_C), output_tokens=|C| * ceil(O_C / K_C))`, with A_C, O_C and K_C the sums of A(i, m), O(i, m) and K(i, m) over C and each ceiling in integer arithmetic, so `mean_tokens` is the model's rounded-up mean new input and output per call (4.3 keeps `token_trials` at most `trials`, so the per-call mean is set through the totals)
    - S_in and S_out = `median_low` over the task's calibration rows of every model of ceil(A / K) and of ceil(O / K). The two window checks then read the forecast last call, `C + K_dec * (S_in + S_out)` (13.4 rule 3, in its v1.38 K-call form) and `B + K_dec * (S_in + S_out)` (5.2 rule 5): one call's read and write, never a summed count. At a row's own K and sizes, K * (ceil(A / K) + ceil(O / K)) is at least A + O, its last call's read and write, so the check never under-states that row's last call; at the medians it is a forecast. (At C = 64,000 the summed counts of the 12.14 `--tokens` matrix put telecom's check at 210,536 to 230,765 tokens though no conversation's last call read more than 33,765)
    - a cell of such a task carries `expected_work_calls`, K_dec; cells of other tasks keep the v1.73 keys
  - reasoning that a later call does not re-read: each b_j is charged as written and as re-read by the later work calls and the 1 + T later parent calls, and the harness cannot tell reasoning apart. A converter writes per-call rows only for a model whose every new input is at least 0, that is whose every later read is at least the previous read plus its output, which the row check enforces since no list entry may be negative; any other model is left out of the matrix with all its rows (12.3 needs every model on every item). On tau2-bench that leaves out `openai/o4-mini-2025-04-16`, whose new input is negative on 2,962 of its 3,849 steps
  - cached re-reads of the work's own history are at list price on both paths: a stated over-statement for an agent whose provider cached them (on tau2-bench list price is 1.79 to 2.35 times the recorded costs of the three OpenAI agents); rho still prices only the parent's reads of C
  - a row without the two keys prices as v1.73 bit for bit, and a matrix with no such row gives the v1.73 result bit for bit
- v1.222 per-call tiers in the held-out costs (row P1c): on a model with `price_tiers` (4.1) each term of a held-out cost above that this model's calls pay is priced call by call, as 13.2 v1.219 prices the decision's terms, with the item's recorded sizes in place of the 5.2 sizes, where v1.212 to v1.221 took `Model.base_cost` of a total. A term paid by a model without tiers keeps its v1.221 expression, so on a registry without tiers every held-out cost, cell and summary keeps its v1.221 bytes, and so do the four committed delegation results files (no committed delegation registry has a tier). Write in(n) and out(n) for a model's prices in a call that reads n input tokens (13.2 v1.219); C, B, R and T are the point's, rho its `cached_read_ratio` (1.0 on a point without it), P the parent and m the model doing the work on a path:
  - work calls on a row without per-call sizes: the point's K calls, call j reading X + j * in(i, m) + (j - 1) * out(i, m), X the path's base context (C inline, B delegated), and writing out(i, m). On a tiered m call j pays in(its read) on its charged input, rho * C + j * in(i, m) + (j - 1) * out(i, m) inline and its whole read delegated, and out(its read) on out(i, m); the work term is the `math.fsum` of the calls' costs, j ascending, which is the 13.2 v1.219 work term at S_in = in(i, m) and S_out = out(i, m)
  - work calls on a row with per-call sizes (v1.74): the row's own K(i, m) calls, call j reading X + a_1 + ... + a_j + b_1 + ... + b_{j-1} and writing b_j, charged likewise (inline, its read with C replaced by rho * C), so each call pays the tier of its own read and no call is priced at a mean; the work term is their `math.fsum`, j ascending
  - shared parent calls on a tiered P, as 13.2 v1.219: a call to which a path adds A input tokens is charged the 13.2 v1.219 input term, A * in(C + A) plus rho * C * (in(C + A) - in(C)), plus at out(C + A) any output the path adds to it. The decision turn on the delegated path has A = 0 and writes B; each of the 1 + T calls after delegated work has A = B + R; each of the 1 + T calls after separate inline work, and each of the T calls after folded inline work, has A = K * (in(i, P) + out(i, P)), or A(i, P) + O(i, P) on a row with per-call sizes
  - folded inline work on a tiered P: the K-th work call is the parent's next call, a shared call with A = K * in(i, P) + (K - 1) * out(i, P) that writes out(i, P), or, on a row with per-call sizes, A = A(i, P) + O(i, P) - b_K that writes b_K, the row's last call; the folded work term is one `math.fsum` over the costs of the K - 1 work calls before it and then that call's charge, as in 13.2 v1.219
  - each path is then the sum of its terms, left to right: delegated, the brief + the delegate's work + (1 + T) * one call after it; separate inline, the work + (1 + T) * one call after it; folded, the folded work term + T * one call after it. A tiered delegate under a parent without tiers moves only the delegate's work term, and a tiered parent only the parent's terms
  - so the decision and its scoring price the same calls the same way: on a row without per-call sizes whose model has the same sizes on every item, which are then its 5.2 sizes in the decision, an item's held-out cost on a path equals the decision's `inline_cost_usd`, or its `delegate_cost_usd` with the delegate `route.primary`, bit for bit, tiered or not. On a tiered model a row of K equal calls, a_j = s and b_j = u, prices as the row (s, u) without the keys at a point with `work_calls` K, bit for bit at any rho, since both price call j from the same integer read and the same charged input (without tiers v1.74 keeps its caveat on a float rho)
  - nothing else moves: the decision (tiered from v1.219), the stats and sizes it reads, `correct`, `success` and every key. A tiered model changes only the `cost_usd` of the paths whose terms it pays, and through them the summary's `cost_usd`. Not charged, as in 13.2 v1.219: the parent's own output in a shared call, which neither the inputs nor a matrix row give a size (13.2 v1.242)
  - README Limitations: the long-prompt bullet names two costs still at the base prices, grok-4.7 over 200K input tokens and the parent's own output in a later call that a path's additions take over a threshold, and says that the held-out costs of the delegation evidence (13.9) price each call at its own tier too. From v1.242 (row P1d) it names one cost still at the base prices, grok-4.7 over 200K input tokens, and says in a sentence of its own that the parent's own output in a later call is charged on neither path, since no input gives it a size (13.2 v1.242), so where a path's additions take that call over a threshold the higher price of that output is left out
- For each strategy, `correct` = the number of items in H on which its model's row is correct, `success = correct / |H|`, and `cost_usd` = the `math.fsum` of its item costs over H
  - v1.132, computed once per path: a strategy's `correct` and `cost_usd` depend only on the cell's task, seed, point and parent, on whether its path works inline or delegated, and on the model doing the work (the parent inline, `route.primary` delegated), never on the bar or the flavor. So a run computes the pair for each (task, seed, point, parent, path, model) once, the first time a cell needs it, and every later cell with that key, at any bar or flavor, reads the same integer and the same float; `decision` reads the pair of the path it takes. Inline and delegated stay two keys when `route.primary` is the parent. Every cell, the summary and the result are the v1.131 ones bit for bit; only the number of held-out sums falls, from three per cell to one per distinct key
  - v1.244, each part once: of a v1.132 pair, `correct` is the sum of the model's correct flags over the held items, which depends only on the task, the seed and the model, and `cost_usd` is the `math.fsum` of one cost per held item, which depends only on the item and on the task, point, parent, path and model, never on the seed (on a per-call task the median call count enters the decision only, never an item's cost). So a run computes each (task, seed, model) count once and each item's cost once per (task, point, parent, path, model), the first time a v1.132 key needs it, and every later key reads the same integer or the same float; `math.fsum` is correctly rounded, so the same floats give the same sum in any order. Every cell, the summary and the result are the v1.243 ones bit for bit; the run keeps one float per item for each (task, point, parent, path, model) it meets, so it holds more memory while it runs and computes far fewer item costs, about one per distinct item and key instead of one per held item and seed
- Cell keys: `bar`, `flavor`, `point` (the index into `points`), `parent`, `seed`, `task`, `calibration_items`, `held_out_items`, `expected_input_tokens` (S_in), `expected_output_tokens` (S_out), (v1.74, only on a task with per-call sizes) `expected_work_calls`, `mode`, `model`, `reason`, `below_bar` and `inline_allowed` from the decision, `delegate` (`route.primary`), then `correct`, `success` and `cost_usd`, each a dict keyed by strategy in strategy order
- Return value keys: `config` (`bars`, `seeds`, `flavors` and `points` as lists in the order given, each point with its keys in the order above (`work_calls` and then (v1.57) `cached_read_ratio` last, each only on a point that has it, so a grid of points without them serialises as before v1.43), `parents` as the list used, `calibration_fraction`, `min_samples`, `confidence`); `tasks` (`{task: {"items": int, "models": [ids ascending]}}`); `cells` in cell order; `summary`, one row per (bar, flavor, point, parent, strategy) in that order, with keys `bar`, `flavor`, `point`, `parent`, `strategy`, `success` (the mean over seeds of that seed's total `correct` over its total held-out items across tasks), `cost_usd` (the mean over seeds of that seed's total held-out cost across tasks), `below_bar_rate` (the fraction of that group's (task, seed) cells whose held-out success is below the bar, strictly: a success equal to the bar is not below it) and `delegate_rate` (for `decision`, the fraction of those cells whose `mode` is `delegate`; `None` for the two baselines)
- Arithmetic and determinism as in 12.3: every success and rate is an integer count over an integer count, every cost total and the sum behind every mean uses `math.fsum`, the same rows in any order give an equal dict, and `json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True)` is byte-identical
- What is measured and what is assumed:
  - measured, from the matrix: whether each model's answer to each item was accepted, and the input and output tokens of that call
  - from the registry: list prices, families and context windows. On public data the prices are recorded list prices, so every cost is a list-price estimate, never a billed cost
  - assumed, the grid: C, B, R, T, `inline_separate_call`, (v1.43) K and (v1.57) rho. No public matrix records a parent's context or a hand-off, so results are stated per point, never pooled across points
  - assumed, quality: the delegate is credited with its recorded answer to the whole item, as if a brief of B tokens carried everything the item needs; the parent is credited with its recorded answer as if C more tokens of context did not change it. Neither a lossy brief nor long-context degradation is measured, and each would lower one path's success. So the results show what the decision rule does under these assumptions, not the quality of a real hand-off
  - the 13.8 limits apply: one call per path unless a point sets `work_calls` (v1.43), with the bar and the success per call, never for the K calls together; and every read at list price (rho = 1.0, no prompt caching) unless a point sets `cached_read_ratio` (v1.57), which prices only the parent's reads of C in its inline work calls at rho times its list input price, an assumed ratio since no public matrix records cached prices
  - (v1.59) one dataset: the committed results (13.10) read only the AutoBench matrix (12.9). [`research/delegation-datasets.md`](research/delegation-datasets.md) finds that the tau2-bench files of 12.14, which hold a success and per-call token counts for each of the 4 agents on each item, cannot feed this harness as it stands: each item is a conversation of 2 to 100 model calls, so in(i, m) and out(i, m) taken as the conversation's summed counts mis-price both paths by amounts that grow with its number of calls; a point's `work_calls` sets one K for every item; and list price leaves out the prompt-cache discount that the recorded costs of the three OpenAI agents imply on about three quarters of their prompt tokens. Pricing each item on its own work calls would have to come first; v1.74 specifies it (the per-call sizes above), implemented by v1.75, and v1.76 specifies the converter mode that writes such rows from the pinned files (12.14 `--calls`), implemented by v1.77; v1.78 specifies delegation results on those rows (13.12) and v1.79 commits them
  - (v1.187) LLMRouterBench as a third dataset: [`research/delegation-datasets.md`](research/delegation-datasets.md) `## LLMRouterBench (2026-11-09)` asks whether the 12.18 matrix, whose items are single calls with a recorded cost and both token counts on all but 10 rows, can feed this harness, which prices every row from a registry. Its script `research/llmrouterbench_prices.py` (standard library only; `python research/llmrouterbench_prices.py --matrix PATH`, PATH the converter's default rows) reads rows with the keys `task`, `item`, `model`, `cost_usd` (a finite number `>= 0`, not a bool), `input_tokens` and `output_tokens` (each an int `>= 0` that is not a bool, or null), and exits 2 with one `error:` line on a missing or unreadable file, a line that is not a JSON object, a missing key, an invalid value, a repeated (item, model) or an item that lacks a model of the rows. It drops every row of an item on which any row has a null count, as a delegation run must, since this harness needs both counts on every row of a complete matrix, and prints: the items, tasks and models, with the largest `input_tokens` of any row; the dropped items per task and the kept items and rows; a header line; then per model, ascending, on the kept items, the `math.fsum` of the recorded costs, the cost at its `LIST_PRICES` entry (the vendor page prices of the section's table, USD per 1M tokens) and the ratio recorded over list when it has one, and `fit(points)`: the least-squares input and output prices over its kept (input, output, cost) rows, each rounded to 4 decimals, called the model's exact pair when with them every kept row's cost is within `TOLERANCE` (1e-9 USD) of the recorded one, else printed with the largest difference, and `None` when the counts fix no pair (no rows, or a singular fit); and a summary of how many models have a list price, an exact pair and either. The section gives each pool model's page, its status there, the input and output price or `none found`, and the context window as the page states it, then a recommendation. No harness, converter or results file changes. (v1.189, review iter-144) A row's `task`, `item` and `model` must be non-empty strings; blank lines are skipped and a file with no rows is an error. The first line's largest `input_tokens` is over every row with an input count, dropped items included, and `none` when no row has one; the second line leaves out its parenthesis of dropped items per task when none is dropped; a model whose kept rows cost 0.0 at its list price prints `ratio none`. `fit` returns four values, the rounded pair, the largest difference it leaves and w, the largest difference the unrounded least-squares pair leaves. A model without an exact pair prints `no pair within 1e-09 USD` when w is above sqrt(n) times `TOLERANCE`, n its kept rows: a pair within `TOLERANCE` of every cost has a sum of squared differences of at most n times `TOLERANCE` squared, which the least-squares pair cannot exceed, so none of its differences could pass sqrt(n) times `TOLERANCE`; else it prints `no 4-decimal pair`, since a pair with more decimals is not ruled out. (v1.190) The line templates, costs to 6 decimals and ratios to 3, are those of `report`, which the small-matrix tests and the cached test pin. (v1.194, row E17a) The section ends with `### Context windows (2026-11-10)`, since 13.4 rule 3 and 5.2 rule 5 read a window for each model a delegation registry holds: for each of the 8 priced pool models, in ID order, the page and the date it was opened, what it states, the window in tokens and its kind, `vendor page` or `weights' position limit` (the open weights' config.json `max_position_embeddings` at a pinned Hugging Face revision, which is how far the weights attend, not the window of the service that recorded the costs); where each window binds at the 13.10 points under rule 3; and a recommendation for the registry of a later version (13.13, v1.196). The two OpenAI windows are those of the List prices table. No script, harness, converter or results file changes
- On the AutoBench matrix (12.9) at the pinned revision, checked when this section was written: the `matrix.csv` column `output_tokens` already counts the reasoning tokens. On 4,931 of the 4,933 scored pool cells (the other 2 have a billed cost of 0), `(input_tokens * input_cost_per_mtok + output_tokens * output_cost_per_mtok) / 1e6`, with the two prices from the `pool.json` `members` entry whose `display_id` is the row's `model`, gives a median ratio of 1.001 to the billed `cost`, within 1% on 47% of the cells, and adding `reasoning_tokens` to `output_tokens` gives a median ratio of 1.718. v1.21: the converter writes those counts with `--tokens` and the registry with `--registry` (12.9)
- Test status: the contract tests (`tests/test_delegation_eval.py`) were committed before `delegation_eval.py` existed, under a module-level `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=ModuleNotFoundError)`, as in 12.5. v1.20: the implementing commit removed that marker, re-exports `evaluate_delegation` from `model_routing` in `__all__`, and names the module in section 3 and in the README Layout block; from then on any failure in them fails the suite. The bar, seed, flavor, `min_samples`, `confidence` and `calibration_fraction` checks are the ones `evaluate` runs (12.3), from one shared helper, so the two cannot drift apart. There is no CLI subcommand: the AutoBench run is a script under `evidence/`. v1.35: the per-model size tests are committed under `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=AssertionError)` ahead of the harness change, on a matrix where one model writes 100 times what another does, so the pooled medians and the per-model means pick different delegates; the `cost_blind` decisions and the 13.4 rule 3 check are pinned by plain tests that pass before and after; the 13.10 results are regenerated in the implementing version. v1.36: the implementing commit removed those markers, and the v1.19 test that rebuilds each decision from the calibration half builds its stats with the token totals. v1.43: the multi-call harness tests (`tests/test_delegation_eval_work_calls.py`, a file of their own as v1.38 had) are committed ahead of the code, each under a `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)` that names what the v1.42 harness raises: `ValueError` where a test runs a point with `work_calls`, which `_points` rejects as a key outside the five, and `AssertionError` where a test expects a v1.43 error message (an invalid `work_calls`, a repeated point, a K-call point with no delegate) that the v1.42 key check raises first with other words. Next to them, plain tests pin that points without `work_calls` give the v1.42 result bit for bit (a SHA-256 of `json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True)` on two fixture runs, taken on the v1.42 harness), that the K-call held-out expressions with K = 1 equal the v1.42 ones exactly, and that the committed 13.10 config has no `work_calls`. The implementing commit removes the markers; v1.44 is that commit. From v1.45 a 13.10 point with K > 1 is a variant point (13.10). v1.57: the cached-point tests (`tests/test_delegation_cached_point.py`, a file of their own as v1.43 and v1.45 had) are committed ahead of the code, each under a `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)` that names what the v1.56 code raises: `ValueError` where a test runs a point with `cached_read_ratio`, which `_points` rejects as a key outside the six, `AssertionError` where a test expects a v1.57 error message (an invalid `cached_read_ratio`, a repeated point with the key read as 1.0, the new key message) that the v1.56 key check raises first with other words, or where it expects the two variant points or the `cached` column of 13.10, which v1.56 does not have. Next to them, plain tests pin that points without `cached_read_ratio` give the v1.56 result bit for bit (a SHA-256 of `json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True)` on fixture runs taken on the v1.56 harness, one with K-call points among them), that the committed `results.json` points and variant point 0 of the committed `variants.json` carry no `cached_read_ratio`, and that the `table` of `results.json` has no `cached` column. The implementing commit removes the markers; v1.58 is that commit (13.10). v1.74: the per-call tests (`tests/test_delegation_eval_call_sizes.py`, a file of their own as v1.43 and v1.57 had) are committed ahead of the code, each under a `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=AssertionError)`: the v1.73 harness ignores the two keys as extra keys, so a test sees the one-call cost of `input_tokens` and `output_tokens`, no `expected_work_calls` and no error, and an expected error is asserted through a helper that raises `AssertionError` when none comes. Next to them, plain tests pin that rows without the keys give the v1.73 result bit for bit (a SHA-256 of `json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True)` on a two-task fixture run with K-call and cached points, taken on the v1.73 harness), that the closed form of Rd is the sum of the calls' reads, and that a point with `work_calls` 1 is the point without it on per-call rows. The implementing commit removes the markers and regenerates both 13.10 files byte-identical; v1.75 is that commit. v1.222: the held-out tier tests (`tests/test_delegation_eval_tiers.py`, a file of their own as v1.43, v1.57 and v1.74 had) are committed ahead of the code, each under a `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=AssertionError)`, since the v1.221 harness prices every held-out cost at the base prices; next to them, plain tests pin that a registry without tiers gives every v1.221 held-out cost bit for bit (written out with `Model.base_cost`) and that, without tiers, the held-out costs of constant-size rows equal the decision's two costs. They replace `tests/test_price_tiers.py` `test_evaluate_delegation_prices_its_paths_at_the_base_prices`. The implementing commit removes the markers

### 13.10 Delegation results on AutoBench (`evidence/delegation/results.py`, v1.22)
Purpose: the 13.9 evidence on public data. The 13.5 decision, always inline and always delegating, scored on held-out AutoBench 2026 questions for every pool model as the parent, regenerated byte for byte from stated commands, with the tables in its RESULTS.md and the README checked against the committed file by the test suite. Standard library and the `model_routing` package only
- Inputs: the 12.9 converter's `--task-by all --tokens` matrix at the default width (one task, `all`, of 203 items over 20 models) and its `--registry` pool registry, so every cost is a list-price estimate from `pool.json` prices (13.9)
- Fixed in the script, and so in the committed config: bars 0.7 and 0.8 (in 12.10 every seed clears at 0.7 and about two thirds at 0.8), 50 seeds, both flavors, `parents=None` (the 20 pool models, ascending), the default `calibration_fraction`, `min_samples` and `confidence`, and a grid whose points 0 to 2 differ only in C: 8,000, 64,000 and 200,000 context tokens, each with B = 500, R = 500, T = 1 and `inline_separate_call` True. 200,000 is above the context windows of the two pool models with 131,072 and 131,000 tokens, so `parent_context_full` blocks inline for them at that point. v1.40 adds point 3: C = 8,000 with the same B, R and T and `inline_separate_call` False, the work folded into the parent's next call. On points 0 to 2 the `cost_effective` decision delegates on every seed for every parent at both bars, so without a folded point the evidence never exercises its inline branch. At K = 1 no folded cost reads C (13.2) and C enters only the 13.4 rule 3 check, so two folded points that differ only in C, both with room in every parent's window, give the same summary rows apart from `point`, and one folded point stands for them. Points 0 to 2 keep their indices
- `points() -> list`: the four point dicts in that order (three before v1.40), keys in the 13.9 order. `run(rows, registry) -> dict`: `evaluate_delegation(rows, registry, bars=[0.7, 0.8], points=points(), seeds=range(50))` keeping only `config`, `tasks` and `summary`, in that order (no `cells`, so the file stays under about 300 KB)
- v1.45, variant points: a point that changes a 13.2 knob the grid above holds fixed goes in a second file, `evidence/delegation/variants.json`, so `results.json` keeps its bytes and each file stays under about 300 KB (the four points above take 269,163 bytes, about 67,000 a point, so a fifth in the same file would pass it). `variant_points() -> list`: the variant point dicts in index order, keys in the 13.9 order (`work_calls` last); a later variant point is appended after them, so the indices of the earlier ones hold. Variant point 0: C = 64,000, B = 500, R = 500, T = 1, `inline_separate_call` False and `work_calls` 2. Folded work of K calls reads C in K - 1 of them on the inline path ((K - 1) * C, 13.2) and only the brief on the delegate path, so this point shows what one re-read of a 64,000-token context does to the decision; base point 3 (folded, K = 1) stands for every C with room in every parent's window, 64,000 among them (above), so the two compare at equal C and differ in K only
- v1.57, variant point 1: base point 2 with `cached_read_ratio` 0.1, that is C = 200,000, B = 500, R = 500, T = 1, `inline_separate_call` True and `cached_read_ratio` 0.1, keys in the 13.9 order (no `work_calls`, so K = 1). It is appended after variant point 0, whose index and summary rows hold, since each point's cells read only that point. Base point 2 has the largest C of the base grid, so its inline path pays most for re-reading C, and with a separate call at K = 1 that path reads C once; the two points have equal sizes and differ in rho only. 0.1 is an assumption, stated as one in RESULTS.md and the README: the AutoBench pool (13.9) records no cached price, and 0.1 is the ratio of the README cached-read `delegate` example (a cached read at a tenth of the list input price). rho moves no 13.4 check, so the two pool models whose context windows are under 200,000 tokens stay blocked from inline (`parent_context_full`) at variant point 1, as at base point 2
- v1.72, variant point 2: variant point 0 with `work_calls` set to `VARIANT_OBSERVED_WORK_CALLS = 15`, that is C = 64,000, B = 500, R = 500, T = 1, `inline_separate_call` False and `work_calls` 15, keys in the 13.9 order (`work_calls` last, no `cached_read_ratio`). It is appended after variant point 1, whose index and summary rows hold, as do variant point 0's, since each point's cells read only that point. 15 is the median of the runs' `model_calls` in the committed `evidence/trail/summary.json` (13.11: `stats`, `model_calls`, `median`, 15.0), rounded up with `math.ceil` when it is not a whole number: the median of a number of whole numbers is whole or halfway between two, so the constant is never below the observed median. The constant is a literal in the script, which never reads summary.json; a test builds it from summary.json so the two cannot drift, and a summary.json whose median moves fails that test until the constant and `variants.json` follow. Variant point 0 is the folded multi-call point, so the two compare at equal C and differ in K only: at K = 15 the inline path re-reads C in 14 calls instead of one ((K - 1) * C, 13.2) while each delegated call reads the brief in its place. Window checks at K = 15 on 64,000 context tokens (checked with a private prototype of this contract on the pinned AutoBench files when this section was written; the cached-file test pins them): over the 50 seeds S_in + S_out, the pooled calibration medians of 13.9, is 1,331 to 1,678, so the 13.4 rule 3 check `C + K * (S_in + S_out)` needs at most 89,170 tokens and the 5.2 rule 5 check of the delegate's last call, `B + K * (S_in + S_out)`, at most 25,670, both below the smallest pool window, 131,000 (`openai/gpt-oss-120b`). So neither check drops a parent or a delegate candidate on any seed: every cell of variant point 2 has `inline_allowed` True, no cell's reason is `parent_context_full`, and the run raises no 13.9 no-route `ValueError`. A delegate candidate dropped by 5.2 rule 5 while others remain would show in no reason, so on that side the bound of 25,670 tokens is what the cached-file test pins. `variants.json` keeps no cells, so the file shows this only by what it lacks, no delegation forced by a window; the cached-file test reads it from an in-process run that keeps the cells. At that C every parent keeps room up to K = 39, and TRAIL's largest run, 31 calls, would need 116,018 tokens. The point is a cost sensitivity at an observed size, never a measured hand-off: TRAIL's runs are another agent (`o3-mini` on GAIA, 13.11) on other work, the AutoBench label is still one answer's (13.9: each of the 15 calls is assumed to read and write what the item's one recorded call did, and whether the 15 calls succeed together is not measured), and the bar is held per call (13.8). RESULTS.md and the README say so
- v1.45, `run(rows, registry, grid="base") -> dict`: `grid` `"base"` is the run above on `points()`; `"variants"` is the same run, with the same bars, seeds and keys kept, on `variant_points()`; any other value, `None` among them, is a `ValueError` whose message starts `grid must be 'base' or 'variants'`, raised before `evaluate_delegation` is called
- `table(results) -> str`: one row per (bar, flavor, point) in config order, columns `bar` (as Python `str` prints it), `flavor`, `context` (that point's C), `separate` (v1.40: that point's `inline_separate_call` as Python `str` prints it, `True` or `False`, since points 0 and 3 share C), then `dec_success`, `dec_cost`, `inl_success`, `inl_cost`, `del_success` and `del_cost` (for `decision`, `inline` and `delegate`, the mean over the config parents of the summary rows' `success` and `cost_usd`, each mean a `math.fsum` over the parents divided by their number) and `delegated` (the same mean of the `decision` rows' `delegate_rate`). Layout of 12.4 (header, a dash row, two spaces between columns, trailing spaces stripped, no newline after the last row); success and `delegated` to 3 decimals, cost to 4
- v1.45, `table` on variant points: when any config point carries `work_calls`, a column `calls` follows `separate`, that point's `work_calls` as Python `str` prints it, and `1` for a point without it; a config whose points carry no `work_calls` prints the v1.40 columns, so the table of `results.json` is unchanged. `parent_table` and `breakeven_table` are unchanged and read a variants file as they read the base one
- v1.57, `table` with cached reads: when any config point carries `cached_read_ratio`, a column `cached` follows `calls` when the table prints that column and `separate` otherwise, that point's `cached_read_ratio` as Python `str` prints it, and `1.0` for a point without it; a config whose points carry no `cached_read_ratio` prints the columns it printed before v1.57, so the tables of `results.json` and of the v1.47 `variants.json` are unchanged. `parent_table` and `breakeven_table` are unchanged
- `parent_table(results, bar, point) -> str`: for one bar and point index, one row per (flavor, parent) in config order, columns `flavor`, `parent`, `delegated`, then the six strategy columns, each from that parent's own summary rows, same layout and formats
- `breakeven_table(results, bar, point) -> str` (v1.37): how much held-out success a lossy hand-off may lose, which the 13.9 quality assumption (the brief carries the whole item) leaves unmeasured, as a number from the committed file with no new run. For one bar and point index, one row per (flavor, parent) in config order, columns `flavor`, `parent`, `delegated` (the `decision` row's `delegate_rate`), `dec_success`, `inl_success` and `del_success` (the `success` of `decision`, `inline` and `delegate`), then two columns computed in floating point from those unrounded values, left to right:
  - `del_margin` = `del_success - inl_success`: the held-out success every hand-off could lose before always delegating does no better than always inline
  - `dec_breakeven` = `(dec_success - inl_success) / delegated`: the uniform drop in held-out success on the seeds the decision delegates at which the decision falls to always inline's success. Exact, up to floating-point rounding, for the success the summary stores when the results have one task, as the AutoBench file does: each summary `success` is then the mean over seeds of one cell's held-out rate, every seed weighted equally whatever its held-out size, and `delegate_rate` the share of seeds delegated, so lowering the rate of every delegated seed by L (for example, losing L of each delegated seed's held-out questions) lowers `dec_success` by exactly L times `delegated`. It is not the drop in a success pooled over every held-out question of every seed, which would weight seeds by their held-out sizes; with several tasks it is an approximation for a second reason, since a seed's success then pools its tasks. `-` where the stored `delegated` is 0, since the decision then never hands off; any other value, however small, is divided by
  - same layout as `parent_table`; every number to 3 decimals; a negative value (the decision or always delegating already scores below always inline with a lossless brief) prints with its sign, and a value that rounds to zero prints `0.000`, never `-0.000`
- All three tables raise `ValueError` for a missing `config` or `summary`, a repeated (bar, flavor, point, parent, strategy) summary key, or a missing row for a (bar, flavor, point, parent, strategy) of the config; `parent_table` and `breakeven_table` also for a bar not in the config bars or a point that is not an index into the config points; v1.269 (row D4h, review iter-029 nit 3): `table` also raises `ValueError` `config has no parents` when the config's `parents` list is empty, checked first, before any summary row is read (to v1.268 the mean over no parents was a `ZeroDivisionError`, which the CLI does not catch, so `--table` printed a traceback), and `config has no (bar, flavor, point)` when its `bars`, `flavors` or `points` list is empty, so it never prints a table of no rows; `parent_table` and `breakeven_table` print the header and dash rows alone for a config with no parents or no flavors, as before
- CLI: `python evidence/delegation/results.py --matrix PATH --registry PATH --out PATH` writes `json.dumps(run(rows, registry), indent=2)` and a newline, with the rows read from the JSONL matrix and the registry from `Registry.load`, through `<out>.part` renamed onto `--out` as in 12.7; `--table PATH` prints `table`; `--table PATH --bar X --point I` prints `parent_table`; `--breakeven PATH --bar X --point I` prints `breakeven_table` (v1.37). Exactly one of `--matrix`, `--table` and `--breakeven`; `--registry` and `--out` both with `--matrix` and never with `--table` or `--breakeven`; `--bar` and `--point` both or neither with `--table`, both with `--breakeven`, never with `--matrix`. Errors are one `error: ...` line on stderr and exit code 2. v1.45: `--grid base` or `--grid variants`, only with `--matrix`, where it is the `grid` of `run` and a missing `--grid` is `base` (the CLI passes `grid="base"`, since `run` refuses `None`); so `--matrix PATH --registry PATH --grid variants --out PATH` writes `json.dumps(run(rows, registry, grid="variants"), indent=2)` and a newline as above. `--grid` with `--table` or `--breakeven` is the usage error `error: --grid is only used with --matrix`, checked after the `--registry` and `--out` one and before the `--bar` and `--point` checks of `--table` and `--breakeven`; any other `--grid` value is argparse's invalid-choice error for `--grid` (`error: argument --grid: invalid choice: ...`); both exit 2 with one line. v1.249 (row H5, review iter-148 must-fix 1 and should-fix 1, as 13.13 v1.198): with `--matrix`, once its usage checks pass, the output path and its `<out>.part` are formed before the matrix or the registry is read, so an `--out` with an empty file name, such as `/` or `.`, is one `error: ` line and exit 2 before any file is read (to v1.248 the run went first and the same line came after it); every exit after those checks leaves no `<out>.part`, one that an earlier run left included; and an `OSError` from removing `<out>.part` is ignored, so it never changes the exit code or the line printed. v1.269 (row D4h, review iter-029 nit 4 and review iter-172): with `--matrix`, once the output path and `<out>.part` are formed, `clashing_input(out, [("--matrix", <matrix>), ("--registry", <registry>)])` is checked, and an option it returns is the usage error `error: --out (or its .part file) names the same file as <option>`, exit 2, before any file is read, written or removed, so every file is left as it was, a stale `<out>.part` included; the v1.249 removal applies to the exits after it. To v1.268 an `--out` naming the matrix or the registry replaced that input with the results on a run that ended well, and a `<out>.part` naming one was overwritten by the run and, on any exit, removed. `clashing_input(out, inputs) -> str | None`: `out` a path with a non-empty file name, `inputs` a sequence of `(option, path)` pairs; it returns the option of the first pair, in order, whose path names the same file as `out` or as `<out>.part`, else `None`, and opens, writes and removes no file. Two paths name the same file when their `os.path.realpath` values are equal, so another spelling (`d/../m.jsonl`) and a symbolic link count as the file they reach (an `--out` that is a link to an input is refused, though the rename would replace only the link), or when both exist and `os.path.samefile` says so, a hard link; an `OSError` from `samefile` counts as not the same. 12.9 v1.21 makes the same kind of check between its two outputs

- v1.112, where a fixed policy beats the decision on both counts: one more Reading it bullet, the last of `## Reading it`, built from the committed file. A (bar, flavor, point, parent) group is dominated by `inline` or by `delegate` when that strategy's summary row has `success` at least the `decision` row's and `cost_usd` at most its, at least one of the two strictly, compared with plain `>=`, `<=`, `>` and `<` on the stored floats (no tolerance, as in 13.5). The bullet gives how many groups are dominated, out of how many, and how many in each flavor; in `cost_blind`, how many by each fixed policy, that always delegating has exactly the decision's `success` in each of its groups, and the one parent of the always-inline groups; and in `cost_effective`, every dominated group, one clause per (strategy, parent, bar) in config order, naming its points by C and `inline_separate_call` (the points with inline work in a call of their own first) and giving each point's `success` and `cost_usd` for that strategy and for the decision (success to 3 decimals, written once when it prints the same at every point; cost to 4). Its reason is in 13.5 and 13.9 terms only: the decision reads only the calibration half, where the bar is held on lower bounds and each path is priced at that half's mean sizes (13.9), so a path chosen there can score lower or cost more on the held-out half; a delegate that clears is taken whatever its cost when the parent does not clear (13.5 row 2), the paths are ordered by bound before cost when neither clears (row 7), and `cost_blind` keeps a tie of equal bounds inline whatever delegating would save (rows 4 and 7), as when the delegate is the parent's own model, whose bound is the parent's (the v1.31 bullet of 13.5). The test builds the bullet whole from the file and asserts that no group is dominated by both strategies, that always delegating's `success` equals the decision's in each `cost_blind` group it dominates, and that the always-inline groups have one parent. On the v1.111 file 30 of the 320 groups are dominated: 26 in `cost_blind` (20 by always delegating, 6 by always inline, all with `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` as the parent) and 4 in `cost_effective` (always delegating with `google/gemini-3.7-flash` as the parent at bar 0.7, point 3, and always inline with `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` at bar 0.8, points 0, 1 and 3).
