Each README: a first line `# Delegation evidence on <dataset>`; a paragraph on what the folder measures that names `DESIGN.md` and its 13 section; a sentence starting `This folder holds` that names every file the folder holds beside the README, the script as `` `results.py` `` and each `.json` file and `RESULTS.md` as a Markdown link to itself; a `## Inputs` section that links the converter whose output the script reads by its path from the folder, gives the converter options its RESULTS.md `## Regenerate` block passes, says the matrix is not committed and links that dataset's evidence README for the source, pinned revision and licence; and a `## Regenerate` section that links `RESULTS.md#regenerate` and holds no fenced block, so the commands stay in one place, the RESULTS.md the 12.13 workflow reruns. ASCII, with no en or em dash; every relative link names a file in the repo, and every `#` fragment a heading of that file. From v1.319 (row E21f) the fourth delegation folder, `evidence/response-bank-delegation` (13.14), has one too. Per folder:
  - `evidence/delegation`: `results.py`, `results.json` (the four base points), `variants.json` (the variant points, whose commands are in RESULTS.md `## Variant points`, linked as `RESULTS.md#variant-points`) and `RESULTS.md`; the converter `../autobench-2026/convert.py` (12.9) with `--task-by all --tokens --registry`, whose matrix and pool registry are not committed; and that the `--table` mode also prints the tables of the 13.12 and 13.13 RESULTS.md files, whose scripts load this one by file path
  - `evidence/tau2-bench-delegation`: `results.py`, `registry.json`, `results.json` (one task per domain), `pooled.json` (the `--task-by all` sensitivity) and `RESULTS.md`; the converter `../tau2-bench/convert.py` (12.14) with `--calls`
  - `evidence/llmrouterbench-delegation`: `results.py`, `registry.json`, `results.json` and `RESULTS.md`; the converter `../llmrouterbench/convert.py` (12.18) at its defaults, whose 13-model matrix the script reduces to the 8 registry models by `select_rows`
  - `evidence/response-bank-delegation` (v1.319, row E21f): `results.py`, `registry.json`, `results.json` and `RESULTS.md`; the converter `../response-bank/convert.py` (12.20) at its defaults (its RESULTS.md `## Regenerate` block passes only `--out`), whose matrix of the 5 models the script keeps whole but for the 4 `mmlu_pro` items with null token counts, which `select_rows`, loaded by file path from `../llmrouterbench-delegation/results.py`, drops
  - Tests, in `tests/test_evidence_delegation_readmes.py`, per folder: the title line; the file set, equal to the folder's `.py`, `.json` and `.md` files beside the README, each named as above; the converter link and each of its options, each also in the converter line of the RESULTS.md `## Regenerate` block; the `RESULTS.md#regenerate` link and no fenced block; every relative link and fragment resolving; the `DESIGN.md` section; ASCII. Docs and test only: no script, results file, top-level README or workflow change

### 13.11 Observed sub-agent sizes: the TRAIL summary (`evidence/trail/summary.py`, v1.67)
Purpose: set real sub-agent usage from public traces beside the sizes the 13.9 grid assumes, the one use `research/hand-off-data.md` finds for TRAIL. The committed delegation points of the base grid and variant points 0 and 1 do one or two work calls (no `work_calls` key, so K = 1, or 2 on variant point 0), while TRAIL's sub-agent runs make 3 to 31. These numbers are observed usage, never 13.1 inputs, and they feed no decision: TRAIL has no success label and no run of a task without its sub-agent, and the sub-agent is its parent's model. From v1.73 one of them sets a grid size: the median of `model_calls` is the `work_calls` of 13.10 variant point 2, held as a literal in `evidence/delegation/results.py` that a test checks against summary.json, so `variants.json` uses it as a cost sensitivity at an observed size, never as a measured hand-off. Standard library only (argparse, hashlib, json, statistics, sys, urllib, pathlib); the package never imports it
- Source, pinned: the public GitHub repo `patronus-ai/trail-benchmark` at commit `REVISION = "0ffbed9db859b4a66250dc783fa4dccf86869595"` (commit date 2025-05-14), folder `FOLDER = "benchmarking/data/GAIA"`, `LICENCE = "MIT"` (the repo's LICENSE file, `Copyright (c) 2025 patronus-ai`), `REPO_URL = "https://github.com/patronus-ai/trail-benchmark"`, `BASE_URL = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/patronus-ai/trail-benchmark/<REVISION>/benchmarking/data/GAIA/"`. The gated Hugging Face copy is never read: its card asks users not to reshare it outside a gated or private repository. `FILES` maps each of the folder's 117 trace files, named `<trace id>.json` with a 32-character lowercase hexadecimal trace id, to its byte size and sha256, in ascending name order: 111,205,744 bytes, 96,308 to 9,036,173 each. The folder has no checksum file, so the pins in the script are the only trust root; when this was written each of the 117 files matched the size and git blob SHA-1 of the repo tree at `REVISION` (the GitHub API). The repo's SWE-bench traces are not read
- `default_cache_dir()` is `~/.cache/ai-model-routing-evidence/trail/` (from `Path.home()`), never inside the repo; `fetch(cache_dir=None) -> Path` and `verify(path, size, sha256)` as in 12.14: `fetch` creates the folder if needed and goes through `FILES` in order; a present file is verified and kept, and one that fails `verify` is a `ValueError` naming it and is left in place; a missing one is downloaded from `BASE_URL` plus its name into `<cache>/<name>.part` with a 60-second timeout, verified and renamed onto `<cache>/<name>`, the `.part` file removed when the download or the check fails, and any error but a `ValueError` while downloading is raised as an `OSError` naming the file; `read_trace(path)` reads one UTF-8 JSON file, and invalid UTF-8, invalid JSON (a number with more digits than the interpreter converts to an int included, 4,300 by default, for which `json` raises a plain `ValueError`, not a `JSONDecodeError`) or nesting past the recursion limit is a `ValueError` naming it, as `read_run` does in 12.14
- `runs(trace, name) -> list[dict]` reduces one parsed trace file, named `name`, to its sub-agent runs, so the script holds one trace in memory at a time. The spans form a tree: `trace["spans"]` is the list of root spans and each span's `child_spans`, when present, the list of its children; the walk is depth first in file order (a span, then its children in list order) and reads no other key for structure (`logs`, `events` and `links` are never walked); it keeps its own stack, so a deep tree of spans cannot reach the recursion limit
  - a sub-agent run is a span whose `span_name` is `ToolCallingAgent.run`; its model calls are the spans of its subtree whose `span_name` ends in `.__call__`. The top agent is the span named `CodeAgent.run`, present once in each of the 117 pinned traces, and in every pinned trace each run lies inside it
  - `ValueError`, its message naming `name` and, for a span whose `span_id` is a non-empty string, that id: `trace` not a dict; a `trace_id` that is not a string equal to `name` without its `.json` suffix; `spans` not a list; a span that is not a dict, whose `span_id` is not a non-empty string, whose `span_name` is not a string, or whose `child_spans` is present and not a list; a run with no `CodeAgent.run` ancestor (no top agent handed it work), with a run ancestor (whose calls it would share) or with no model call; a model call inside a run whose `span_attributes` is not a dict, or whose `llm.token_count.prompt` or `llm.token_count.completion` is neither a non-empty string of at most 19 ASCII decimal digits (as all 1,507 counted calls in the pinned files store them) nor an int that is not a bool, its value in either case from 0 to `2**63 - 1`, the largest signed 64-bit integer. The cap is checked before any conversion, so no count string reaches the interpreter's limit on converting digits to an int (4,300 by default, whose own `ValueError` would not name the file), and every median converts to a float. When a trace holds several of these faults, which one is reported is not promised (each test holds one)
  - returns one dict per run in walk order, with the keys `trace_id`, `span_id`, `model_calls` (the number of model calls in the run), `prompt_tokens` and `completion_tokens` (the sums of the two counts over those calls, as ints) and `first_call_prompt_tokens` (the prompt count of the run's first model call in walk order), in that order. A trace with no run gives `[]`. Model calls outside a run are never read, so the one `LiteLLMModel.__call__` span of the pinned files that has no token counts (in a top agent's step) changes nothing
- `summarize(per_trace) -> dict` over a list of `runs` results, one per trace read: `ValueError` for `per_trace` that is not a non-empty list or tuple, an entry that is not a list, no run in any entry, or a (`trace_id`, `span_id`) that repeats. Returns the keys `source` (`{"repo": REPO_URL, "folder": FOLDER, "revision": REVISION, "licence": LICENCE}`), `traces` (the number of entries), `traces_with_runs` (the entries with at least one run), `run_count`, `stats` and `runs`, in that order. `stats` maps each of `model_calls`, `prompt_tokens`, `completion_tokens` and `first_call_prompt_tokens`, in that order, to `{"min": int, "median": float, "max": int}` over all runs, the median being `float(statistics.median(values))`; `runs` lists every run, entries in input order and each entry's runs in its order. The input is not modified
- CLI: `python evidence/trail/summary.py --out PATH [--cache DIR]`, entry point `main(argv=None) -> int`. Options are checked first, so a bad one downloads nothing (`--out` must pass the 12.14 `--out` check, v1.171; to v1.170 an empty path, `.`, `..`, `/` or a path ending in `/` was a bad option). Then it fetches, reads and reduces the files one at a time in `FILES` order, summarizes, writes `json.dumps(summary, indent=2)` and a newline to `<out>.part`, prints `json.dumps` of the summary without its `runs` key, `indent=2`, flushing stdout, and only then renames `<out>.part` onto `--out`, so a write to stdout that fails (a closed pipe) is an I/O error that leaves `--out` as it was. After a `BrokenPipeError` from that print or its flush (v1.150), `main` points the stdout file descriptor at `os.devnull` (`os.dup2`, the pattern of the Python `signal` module docs' note on SIGPIPE) and then handles the error as before: the failed flush leaves the printed bytes in the stdout buffer, and the interpreter's own flush at exit retried them, so the process exited 120 with an `Exception ignored in: <_io.TextIOWrapper name='<stdout>' ...>` line and a `BrokenPipeError` line after its one `error:` line (reproduced on 3.10 for a report under the stdout buffer and on 3.14 for a 100,000-character one as well); now it exits 2 with the one line. A stdout with no file descriptor (a stand-in object, as in-process tests use) is left as it is, and so is one the redirect fails on It returns 0 on success and 2 with exactly one `error: ...` line on stderr (a carriage return or line feed in the message written as `\r` or `\n`) and nothing on stdout for a missing or bad option, a size or hash mismatch, invalid data or an I/O error, but for a rename that fails after the print, the one failure with the summary already on stdout; on any failure `--out` is not written (an existing one is left as it was) and `<out>.part` is removed
- Choices (v1.67):
  - sizes from the model-call spans, not the run span: each run span also carries `llm.token_count.prompt` and `llm.token_count.completion`, but on each of the 60 pinned runs both are below the sums over the run's model calls (the run spans' own prompt counts span 3,230 to 245,946, the sums 5,508 to 357,993), and the files do not say what they count, so they are never read
  - first call in walk order: on all 60 pinned runs the walk order of a run's model calls is also the order of their `timestamp` values, so the first call is the earliest, with no earlier call of the run in its prompt; the files do not mark which part of that prompt is the brief
  - observed usage, not 13.1 inputs: each call's prompt count is everything that call read (the instructions, the brief and the run's history so far), while 13.1 and 13.2 take the new input each call adds and grow the context from it, so `prompt_tokens` counts each re-read again and must never be read as K times the work's input per call. `model_calls` is the one number comparable with 13.1's K, though it counts every model call of the run, whatever the call's role (a planning call included)
  - GAIA only, one model: every counted call in the pinned GAIA traces names `o3-mini`, so the summary keeps no model column and compares no price; the SWE-bench traces were not scanned for runs (`research/hand-off-data.md`)
- Default output on the pinned files (checked with a private prototype of this contract when this section was written; the cached-file test pins it): 117 traces, 47 with runs, 60 runs (38 traces with one, 5 with two, 4 with three); `model_calls` 3, 15.0 and 31; `prompt_tokens` 5,508, 94,868.5 and 357,993; `completion_tokens` 1,990, 8,343.5 and 21,188; `first_call_prompt_tokens` 549, 594.5 and 765 (min, median and max), the numbers `research/hand-off-data.md` states
- Committed files (v1.70 specifies them and v1.71 commits them; before v1.70 this bullet left them to a later version):
  - `evidence/trail/summary.json`, the output of `python evidence/trail/summary.py --out evidence/trail/summary.json` on the pinned files, under 300,000 bytes. Its text is `json.dumps` of its content with `indent=2` and a newline; its `source` holds the script's constants and `traces` the length of `FILES`; `run_count` is the length of `runs`, `traces_with_runs` the number of distinct `trace_id` values among them, each a pinned file's name without `.json`; each run has the keys `runs` returns, in that order; and `summarize` of its runs, grouped by trace in file order, with one empty entry for each trace without a run, gives the file's content again. The cached-file test regenerates it from the 117 cached files and compares the bytes
  - `evidence/trail/RESULTS.md`, starting `# TRAIL sub-agent sizes`: the sentence "In {traces_with_runs} of the {traces} GAIA traces the top agent (`CodeAgent.run`) hands work to a sub-agent (`ToolCallingAgent.run`), {run_count} sub-agent runs in all." followed by "{n1} traces hold one run, {n2} two and {n3} three.", the number of traces with one, two and three runs (no pinned trace has more); a table with the header `| count | min | median | max |`, a `|---|---|---|---|` row and one row per `stats` key in its order, `` | `<key>` | <min> | <median> | <max> | ``, each number with thousands commas and a median that is a whole number written without `.0`; a link to summary.json with its size, `[summary.json](summary.json) (<size> bytes)`; the repo URL, the revision linked to its tree and followed by `(commit date <date>)`, the date `research/hand-off-data.md` gives for it, the folder, `Licence: MIT` and the line `Copyright (c) 2025 patronus-ai`; the regenerate command alone in a `bash` block, with the number and total size of the pinned files (`<n> pinned files (<bytes> bytes)`); the observed-usage caveat of the Choices bullet ("observed usage, not the inputs of DESIGN 13.1", and that `prompt_tokens` "must never be read as K times the work's input per call"); what TRAIL lacks, one bullet each, starting `- No success label`, `- No run of a task without its sub-agent` and `- The sub-agent is its parent's model`, so that its numbers "feed no decision", with (v1.73) the one number a results file uses named: "One of them sets a grid size: the median of `model_calls`, {median}, is the `work_calls` of variant point 2 of [variants.json](../delegation/variants.json)", a literal in the delegation script checked against summary.json, "never a measured hand-off", and the phrase "no results file" nowhere; the delegation points sentence (v1.73) "The committed delegation points do one work call (`evidence/delegation/results.json`), two (variant point 0 of `evidence/delegation/variants.json`) or {median} (variant point 2 of the same file, the median below); every run here makes more than one or two."; and the workflow choice below ("The `evidence` workflow does not rerun it"). No folder README: as in `evidence/delegation/`, the script writes the committed file itself, so RESULTS.md holds the source and the command a converter README would
  - README, `### Delegation on AutoBench`: one sentence after the first link to `research/hand-off-data.md` and before the first `text` block, built from summary.json and the committed points: "Real sub-agent runs make more model calls than the grid's one, or two at variant point 0 of [RESULTS.md](evidence/delegation/RESULTS.md#variant-points), whose variant point 2 takes their median (`work_calls`): in the public TRAIL traces (licence MIT), the top agent hands work to a sub-agent {run_count} times in {traces_with_runs} of {traces} GAIA traces, and each of those runs makes {min} to {max} model calls (median {median}), observed usage only, since TRAIL has no success label and no run of a task without its sub-agent ([evidence/trail/RESULTS.md](evidence/trail/RESULTS.md), `DESIGN.md` 13.11).", numbers from `stats.model_calls` written as in RESULTS.md (v1.348, row DOC19b: to v1.348 it read `or two at variant point 0 below, and variant point 2 below takes their median`, and from v1.349 it points at RESULTS.md, since the 13.10 README trim takes the variant points out of the subsection). Its claims are checked against the committed points: every point of `evidence/delegation/results.json` has K = 1, variant point 0 of `variants.json` has `work_calls` 2, the smallest run's `model_calls` is above the K of every committed point but variant point 2, and (v1.73, which opens the sentence with variant point 2) variant point 2's `work_calls` is the rounded-up median of 13.10. The Layout block gains the line `(TRAIL sub-agent sizes: evidence/trail/RESULTS.md)` right after the delegation line, at the same indent
  - `research/hand-off-data.md` states the summary's numbers: a test builds each TRAIL figure it gives (the file count and total bytes from `FILES`; traces with runs and runs; the min, median and max of `model_calls`, `prompt_tokens` and `completion_tokens`; the min and max of `first_call_prompt_tokens`) from summary.json, in the file's own wording, and finds it there
  - Workflow (v1.70 choice): the 12.13 workflow does not rerun the script. Its numbers feed no decision, the one number `variants.json` uses (v1.73) is a literal in the delegation script that a test checks against the committed summary.json, the cached-file test compares the committed bytes wherever the 117 files are cached, and a rerun would add 111,205,744 bytes to the job's downloads; rerunning it there is left to a later version
- Test status: `tests/test_evidence_trail.py` is committed before the script, under a module-level `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=ModuleNotFoundError)`, which v1.69, the implementing version, removed. Every test uses small synthetic traces shaped like a trace file (top keys `trace_id` and `spans`; span keys `timestamp`, `span_id`, `parent_span_id`, `span_name`, `span_attributes` and `child_spans`), except one that reads the pinned files only when all 117 are in the default cache and skips otherwise (CI never downloads them). `tests/test_evidence_trail_results.py`, the contract of the committed files, is committed before them (v1.70) under a module-level `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, raises=FileNotFoundError)`, since every test reads a committed TRAIL file first, which v1.71 removes

### 13.12 Delegation results on tau2-bench (`evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/results.py`, v1.78)
Purpose: the 13.9 evidence on a second public dataset, in the shape of 13.10, where each item is a multi-call agent conversation priced on its own calls (13.9 v1.74 per-call sizes) instead of one answer: the 13.5 decision, always inline and always delegating, scored on held-out tau2-bench conversations for each kept agent as the parent, regenerated byte for byte from stated commands, with the tables in its RESULTS.md checked against the committed file by the test suite. Standard library and the `model_routing` package only. v1.78 specifies it with contract tests ahead of the code and files; v1.79 commits the script, `registry.json`, `results.json` and RESULTS.md; v1.80 specifies the README subsection and the workflow lines (12.8, 12.13) and v1.81 adds them; v1.82 specifies a pooled run beside the per-domain one and v1.83 commits it
- Matrix: the 12.14 converter's `--calls` output at its other defaults (trial 0, one task per domain: `airline` 50 items, `retail` 114 and `telecom` 114), which leaves out `openai/o4-mini-2025-04-16` (its new input is negative on 2,962 of its calls) and keeps 3 agents and 834 rows. The matrix, 341,633 bytes (its size and SHA-256 pinned by the 12.14 cached-file test), is never committed
- Per-domain tasks, as 12.15: each domain is one kind of job. At the default `min_samples` 30 airline's calibration half is under 30 on 40 of the 50 seeds (12.14), so no model clears a bar there and every airline cell of those seeds is 13.5 row 7 (`none_clear`) unless 13.4 blocks inline (row 1); RESULTS.md says so. v1.82 runs the pooled task (`--task-by all`) beside it as a sensitivity (the Pooled run bullet below)
- Registry, committed as `evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/registry.json` and written by hand: the models.json format (section 9) with the top keys `as_of` (`"2026-11-06"`, the day the pages were opened), `note` (a sentence that names `research/delegation-datasets.md`) and `models`, in that order; each model with the keys `id`, `family`, `price_in`, `price_out`, `context_window`, `source` and `notes`, in that order, models in ID order; no `price_valid_until`, since no page states an end date and these prices only reprice recorded 2025 runs. Its text is `json.dumps` of its content with `indent=2` and a newline. The three models, with prices in USD per 1M tokens:
  - `anthropic/claude-3-7-sonnet-20250219`, family `anthropic`, 3 and 15, window 200,000, source `https://github.com/sierra-research/tau2-bench/tree/<REVISION>/data/tau2/results/final` with the 12.14 `REVISION`. No current vendor page states its price or its window ([`research/delegation-datasets.md`](research/delegation-datasets.md)): the prices are the ones its recorded costs give exactly on all 4,371 trial-0 calls, and the window is an assumption, which `notes` says (with the word `assumed`) along with its retirement on the Claude API on February 19, 2026
  - `openai/gpt-4.1-2025-04-14`, family `openai`, 2.00 and 8.00, window 1,047,576, source `https://platform.openai.com/docs/models/gpt-4.1`; `notes` says the page lists it as Deprecated
  - `openai/gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14`, family `openai`, 0.40 and 1.60, window 1,047,576, source `https://platform.openai.com/docs/models/gpt-4.1-mini`; `notes` says the page lists it as Deprecated
- Script constants: `BARS = (0.3, 0.4, 0.5, 0.6, 0.7, 0.8)`, the 12.15 bars, so the routing and the delegation evidence on this dataset read the same bars; `SEEDS = 50`; `KEYS = ("config", "tasks", "summary")`. `points()` returns the 13.10 `points()` of `evidence/delegation/results.py`, the four base points, loaded by file path with `importlib`, so the two grids cannot drift. The 13.10 variant points are not run: variant points 0 and 2 set `work_calls` above 1, which 13.9 v1.74 refuses on per-call rows (a row's lists are already its work calls), and variant point 1, cached reads of C at 0.1, runs in process for RESULTS.md only (the Cached point bullet below, v1.88)
- `run(rows, registry) -> dict`: `evaluate_delegation(rows, registry, bars=list(BARS), points=points(), seeds=range(SEEDS))`, keeping only `KEYS` in that order (no `cells`, so the file stays small)
- CLI: `python evidence/tau2-bench-delegation/results.py --matrix PATH --registry PATH --out PATH`, entry point `main(argv=None) -> int`, all three options required (a missing one is an argparse usage error, exit 2). It reads the rows from the JSONL matrix and the registry with `Registry.load`, writes `json.dumps(run(rows, registry), indent=2)` and a newline to `<out>.part` and renames it onto `--out`, and returns 0. An unreadable or invalid file, or a `ValueError` from the harness, prints one line starting `error: ` to stderr and returns 1, with `--out` untouched and no `.part` left. The tables come from the 13.10 CLI, which reads any results file of this shape: `python evidence/delegation/results.py --table <file>`, and with `--bar X --point I` its `parent_table`. v1.249 (row H5, review iter-148 must-fix 1 and should-fix 1, as 13.13 v1.198): the output path and its `<out>.part` are formed inside that same error handling, so an `--out` with an empty file name, such as `/` or `.`, is that one `error: ` line and exit 1 too, before any file is read (to v1.248 it was a `ValueError` traceback); every exit 1 after the options parse leaves no `<out>.part`, one that an earlier run left included, while exit 0 leaves none either; and an `OSError` from removing `<out>.part` is ignored, so it never changes the exit code or the line printed. v1.269 (row D4h, as 13.10 v1.269): once the output path and its `<out>.part` are formed, the 13.10 `clashing_input`, taken from evidence/delegation/results.py loaded by file path as `points()` loads it, is checked on `--matrix` and then `--registry`; an option it returns prints the one line `error: --out (or its .part file) names the same file as <option>` and returns 2, a usage error apart from the exit 1 of a bad file, before any file is read, written or removed, so every file is left as it was, a stale `<out>.part` included
- Checked with a private prototype of this contract on the 12 pinned files when this section was written (the cached-file tests pin each number): the run takes about 2 seconds over 21,600 cells; the results file is 132,475 bytes with 432 summary rows (6 bars, 2 flavors, 4 points, 3 parents and 3 strategies); over the seeds `expected_work_calls` (K_dec) is 10 to 12 on airline, 11 to 12 on retail and 17 to 19 on telecom, and the per-call `S_in + S_out` is 604 to 718, 643 to 675 and 512 to 574. So the forecast work `K_dec * (S_in + S_out)` is at most 10,242 tokens, the 5.2 rule 5 check of the delegate's last call needs at most 10,742 and no cell lacks a route, and the 13.4 rule 3 check needs at most 74,242 at C = 64,000. At C = 200,000 (point 2) it is above the assumed 200,000-token window of `anthropic/claude-3-7-sonnet-20250219` on every cell, so inline is blocked (`parent_context_full`) on all 1,800 of that parent's point 2 cells and on no other cell. That block follows from the assumed window, and RESULTS.md says so
- Prototype results, for the reading below: in `cost_effective` the decision delegates on every cell (`delegate_rate` 1.0 on all 72 decision rows), but mostly by 13.5 row 7, not by cost: of its 10,800 cells 6,941 are `none_clear` (no agent clears the bar, so the calibration bounds decide), 2,301 `delegate_cheaper` (no cell is `inline_cheaper`), 900 `parent_context_full` and 658 `parent_below_bar`; the 10,800 `cost_blind` cells hold the same 6,941, 900 and 658 with 1,375 `delegate_better` and 926 `inline_as_good`. At bar 0.3 and point 0 the means over the three parents are 19.8422 USD on a held-out half for the decision against 67.1856 for always inline, with held-out success 0.550 against 0.574; per parent the decision costs more than always inline only for `openai/gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14`, 18.9993 against 17.5937

- Cached point (v1.88, its RESULTS.md parts committed by v1.89): the 13.10 variant point 1, base point 2 with `cached_read_ratio` 0.1 (C = 200,000, B = 500, R = 500, T = 1, `inline_separate_call` True), applies to per-call rows as 13.9 defines it. It sets no `work_calls`, so the 13.9 v1.74 point check accepts it, and its ratio prices only the parent's reads of C in its inline work calls: in each held-out inline cost the integer `K(i, P) * C` times rho, to which Rd(i, P) is added (13.9 v1.75), and in the decision `K_dec * C * rho` (13.2 v1.41 with `work_calls` K_dec); never the work's own history, which a per-call row prices at list price on both paths (13.9 v1.74), and never the delegate path. It runs in process on both matrices with the committed registry, `BARS`, the 50 seeds and four points: base point 2, `variant_points()[1]` of `evidence/delegation/results.py` (loaded by file path, as `points()` is), and that point with the ratio at 0.25 and at 0, two sensitivities of the stated 0.1: 0.25 is the cached-input price over the input price on both OpenAI agents' model pages in `research/delegation-datasets.md` (0.50 against 2.00 and 0.10 against 0.40 USD per 1M tokens), and 0 charges no read of C at all
  - Result: at each of the three ratios the decision has the mode, model and reason of base point 2 on every cell of both runs, in both flavors, and so do the cell's `below_bar`, `delegate`, `inline_allowed` and per-strategy `correct` and its `delegate` cost; the `decision` cost is the `delegate` one where the decision delegates, as on every `cost_effective` cell, and the `inline` one where it works inline, as on some `cost_blind` cells, so only the `inline` cost and with it those `cost_blind` decision costs move. Why: only `inline_cost_usd` reads rho, and it does not fall as rho rises (each step of it multiplies or adds non-negative numbers, and a rounded multiply or add is monotone), so a cell that delegates at 0 because its delegate path is cheaper delegates at every ratio up to 1, as does a cell that delegates by a row that reads no cost; at base point 2 every `cost_effective` cell delegates, and at 0 none turns inline. In `cost_blind` no row reads cost (13.5 row 4)

### 13.13 Delegation results on LLMRouterBench (`evidence/llmrouterbench-delegation/results.py`, v1.196)
Purpose: the 13.9 evidence on a third public dataset, whose items are single calls as on AutoBench (13.10), over the 9 kinds of job of 12.18, in the shape of 13.12: the 13.5 decision, always inline and always delegating, scored on held-out LLMRouterBench items for each priced pool model as the parent, regenerated byte for byte from stated commands, with the tables in its RESULTS.md checked against the committed file by the test suite. Standard library and the `model_routing` package only. v1.196 specifies it with contract tests ahead of the code and files (row E17b); the commit that adds the script, `registry.json`, `results.json` and RESULTS.md is row E17c; the README lines, the README Limitations line that the delegation evidence reads three datasets, and the 12.13 workflow lines are row E17d
- Pool, the script's `POOL`: the 8 of the 12.18 pool's 13 models that [`research/delegation-datasets.md`](research/delegation-datasets.md) `## LLMRouterBench (2026-11-09)` prices by the registry rule it recommends, a vendor page price, else the one price pair the recorded costs give within 1e-9 USD on every kept item (as 13.12 prices `anthropic/claude-3-7-sonnet-20250219`): `anthropic/claude-sonnet-4`, `openai/gpt-5`, `openai/gpt-5-chat` and `z-ai/glm-4.6` (page prices) and `deepseek/deepseek-r1-0528`, `deepseek/deepseek-v3-0324`, `deepseek/deepseek-v3.1-terminus` and `internlm/intern-s1` (exact pairs), a tuple in ID order. 13.9 needs every model of the rows in the registry, so the other 5, which have neither (`google/gemini-2.5-flash`, `google/gemini-2.5-pro`, `moonshotai/kimi-k2-0905` and the two Qwen3 models), are left out. That leaves out `google/gemini-2.5-pro`, the best single model of the routing results (12.19), which RESULTS.md says
- Matrix: the 12.18 converter's default output, the 13-model rows that the first command of 12.19 writes, reduced in the script by `select_rows`; the converter is unchanged. `select_rows(rows, models) -> tuple[list, list]` keeps, in input order, the rows whose `model` is in `models`, then drops every kept row of an item on which a kept row has a null `input_tokens` or `output_tokens`, since 13.9 needs both counts on every row of a complete matrix; it returns the rows left and the dropped item IDs, ascending as strings, and does not modify its input. Each a `ValueError`, before any output: `models` that is not a non-empty list or tuple of distinct non-empty strings; `rows` that is not a list or tuple; a row that is not a dict or lacks one of the keys `item`, `model`, `input_tokens` and `output_tokens`; a model of `models` that no row has; no row left. A row whose model is outside `models` is ignored whatever its counts, and every other check is 13.9's, on what `select_rows` returns
- Why a filter and not `convert(extracts, pool=POOL)` (12.18), checked on the pinned file with a private prototype when this section was written: with the 8-model pool `convert` keeps the 9 tasks of 12.18 with the same items, its rows on them are those of the filtered default matrix, and it adds a tenth task, `swe-bench` (split `verified`, 500 items), which in the 13-model pool lacks only `qwen/qwen3-235b-a22b-thinking-2507`. It is left out of this version, for three reasons. On its items the registry prices below miss the recorded costs by up to 0.047418 USD (`internlm/intern-s1`) to 0.790308 USD (`anthropic/claude-sonnet-4`) for every model but `openai/gpt-5`, whose list price gives its recorded costs there within 1e-9 USD, so the five exact pairs are exact on the 9 benchmarks only and swe-bench's costs come from another price table. Its prompts reach 263,446 input tokens, against 16,563 on the 9, above the window the registry gives 7 of the 8 models, on 1 item each (2 for `openai/gpt-5-chat`). And the routing results read the 9, so the two runs score the same benchmarks. These swe-bench numbers are not pinned by a test: that needs the extracts, and a suite run reads the pinned file once, through the converter's CLI run in the session fixture of 12.19 (v1.156). Row E17e asks whether a later version adds it
- Dropped items: the 10 items on which `openai/gpt-5`'s output count is null (12.18), `hle|1144`, `hle|1265`, `hle|2000`, `hle|2072` and `hle|554`, and `simpleqa|1137`, `simpleqa|2574`, `simpleqa|2683`, `simpleqa|3592` and `simpleqa|4113`, 80 rows of the pool, dropped rather than priced at a count of 0, which would make the item's output free. Left: 12,056 items and 96,448 rows, `aime` 60, `arc-agi` 400, `arenahard` 749, `gpqa` 198, `hle` 2,152, `livecodebench` 1,055, `livemathbench` 121, `mmlupro` 3,000 and `simpleqa` 4,321. - Registry, committed as `evidence/llmrouterbench-delegation/registry.json` and written by hand in the 13.12 format: the top keys `as_of` (`"2026-11-10"`, the last day a page it cites was opened), `note` (a sentence that names `research/delegation-datasets.md`) and `models`, in that order; each model with the keys `id`, `family`, `price_in`, `price_out`, `context_window`, `source` and `notes`, in that order, models in ID order, each `family` the vendor part of its ID; no `price_valid_until`, since these prices only reprice recorded 2025 calls; its text `json.dumps` of its content with `indent=2` and a newline. Prices in USD per 1M tokens; each price and window is the research note's, which a plain test checks against its tables:

  - `deepseek/deepseek-r1-0528` 0.5 and 2.15, `deepseek/deepseek-v3-0324` 0.25 and 0.88 and `deepseek/deepseek-v3.1-terminus` 0.27 and 1, each with window 163,840, and `internlm/intern-s1` 0.18 and 0.54 with window 65,536: source `https://huggingface.co/datasets/NPULH/LLMRouterBench/tree/<REVISION>` with the 12.18 `REVISION`; `notes` says no vendor page found states its price, that the prices are the pair its recorded costs give within 1e-9 USD on all 12,056 kept items (`research/llmrouterbench_prices.py`), and that the window is the open weights' `position limit`, `max_position_embeddings` in the config.json that the research note's table links (that URL, with its pinned revision), `not a served window`
  - `openai/gpt-5` 1.25 and 10, window 400,000, source `https://platform.openai.com/docs/models/gpt-5`, and `openai/gpt-5-chat` 1.25 and 10, window 128,000, source `https://platform.openai.com/docs/models/gpt-5-chat-latest`: `notes` gives the page's status, `Deprecated` and `Shut down`, and that the recorded costs are 0.906 and 0.967 of these list prices
  - `z-ai/glm-4.6` 0.6 and 2.2, window 200,000, source `https://docs.z.ai/guides/overview/pricing`; `notes` says the window is the `200K` of `https://docs.z.ai/guides/llm/glm-4.6`, read as 200,000, and that the recorded costs are 0.996 of these list prices
- Script constants: `POOL` above; `BARS = (0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.8)`, every other 12.19 bar from its lowest and then its highest, the two ends being the bars `research/check-fallback.md` and `research/below-bar-fallback.md` replay: all eight 12.19 bars would make a file of about 456,000 bytes (455,947 in the prototype), above the about 300 KB rule, and four make about 230,000. Each is strictly inside the span of the per-task, per-model success rates on the kept rows, the 12.17 rule, from 0.0362 (`deepseek/deepseek-v3-0324` on `hle`, 78 of 2,152) to 0.8833 (`openai/gpt-5` on `aime`, 53 of 60). `SEEDS = 50`; `KEYS = ("config", "tasks", "summary")`; `points()` returns the 13.10 `points()` of `evidence/delegation/results.py`, loaded by file path as 13.12 loads it, so the three grids cannot drift. No variant point is run in this version
- `run(rows, registry) -> dict`: `evaluate_delegation(rows, registry, bars=list(BARS), points=points(), seeds=range(SEEDS))`, keeping only `KEYS` in that order, with `rows` what `select_rows` returns
- CLI: `python evidence/llmrouterbench-delegation/results.py --matrix PATH --registry PATH --out PATH`, entry point `main(argv=None) -> int`, all three options required (a missing one is an argparse usage error, exit 2). It reads the rows from the JSONL matrix, a blank line skipped, and the registry with `Registry.load`, calls `select_rows(rows, [m.id for m in registry.models])`, writes `json.dumps(run(<the rows left>, registry), indent=2)` and a newline to `<out>.part`, renames it onto `--out`, and returns 0. An unreadable or invalid file, or a `ValueError` from `select_rows` or the harness, prints one line starting `error: ` to stderr, a newline or carriage return escaped, and returns 1, with `--out` untouched and no `.part` left. The tables come from the 13.10 CLI, as in 13.12. v1.198 (review iter-148 must-fix 1 and should-fix 1): the output path and its `.part` are formed inside that same error handling, so an `--out` with an empty file name, such as `/` or `.`, is that one `error: ` line and exit 1 too, before any file is read; and every exit 1 after the options parse leaves no `<out>.part`, one that an earlier run left included, while exit 0 leaves none either. v1.269 (row D4h, as 13.12 v1.269): the same check, line and exit 2, once the output path and its `.part` are formed and before the registry or the matrix is read
- Checked with a private prototype of this contract on the pinned file when this section was written (the cached-file tests pin each number): 115,200 cells (4 bars, 2 flavors, 4 points, 8 parents, 50 seeds and 9 tasks), about 25 seconds after the 12-second read; 768 summary rows in a file of 230,012 bytes; the per-model mean sizes give `S_in + S_out` of 377 to 6,803 tokens, so no cell lacks a route; inline is blocked (`parent_context_full`) at point 2 (C = 200,000) on every cell of the 7 parents other than `openai/gpt-5`, at point 1 (C = 64,000) on 2,184 of the 3,600 cells of `internlm/intern-s1`, whose window leaves 1,536 tokens past C, and on no other cell. Of the 57,600 `cost_effective` cells 19,921 are `delegate_cheaper`, 14,840 `none_clear`, 13,692 `parent_context_full`, 6,555 `parent_below_bar` and 2,592 `inline_cheaper`; the 57,600 `cost_blind` cells hold the same 14,840, 13,692 and 6,555 with 17,674 `delegate_better` and 4,839 `inline_as_good`. At bar 0.1 and point 0, averaged over the 8 parents, the `cost_effective` decision costs 69.5970 USD on a held-out half against 133.3194 for always inline, with held-out success 0.450 against 0.445; at bar 0.8 it costs 221.6676 against 133.3194, with 0.582 against 0.445

### 13.14 Delegation results on the LLM Routing Response Bank (`evidence/response-bank-delegation/results.py`, v1.310)
Purpose: the 13.9 evidence on a fourth public dataset, whose items are single calls as on AutoBench (13.10) and LLMRouterBench (13.13), over the 4 task families of 12.20, in the shape of 13.13: the 13.5 decision, always inline and always delegating, scored on held-out items for each of the five models as the parent, regenerated byte for byte from stated commands. Standard library and the `model_routing` package only. [`research/delegation-datasets.md`](research/delegation-datasets.md) `## LLM Routing Response Bank (2026-11-12)` recommends it (row R26) and its `### Context windows (2026-11-12)` gives the windows (row E21a). v1.310 specifies the script, its registry and its results file with contract tests ahead of them (row E21b); the commit that adds the script, `registry.json` and `results.json` is row E21c (v1.312); RESULTS.md, with its sentences stated here and its tests committed red first, is row E21e, split from E21c in v1.312 so that each commit stays one reviewable slice; the README subsection with its Results at a glance bullet and Limitations words (12.8) and the 12.13 workflow lines are row E21d, and the folder README (as 13.10 v1.271) is row E21f (v1.316, v1.319)
- Pool, the script's `POOL`: all five 12.20 models, a tuple in ID order, `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash`, `deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro`, `moonshotai/kimi-k2.7-code`, `z-ai/glm-5.3` and `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`. The registry rule below prices each one, so, unlike 13.13, no model of the routing results (12.21) is left out
- Matrix: the 12.20 converter's default output, the rows the first command of 12.21 writes; the converter is unchanged, so the routing and the delegation results read one matrix and the workflow builds it once. 13.9 reads its `input_tokens` and `output_tokens` and ignores its `cost_usd`, the bill
- Input size: a row's `input_tokens` as 12.20 writes it, `usage.input`, the part of the prompt not read from the cache, and not `input + cache_read`, the whole prompt, which the converter does not keep. Three reasons:
  - cost: 13.9 charges every input token at the list input price and a registry model has no cache-read price, while each model's cache-read price is under half its input price (0.0028 of 0.14, 0.003625 of 0.435, 0.19 of 0.95, 0.26 of 1.4 and 0.03 of 0.15 USD per 1M tokens, in `POOL` order). So leaving the cached tokens out puts a cached response's list cost below its bill by `cache_read * price_cache_read`, a smaller gap than the one charging them at the input price puts above it, `cache_read * (price_in - price_cache_read)`. On the kept rows a model's list cost at the registry pair, summed over its responses, is 0.9999, 0.9995, 0.9901, 0.9855 and 0.9824 of its summed bills, in `POOL` order
  - windows: 13.4 rule 3 and 5.2 rule 5 read the task's S_in and S_out, which 13.9 takes on each calibration half. The research note's figures are whole-set evidence: with whole-set `median_low` sizes and S_in the whole prompt no window is reached at the 13.10 points, the smallest margin 56,976 tokens, under `moonshotai/kimi-k2.7-code`'s window at point 2, 11.0 times the largest whole-set S_in + S_out (`aime`, 182 and 4,986, the same under both readings). That does not bound the half-by-half sizes; the prototype below does, for this run: inline is blocked on no cell and no cell lacks a route, which a cached-file test pins. On any one half `usage.input` is never larger than the whole prompt, so its sizes are never the larger of the two; whether the whole-prompt run blocks a cell is not checked, since this version does not run it
  - one matrix, as above
- What the input size misses: on `mmlu_pro`, where 25,478 of the 60,154 responses read from the cache, the work's input is its uncached part, a whole-set `median_low` of 980 tokens against 1,281 for the whole prompt. The research note counts the (parent, delegate) pairs whose cheaper path differs between families at the 13.10 points as 10, 3, 1 and 16 of 25 with `usage.input` and 10, 3, 1 and 14 with the whole prompt. RESULTS.md's `## Assumed` says so
- Dropped items: `select_rows` is 13.13's, loaded by file path from `evidence/llmrouterbench-delegation/results.py` as `points()` is loaded, so its null-count rule and its checks cannot drift between the two runs; the script calls it with the registry's model IDs, which are `POOL`. It drops the 4 `mmlu_pro` items that hold the six `missing` cells of 12.20, which have no response and so null token counts: `mmlu_pro:10969`, `mmlu_pro:10990`, `mmlu_pro:11007` and `mmlu_pro:7872` (ascending as strings), 20 rows, since 13.9 needs both counts on every row of a complete matrix, and counts of 0 would give a call whose sizes are unknown a work-token cost of 0 (no response or bill is recorded for any of the six), so 13.9's rule that every row carries both counts drops them, as 13.13 drops its null counts. Left: 13,311 items and 66,555 rows, `aime` 30, `gpqa_diamond` 198, `livecodebench` 1,055 and `mmlu_pro` 12,028. The routing results keep the 4 items, each `missing` cell a miss at a cost of 0.0 (12.20 Choices); here they are 4 of 12,032. The 12.2 split reads only the seed and the item IDs, so on the 3 other tasks every seed's halves are those of 12.21, and `aime`'s calibration half holds 10 to 21 items, under `min_samples` 30 on every seed, so no model clears `aime` and its cells are `none_clear` (13.5 row 7)
- Registry, committed as `evidence/response-bank-delegation/registry.json` and written by hand in the 13.13 format: the top keys `as_of` (`"2026-11-12"`, the day every page it cites was opened), `note` (a sentence that names `research/delegation-datasets.md`) and `models`, in that order; each model with the keys `id`, `family`, `price_in`, `price_out`, `context_window`, `source` and `notes`, in that order, models in ID order, each `family` the vendor part of its ID; no `price_valid_until` or `price_tiers`, since these prices only reprice recorded calls; its text `json.dumps` of its content with `indent=2` and a newline. The rule is the research note's Recommendation: every model at the input and output pair its bills give on its responses with `cache_read` 0, whatever its page says, since a page price that differs from a bill would price the work at a price it was not billed at; a page states that pair for three of the five. The windows are the context-windows subsection's, each `1M` read as 1,000,000. Prices in USD per 1M tokens; a plain test checks each against the note's tables:
  - `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash` 0.14 and 0.28 and `deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro` 0.435 and 0.87, each with window 1,000,000, source `https://huggingface.co/datasets/Lurume/llm-routing-response-bank/tree/<REVISION>` with the 12.20 `REVISION`; `notes` says the pair is the one its bills give on its responses with `cache_read` 0, that its cache-read price (0.0028 and 0.003625) is not in the registry, that `https://api-docs.deepseek.com/quick_start/pricing`, opened 2026-11-12, states other prices (for `deepseek-v4-pro` 0.66 and 1.98 off-peak and 1.32 and 3.96 at peak; for `deepseek-v4-flash` that the name is retired and its requests are served by DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash-1017 at the `deepseek-flash` price, 0.15 and 0.6 off-peak and 0.3 and 1.2 at peak), and that the window is that page's `1M` read as 1,000,000 (for `deepseek-v4-flash`, the `deepseek-flash` window), the weights' `max_position_embeddings` 1048576
  - `moonshotai/kimi-k2.7-code` 0.95 and 4, window 262,144, source `https://platform.moonshot.ai/docs/pricing/chat`, the List prices table's page; `notes` says the page now redirects to `https://platform.kimi.ai/docs/pricing/chat`, states this pair and a cache-read price of 0.19, which is not in the registry, and the window `262,144` for every K2 series model, and closes the K2 series to new users from October 31, 2026 and retires it by the end of December 2026
  - `z-ai/glm-5.3` 1.4 and 4.4 and `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` 0.15 and 0.5, each with window 1,000,000, source `https://docs.z.ai/guides/overview/pricing`; `notes` says the page states this pair and a cache-read price (0.26 and 0.03), which is not in the registry, and that the window is the `1M` of its model page (`https://docs.z.ai/guides/llm/glm-5.3` and `https://docs.z.ai/guides/llm/glm-5.3-flash`), read as 1,000,000
  - v1.311 (review iter-207 should-fix 1): a test finds these phrases in `notes`. In every model's: its cache-read price, `not in the registry`, its window's page URL and its window with thousands commas. In the two DeepSeek models': `responses with cache_read 0`, the DeepSeek pricing page URL, `opened 2026-11-12`, `off-peak`, `at peak`, `max_position_embeddings` and `1048576`, and `1M` and `1,000,000`; `retired`, `DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash-1017`, `deepseek-flash`, `0.15 and 0.6` and `0.3 and 1.2` for `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash`; `0.66 and 1.98` and `1.32 and 3.96` for `deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro`. ## 14. Non-goals for v1 (two-way doors, deferred)
- Native vendor SDK adapters (the OpenAI-compatible adapter covers gateways), async execution, streaming
- Learned or contextual routers (per-prompt difficulty prediction); v1 routes per task
- Online drift detection beyond re-calibrating from audited results; budgets and alerts
- Price refresh automation
- In the delegation cost model (13): prompt caching beyond one cached-read ratio on the parent's reads of its context (from v1.41, 13.2), such as cache-write surcharges, cache expiry and discounts on other re-reads, and latency, including the speed-up of parallel sub-agents (multi-call work is costed from v1.38, 13.2)

## 15. Changelog
The changelog is in [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md), from v1.0, oldest first, with the version numbers it had here, so a `15 vX.Y` reference names its line there.
